# Channel Spec

> **Status:** Draft for Tom's review (2026-10-02). **[Default]** = proposed value, easy to change. **[Open]** = still to decide.
> Every real-time number here lives in `config/settings.json`, not in code.
> **Colours (2026-10-21):** where a colour named below differs, `docs/colours.md` wins: gold is Fan Coins' alone (records, Cups, legends, previews, big matches and MOMENTS' secrets are amber), red live, blue replays, white names, grey secondary.
> **Between matches (2026-10-05):** §16, marked **[Between]**, describes what Milestone 3¾ builds (`docs/tasks.md`, Tasks 91–108): the scene, the post-match show's new order, talking heads, the interview, transitions and ads. Where it differs from §7 and §7a, §16 wins once its task lands.
> **Gameplay pass 2 (2026-10-05):** §15, marked **[Gameplay]**, describes what Milestone 3½ builds (`docs/gameplay-tasks.md`; summary in `docs/gameplay-summary.md`).
> **Stadium and sound (2026-10-04):** §14, marked **[Stadium]**, describes what Milestone 3 builds (`docs/stadium-tasks.md`).
> **Match day milestone (2026-10-03):** sections marked **[Match day]** describe what that milestone builds (`docs/match-day-tasks.md`). Until a task lands, the code still does what the rest of the section says.

The channel is the presentation layer for Milestone 2 ("you can watch it"). It turns the league into a live pixel-art sports channel that's pleasant to leave open on a second monitor. It sits on top of the simulation and never changes it.

---

## 1. Principles

- **The channel only reveals.** The sim decides everything; the channel shows it as time passes. A match is fully simulated at kick-off, and its events appear on screen at their game-clock times.
- **No spoilers.** Nothing on screen is ahead of "now". The table updates at full time, results appear at the final whistle, and the ticker never shows a score before the goal has been shown.
- **Read-only.** Channel code reads league state and the event log. It never writes to them. `src/sim` and `src/chronicle` never import from `src/channel`.
- **Seeded visuals.** Any visual randomness, such as where a player drifts or which way a celebration runs, comes from a seeded stream keyed by `matchId` (`src/sim/rng.js`). It never uses `Math.random`. The same moment of the same match looks the same on every screen.
- **Logic separate from drawing.** Everything that decides *what* is on screen is pure, DOM-free code that's unit-tested in Node. Only a thin layer draws it.

## 2. Rendering decision: Canvas 2D plus HTML overlays

**Decided 2026-10-02.**

- **The pitch** is drawn on a `<canvas>` with the 2D API at a logical resolution of **480 × 270** **[Default]**. It is scaled up to fill the window, with image smoothing off (Task 277c, Tom: until then by a whole number, ×2, ×3, ×4, for perfectly even pixels, which left wide empty borders; the slight unevenness isn't noticeable; `?fit=whole` on the dev server shows the old way). It's letterboxed if the window isn't 16:9: on a desktop the bars are the panels' night blue (`--panel`) with a thin edge (`--panel-edge`) a stage pixel off the picture (Tom's pick D on `bars.html`, 2026-10-18; a wider stage would mean every scene laid out again); the phone keeps black bands for its buttons (§28).
- **Overlays** are HTML/CSS layered over the canvas: the scorebug, ticker, table, captions and cards. Text, layout and later clickable profiles (Milestone 4) are much easier in HTML.

**Why not Phaser:** the sim is zone-based with no physics, so Phaser's physics, scene and input systems would mostly go unused. It would be the project's first dependency (about 1 MB, vendored into the repo because there's no build step). Its text and UI tools are also weaker than HTML for broadcast overlays.

We can revisit this if sprite animation outgrows hand-rolled code; the pure logic layer (§11) wouldn't change.

## 3. How the browser league runs

Until Milestone 6 there is no server. Each browser **rebuilds the league from scratch** every time the page loads.

- `config/settings.json` gives the live league's `seed`, `firstSeasonStarts` (a Monday) and `timeZone`. Season N starts at 00:00 local time on the Monday `(N − 1)` weeks after `firstSeasonStarts`.
- **On load:**
  1. Every finished season is fast-forwarded, including its off-season. This takes about 0.1 s per season, so a year of history is about 5 s. A loading card covers it. *(Measured in Task 217, below: about 80 ms a season. **The loading card** came in Task 227 (Tom): SCORCHBALL in the ticker's gold box and TUNING IN… blinking under it, centred on the backdrop, in `index.html` from the first paint; `main.js` lets it paint, builds the league, then hides it as the stage shows, or on an error.)*
  2. The current season is then stepped match by match, up to the last kick-off before "now".
- **Load time (Task 217, It remembers; measured in Chrome, the channel's own `LiveLeague`, the chronicle included):** after 0 seasons 6 ms; 9 seasons 0.7 s; 49 seasons 3.7 s; 99 seasons 7.9 s: about 80 ms a season, nearly all of it the sim (the chronicle adds about 2%). The dev panel shows each load's (`built in … ms`). The schedule pane's worker (`guideWorker.js`) rebuilds a league of its own once a day when the pane is first opened, the same cost again, off the drawing thread behind LOADING THE DAY…. **Why no fix yet:** rebuilding from the seed only lasts until Milestone 6 (a server holds the league; the launch starts a fresh seed), so it grows only with `flareball`'s age, one season a week from 2026-10-05: about 0.7 s by mid-December 2026, 2 s by late March 2027. **The trigger:** if a load passes about 2 s before Milestone 6, cover it (a loading card painted before the build, or the build in a worker), or skip match detail while fast-forwarding finished seasons.
- **While running,** each match is simulated the moment its kick-off arrives, and its events are revealed as the clock runs.
- **Memory:** the current and previous seasons' match events are kept for highlights. Older match events are dropped, since the channel doesn't need them until the chronicle (Milestone 4). League events (standings, retirements, draft and so on) are all kept.
- **Known trade-off:** changing rules or code changes history, because the rebuild replays everything with the new version. This is accepted while prototyping. Save and load move to Milestone 6, when the server owns the one league.
- **Needs from the sim:** `runSeason()` is split into steps (`startSeason`, `nextFixture`, `playNextMatch`, `seasonRecord`; simulation.md §6a), so the live league and fast-forward share one code path. Done in Task 2.
- **Log order isn't screen time.** League events from a step (for example `standings` at the end of a round) are logged when the match is *simulated*, at kick-off, not when it ends on screen. The channel must time its reveals from match kick-off and length, never from when an event was logged.

## 4. Calendar

A season is one real week, starting on a Monday at 00:00 **[Default]**. Times below are written as "day N HH:MM" from the season's start.

| When | What |
|---|---|
| Day 1 00:30 → day 6 ~17:20 | **Regular season, rounds 1–13:** 52 matches one at a time, in round order, evenly spaced (about one every 2h41m) |
| Day 6 20:00 | **Final round:** all 4 matches kick off together |
| Day 7 14:00 | Semifinal 1 (1 v 4) |
| Day 7 17:00 | Semifinal 2 (2 v 3) |
| Day 7 20:00 | Final |
| After the final | Champion card, off-season summary, countdown to the next season |

**[Match day]** Kick-off times stay as they are. Each live match now has an hour slot around it (§7a), and full re-airs fill the rest of every gap, including the long gaps after the final round, between knockouts and before the new season. With 20-minute matches a knockout takes at most about 31 real minutes, overtime, a shootout and pauses included, so the fixed playoff times have plenty of room.

- **Spacing:** the gap between kick-offs is `(final round − first kick-off) / 52`. Changing the first kick-off or the final round time re-spaces the whole season automatically.
- **Playoff times are fixed** rather than "the previous match plus a gap". A knockout match can run long (overtime, a shootout), and fixed times keep the calendar a pure function of the settings. With 20-minute matches a knockout takes at most about 31 real minutes (overtime, a shootout and the pause allowance included), so the calendar checks need 39 minutes between knockouts, pre-match and full time included. (Semifinal 1 moved from 16:00 to 14:00 when matches were 60 minutes.)
- **Off-season timing:** the sim runs the off-season as soon as the final ends. The channel reveals it as one summary segment (§7).
- **Time zone (decided 2026-10-02): US Eastern, `America/New_York`.** "day N HH:MM" is wall-clock time in that zone, so kick-offs keep their local times across daylight-saving changes, and every browser agrees on what's live whatever its own zone.
  - US clocks change at 02:00 on a Sunday, which is day 7. No kick-off is near that hour, so no league time is ever skipped or repeated.
  - Spacing is worked out in wall-clock minutes. On a change week, the gap that spans the change is an hour longer or shorter.
- **Validation:** `src/config.js` rejects a calendar that doesn't fit. It checks that:
  - the start is a real Monday and the time zone is known
  - regular-season matches, with their pre-match and full-time segments, never overlap
  - there is one playoff kick-off per knockout match
  - knockouts leave room for overtime and a shootout with two rounds of sudden death
  - the final and its segments end within the week

## 5. Match playback

**Pace:** game clock to real time is linear while the clock runs. A 40 game-minute match plays in 20 real minutes (Task 22; it was 60), so one game-second is half a real second.

| Part of the match | Real time |
|---|---|
| Half-time | 1 minute |
| Goal pause | 11 seconds, clock stopped (5 s of celebration, then everyone back to their spots) |
| Substitution pause | 9 seconds, clock stopped |
| Overtime | 5 game-minutes at the same pace (2½ real minutes), after a short break |
| Each shootout kick | A few seconds |

All of these come from settings.

**The clock pauses for goals and substitutions** (Task 22):
- After a goal the game clock stops for `match.goalPauseSeconds` (11; 7 until Task 42) while the scorer peels away toward the corner they attacked (the two nearest teammates join them), everyone else drifts back toward the restart, and the conceding keeper collects the ball. After `animation.goalCelebrateMs` (5 s) the celebrators jog back too, so everyone is in their spot when the keeper restarts (Task 42). The ball is dead, so the heat strip drops to cool.
- A substitution stops it for `match.subPauseSeconds` (9; 5 until Task 41): the carrier keeps the ball, nobody passes, and the sub runs on from their seat on the bench (Task 30). The player coming off is seated a second before play restarts: they walk, or jog if the bench is far (Task 41).
- **A free shot waits a beat** (Task 67): the sim has a foul, its free shot and the shot at the same game second; the clock now stops for `match.freeShotPauseSeconds` (2.5) between the foul and the free shot while the referee places the ball (everyone holds), so the whistle, FOUL! and the shot don't all land at once. About 1.4 free shots a match; over 30 seasons the longest match's pauses came to 4.1 minutes, inside the allowance.
- **The free shot staged (Task 143; Tom's pick B from `freeshots.html`):** the pause is 6 s (`freeShotPauseSeconds`). The foul that gives one is now a moment of its own in `motion.js`: the play heads for it like a shot, the fouled player is in an invisible box in front of the goal (`FREE_SHOT_BOX`: 0.1–0.2 pitch lengths from the goal line, the middle 60% across, at a seeded depth; Tom: picture only, the sim's attacking-zone foul is the box foul) and the defender who fouls closes in like a tackler. In the pause (`freeShotStaging`, scaled to its length): everyone stops at the whistle; the referee walks to the ball, carries it to the mark (the shootout's, 0.18 from the goal line) and steps aside, off the side of the box, with an arm up for the kick (`pitchside.js`, blending back into following over 3 s after); from 30% of the pause the keeper goes to the line, the taker behind the ball and everyone else (but the other keeper) to an arc 0.1 behind the mark in their order across; a beat; the taker's run-up; the shot leaves from the mark as the pause ends. The ball in the pause is `placed` (a dead ball). Over 40 matches 65 of 66 free-shot fouls land in the box (median 0.15 out; before, 0.29 and a tenth past 0.55). The longest match's pauses over 30 seasons: 4.0 minutes, inside the 5-minute allowance.
- **The referee from far off (Task 175):** in a free shot's pause the referee chases the staged spot at no more than twice running pace (`FREE_RUN` in `pitchside.js`), worked out once per pause; from close by nothing changes, from far off (a foul 0.4 pitch lengths away) they reach the ball late, on its way to the mark, instead of sprinting at 3.5× running pace.
- **Any other foul, a short restart (Task 144, Tom):** the clock pauses `match.foulPauseSeconds` (3) after a foul that doesn't give a free shot (a pause kind of its own, `foul`; one pause per moment, the longest). The foul is a moment in `motion.js` too: the play heads for it, the player the picture has on the ball is the one fouled (the sim's fouled player may be shown elsewhere), never inside the free-shot box (`outOfBox`), and the defender who fouls closes in. In the pause (`foulPlan`) the ball lies still where the foul was (`placed`); everyone carries on a moment, then drifts into their shape for the fouled team on the ball, opponents giving it 0.05 pitch lengths of room; the fouled player stands just behind it; the referee comes over (as close as a run gets them by 45% of the pause), stands by it to 75%, then heads back into following. As the pause ends the fouled player rolls a restart pass to the nearest teammate with an open lane (unless the next moment comes straight away). About 2.9 a match, so matches pause about 9 s more on average; the longest match's pauses over 30 seasons: 4.1 minutes, inside the allowance.
- **Golden goals (Task 147, Tom's watch):** the match used to end the moment a golden goal went in: no goal pause, so no celebration, commentary, lower third or replay, the highlight cut off before the shot, and (the match already "finished" at the goal, which reads as the second half's direction) the shooter heading for the wrong end and shooting from their own half. Now a golden goal is paused like any goal (`goalPauseSeconds`) and the final whistle (`match_result`) comes after it; `timeline.goldenAt` marks it. It's made special: GOLDEN GOAL! in a gold bubble and lower third (GOLDEN GOAL! SCORCHER! for a hot one), the biggest roar there is through the whole pause (`GOLDEN_CHEER`; smaller for an away one), gold confetti for a home golden goal, the ball left in the net (no restart) and the whole scoring team, keeper too, mobbing the scorer in a ring while the other side stays where they were. The goal replay inset and the highlight (which now runs on past the goal) show it like any goal.
- **A sub brought on as play restarts (Task 153, Tom saw a keeper subbed at a burn's restart):** the ball went to the new keeper still at the bench (or waiting at the touchline), and they carried it all the way to goal. Now, while the player brought on in the substitution whose pause is on is more than 0.012 pitch lengths from their spot (as drawn: a keeper's tracking lags their plan), any ball for them waits there (`arrivingSpot`, a dead ball), rolled there from the board or the burn instead of toward the bench; they pick it up on arrival, before play restarts.
- **Coming back to the tab (Task 154, Tom: a moment louder, in Firefox):** browsers stop drawing a hidden tab's frames, so the channel starts no new sounds while you're away, but those already started keep playing. Coming back is a jump, and the sounds under way were all started again part-way (Task 62), including ones still ringing from before: a cheer or an anthem doubled for a moment. Now the channel notes until when each cue it starts rings (`playCue`, `cueKey`) and on a jump starts again only kinds not still ringing, and only the latest of each kind (`sounds.js` `resumeCues`): a goal's cheer, four staggered roars, comes back as one.
- **A hidden tab goes quiet; a switch swells in** (Fixes, Task 181, Tom: quick loud bursts in a background tab, as if queued sounds fired together; switching to a final-round match as its home team scored peaked very loud). Each frame plays the sounds due since the last at once, so wherever a hidden tab's frames are slowed rather than stopped, sounds meant to be spread out came out together. Now the channel goes quiet while the tab is hidden (`visibilitychange`, `audio.setHidden`: the master fades out over `sound.resume.hiddenFadeMs` (300) and nothing new starts) and fades back in when shown; the first frame back is a jump, so what's under way picks up as before. And whatever joins part-way after a jump (switching matches, coming back, loading mid-match, turning sound on) **swells in** over `sound.resume.fadeInMs` (700; it came in at full strength at once, `renderRecipe` `fadeIn`), the crowd's sounds at `sound.resume.crowd` (0.5) of their loudness: switching into a goal's cheer in the final round (the biggest roars there are) now comes in at half and rising, and the cheer's later roars play as usual. **Switching matches dips the new feed** (Tom, after Task 181: a home goal's cheer on a feed switched to sometimes still sounded too loud, not away goals): the swell only covered the roar already under way, while the murmur came in at the new match's level at once and the cheer's later roars at full. Now a switch between the matches on now (`liveGroup`) drops everything but the voices (`audio.enter`, a gain between the rest and the master) to `sound.resume.switchLevel` (0.5) and brings it back over `switchReturnMs` (2500), so the new feed eases in; the commentator stays at full. **Still a spike (Tom): which sound?** The crowd's roars and the murmur now each have their own way into the mix (`audio.js` buses), and a switch also silences one, the other or both for `sound.resume.hushHoldMs` (2000) before fading them in over `hushFadeMs` (1500): `sound.resume.hush` (roar for now), or `?hush=roar|murmur|both|none` in the URL to compare by ear (a dev-panel jump keeps it). **Tom listened (2026-10-08): it was the roars** (murmur alone still spiked; roar and both fixed it), so `hush` stays `roar`: on a switch the cheer is silent for 2 s and fades in over 1.5 s, the murmur only dips with the rest. Keeping time in a hidden tab (sound as radio) would need the sound driven by a timer apart from the drawing: not done.
- **The studio in the playoffs (Task 155, Tom: after a semifinal's highlights the desk talked about the season in general):** from the first semifinal's kick-off the gap's standings clip is the playoffs clip (`talk.js` `sayPlayoffs`): once a semifinal has finished on screen, who's through (`knockoutSemi`, voiced for every team) and that they'll meet the other semifinal's winner (`playoffsOtherSemi`, the other semifinal and its time on screen); once both have, the final set (`playoffsFinalSet`, THE FINAL and its time on screen), `standingsFinal` if a finalist topped the table, and a reaction (`playoffsReact`). The scorers clip names the season's top scorer as it finished and then the playoffs' (`scorersPlayoffs`). After the final the match talk, interview, wrap-up and champions talk already fit. Team names are spoken "the {name}" (`spokenTeam`). 16 new desk clips (8 lines, two presenters), about 526 credits; `sounds.html` has them for each presenter.
- One pause per moment, the longest of its events. There's no pause at the very end of a period, so a golden goal still ends the match at once.
- The pauses are presentation only. Game time and the sim are unchanged, and the keeper's restart after a goal is still immediate in game time (sport.md §5).

**Passes take longer the further they go** (Groundwork, Task 169): every pass took `animation.passMs` (0.35 s), so a cross from the touchline flew across most of the pitch in a third of a second. Now a pass's time is its length over `animation.passSpeed` (0.8 pitch lengths a real second), never quicker than `passMs` (a short pass, and the ball's other quick moves: after a turnover, a burn, a restart) nor slower than `passMaxMs` (0.8 s): a pass across the pitch's width about 0.6 s, a long ball 0.8 s (`passDuration` in `motion.js`). A pass to the sim's next moment sets off that much earlier, so it still arrives as the moment happens (its length taken from the players' spots in the phase's plan, so it doesn't wobble frame to frame); a pass at the start of a phase ends by the next phase; the keeper's outlet is only played if its longer time still fits before the tackle. The pitch map is unchanged within 0.2 points (the picture keeps up), the replay camera test passes.

**Not every goal first time** (Groundwork, Task 170): the look-ahead pass arrives as the shot leaves the foot, so about three goals in four were first-time finishes (66–76% under 1 s on the pitch map; 73% in `docs/stats-check.md`). Now, in the last phase before a shot, with chance `animation.touchChance` (0.85), the shooter is passed the ball early, in time for a touch of `touchMinMs` to `touchMaxMs` (1–1.6 s, seeded) before the strike, and on the ball steps in to shoot like any carrier (the formation's edge rule). Only when the phase has room for the pass and the touch; a pass the phase opens with goes to the shooter instead; never on a breakaway. Shots first time: about 49% (40 matches of two seeds: 52% and 48%); the other pitch-map numbers are unchanged (the tether 0.9–1.0%). The motion test of where shooters strike from allows 0.05 pitch lengths between working and shooting (it was 0.06): a shooter taking a touch steps in from their spot as they get the ball, a little further out than a first-time finish.
- The timeline inserts them itself (`timeline.pauses`, and `paused` in the match state), so playback is still a pure function of "ms since kick-off" and can't drift. A match's `duration` grows by its pauses: the opening match of the default league has 12 goals and 6 subs and lasts 22.9 minutes. The calendar checks allow `match.pauseAllowanceMinutes` (4).
- The scorebug clock blinks while it's stopped.

**How the timeline places events** (`src/channel/timeline.js`):
- Times are "ms since kick-off", so highlights at 1.5× just feed in 1.5 times the elapsed time.
- Each event's period comes from the kick-offs before it, not from its clock. Event clocks are whole game-seconds, and the half-time whistle and the second-half kick-off share 20:00.
- The second half is shifted by the half-time break, and overtime by one more break.
- A shootout starts one break after overtime ends. Each kick lands `shootoutKickRealSeconds` after the one before (struck `shotMs` ahead of its moment, like any shot), and the final whistle comes a goal's pause (`goalPauseSeconds`) after the last, which stays in the net or with the keeper till then (Task 277b: re-airs, highlights and live cut it off).
- **The shootout staged** (Task 277b, Tom's pick B on `shootouts.html`; `settings.animation.shootout`, motion.js `livelyShootout`; picture only, the kicks' moments unchanged): each player keeps a spot near the centre circle (keepers by the box) and mills about; the next taker walks up to just behind the mark, stands, runs up and strikes (the kick's pose), then walks back as the next comes up; the keepers take turns in goal, bouncing on the line and diving (the right way or the wrong way for a goal, to the ball for a save); the ball rolls back to the mark; the referee stands on the goal line by the top wall, an arm up for each goal; a team that scores puts its arms up, and after the last kick the winners cheer and the losers hold their heads to the whistle. Highlights' shootout clip starts 15 s before the first kick, so the first walk-up is in.
- Events after a pause move later by its length.
- The match's on-screen length (`duration`) ends at the final whistle (after a golden goal's pause, or a goal's pause after a shootout's last kick). The final whistle and the crowd's reaction to it, at that very end, are played by the handshakes or the lift as they take over (Task 277b: the live picture never reaches it, so they were lost). A regular match is 21 minutes plus its pauses.
- Heat is worked out from the game time since the last change of possession, kick-off or free shot. It matches the engine's `heat_stage` events to within a few points.

**Movement** (`src/channel/motion.js`, Task 10):
- Matches are simulated before they're shown, so the animation can look ahead, but only within one possession.
- Between events, players run steadily toward their spots for the next advance or shot. The ball is passed to whoever makes it.
- **Players have legs:** each heads for their spot at a top speed (`animation.runSpeed` near the ball, easing to `jogSpeed` at `nearBall` or further) and slows into place over `settleMs`. If the spot changes mid-run they turn and carry on. Only the first kick-off places players directly, in kick-off shape, which is exactly where the walk-out leaves them (§7a).
- **Kick-offs:** the ball sits on the centre spot with the midfielder, a KICK-OFF! bubble shows over the referee (Task 64; at the ball before), everyone is in their own half with only the side kicking off in the centre circle (Task 76), and everyone holds still for `animation.kickoffHoldMs` (0.8 s) before play starts.
- **Breaks:** during half-time and the break before overtime, outfield players gather by their bench and keepers change ends (at an even jog across the whole gather, never faster than a run: Task 278m; they had sprinted the length in about 12 s, starkest before overtime and in a re-air's quicker half-time); then everyone jogs to their spot for the next kick-off (the ends swap), so the restart has no jump. **Task 66:** each manager walks out to stand in front of their players and talk (an arm up now and then), and the substitutes step out in front of the dugout; both are back (on the touchline, in their seats) by the restart.
- Nobody anticipates a turnover. After one, players next to the ball react after `reactNearMs` and those far away after up to `reactFarMs`, carrying on with their old run until then, so the reaction ripples out from the ball.
- Shots are struck `animation.shotMs` before their moment and land exactly as it happens, so the score never changes before the ball arrives. **Bending shots** (Tom, 2026-10-16; `animation.shotBend`, `motion.js` `shotBendOf`; **off for now**, share 0: without the ball visibly spinning a curl looked odd, `docs/backlog.md`): when on, a quarter of shots (free shots too, not shootout kicks) leave the foot straight and curl off late (Tom, after watching: not curving from the outset; the bow t²(1 − t), widest two thirds along), 0.025–0.05 pitch lengths at the widest and at most 18% of the shot's length, either way, spinning the way they curl, and land where they would anyway; seeded by the shot from a stream of its own, so where shots land never changes.
- Defenders mark the nearest attacker, goal-side. Who marks whom is fixed for each possession spell.
- Keepers stay inside their crease, with or without the ball, and outfield players never stand in one (sport.md §1); a run that would cross a crease goes round its edge (Task 35; it happened in about 0.7% of moments before).
- **Keepers track the ball** (Task 35): in play, a keeper moves across with the ball (up to about the posts), comes off their line toward the crease edge (about 20 px) when it's in the other half, and drops back (about 10 px off the line) when it's in their own third. They follow where the ball has been over the last `animation.keeperTrackMs` (1 s), so they react about half a second late and never jump; at kick-offs, pauses and breaks they take their formation spot, blending in and out of tracking the same way.
- **Long spells keep moving:** a spell with no events splits into phases of about `animation.probeMs`. Attackers make fresh runs, the carrier moves with the ball, the defender marking the ball closes in harder as the spell goes on (hardest near goal), and the ball may go to a teammate (`holdPassChance`). The last pass still reaches whoever makes the next action.
- Teammates keep a little space between them, so nobody stands on top of anyone.
- **Breakaways** (Task 37): when the sim calls an advance a breakaway, a BREAKAWAY! bubble pops over the player as the ball reaches them (the moment the sim identifies it, before the shot). They keep the ball and run straight at goal, timing the run to reach the shooting spot as they strike it (aiming a little past it, at up to 1.6 times their usual top speed), so they shoot on the run; the shot comes 0.7–1.7 s after the bubble (Task 40). The defence is beaten: from the through ball on, its outfield players trail behind the ball (the nearest 0.06 pitch lengths behind, the others further) and react late. In the test match no outfield defender is goal-side at a breakaway shot, against usually two for other shots.
- **Working the ball in the attacking zone** (Task 37): the carrier hangs back at the edge of range (0.12 pitch lengths short of the shooting spot) and steps in only in the phase before a shot, so a carrier in a prime position is about to shoot.
- **Substitutions mid-move** (Task 37): a moment that doesn't change who has the ball (a substitution, injury or foul) leaves it with whoever has it (it used to snap back to the sim's carrier). If the player on the ball is substituted, an on-pitch teammate takes it, passed from where it was. Both teams substituting at the same game second share one moment and one pause. A substitution at the same game second as a goal gets its own pause straight after the goal's, and the incoming player doesn't run on during the goal pause.
- **Who is shown on the ball** (Task 36): the sim's carrier, except at advances, which nothing else on screen shows (no stat, caption or callout), so shots, tackles, fouls and saves always show the sim's players.
  - **A keeper credited with an advance** (the sim's keeper playing it out of defence) doesn't get the ball back from a teammate: whoever had it keeps it, and a keeper who had it (after a save or restart) plays it out to the midfielder. Before, this showed as a long pass back to the keeper, who then held the ball in the crease while the team was in midfield.
  - **Runs:** at each kick-off, change of possession or advance, with seeded chance `animation.runChance` (0.12), the outfield player on the ball sets off on a run. They keep it through every advance (no passes in between, whoever the sim credits) until the possession ends or the next shot, passing to the shooter just before it if that isn't them. Each advance takes them a zone further, so a run can go from their own third to a shot. In six matches: about 22 runs a match, mostly short; about 2 a match carry the ball over half the pitch (1 in all six matches before).
- **Passes never travel through players** of either team (Task 34). A pass keeps `PASS_CLEAR` (0.025 pitch lengths, about 9 px) from everyone but the passer and receiver, except while it's within twice that of either of them (a marker at their side). Three steps:
  - In a long spell, the passer picks a teammate in an open lane (seeded among the open ones), not just anyone.
  - The receiver of the pass before the next advance or shot steps sideways (up to 0.06 pitch lengths) to open the lane.
  - Each pass is checked against where everyone will be during it. Straight if that's clear; else it bends round, with the smallest bulge that clears (up to 0.12 pitch lengths); else it's **chipped**: the ball rises above its shadow (up to about 11 px) and drops at the receiver. In the default league about 82% of passes go straight, 17% bend and under 1% are chipped.
  - This covers passes between teammates and the ball going from the net or board to the keeper after a shot. Turnovers and burns go to whoever won the ball, so they aren't passes.
- **Spin** (Task 34 follow-up): whenever the ball travels, a dark pixel steps round its core's corners, so it reads as spinning (`ball.spin`, quarter turns per second; held or resting, it doesn't spin). Rolling passes and turnovers spin gently (clockwise going right), chips tumble faster, shots fastest. A bent pass spins the way it curls, faster for a bigger bend, and leaves a short trail (`ball.curling`) like a shot's, so the curve reads as a shape rather than a glitch.
- **A round ball, spinning by its speed** (Tom, 2026-10-16; on `ballspin.html`): the ball is a 4×4 without its dark corners, its edge 8 pixels round a 2×2 core. Travelling, a dark pixel steps round the core and the round ball's corner beside it is filled in, turning with it, a lopsided ball turning over (Tom's pick D on `ballspin.html`, round 2), the way `ball.spin` says (its sign: the way it travels or curls), at a rate that follows its speed: `animation.ballSpin.perPixel` (0.03) steps a second for each stage pixel a second, at most `maxSteps` (12; a pixel ball turning faster just flickers): a long pass about 4 as it leaves, 11 at its fastest, 6 as it arrives; a shot 12. (Tom's first pick, C, also ran two light pixels round the edge; on the smaller round ball it made the ball harder to follow, so it's just the core.) The turn is kept frame to frame for each match on each canvas (`ballSpin.js` `spinOn`, `main.js`), starting afresh after a jump of more than `jumpMs` (a replay, a skip); at a player's feet it holds still. The dev pages, without a turn, keep the old core pixel at the fixed rates.

**What's on the pitch:**
- The pitch is a walled rectangle, 2:1, with boards, a halfway line, a centre circle, two creases and two goals. It's split invisibly into the three sim zones.
- **Size (Task 28, layout D):** 358×179 stage pixels on the left of the 480×270 stage, leaving 80 px on the right for the live-stats panel (Task 32), with room for a bench strip above and below (Task 30). Goal mouths are 40 px, nets 10 px deep. It was 336×168; layout B (384×192, no panel) was tried first.
- There are 10 players as 7×10 placeholder sprites in kit colours (they were 5×8). Keepers wear a fixed contrasting shirt, with sleeves and shorts in their team's colour.
- **End boards** are painted in the colours of the team defending that goal, with a trim line along the outer edge, so it's clear which side is which. They swap with the ends at half-time and back for overtime, and show whenever a match is on the pitch (live, highlights, re-airs, the walk-out and handshakes).
- **The ball** has a dark edge, so it reads on the turf. A held ball is drawn just after its carrier, so it covers their feet but goes behind anyone standing in front of them; a ball in the air goes over everyone (Task 44).
- **Nets** (Task 44): white mesh (a diagonal crosshatch, 4 px diamonds, from Task 69; straight lines read as vertical stripes) over a shadowy inside, a solid white frame along the back and sides, and white posts at the mouth.
- **Sidelines** (Task 30; `sidelines.js`, pure): both dugouts on the top touchline, spread out toward their own halves with the middle open, home on the left (Tom, 2026-10-03: football style; facing each other top and bottom was tried). Each has a roof along its back in the team's colours, a shadow under it, a bench plank with legs at hip height and a lighter front edge (Task 44). They don't swap at half-time. The substitutes stand in them (keepers in the keeper's shirt), idling: a shift of weight about every second and a pixel of sway (Task 44).
  - At a substitution the incoming player runs from their seat onto the pitch (motion.js starts them there) and the player coming off walks from where they were to the seat just freed. An injured player walks off to the bench too. Walking is at `animation.jogSpeed`.
  - Benches show whenever a match is on the pitch, in the walk-out and the handshakes.
- **The side boards** look like hockey boards: white, with a yellow kick plate along the turf. The end boards carry the team colours.
- **The apron:** darker turf all round the boards, past the nets and out under the dugouts on the top, so the pitch and benches read as one ground rather than sitting on the backdrop.
- A held ball never leaves the pitch: if the player who has just won it is subbed off at that moment, it waits at the touchline for the sub running on.
- **Movement:** each player's spot depends on which zone the ball is in and who has it, plus a little seeded offset that stays the same for the whole half. The ball runs in the carrier's own lane, so play uses the width. The sim doesn't track coordinates, so positions are illustrative.
- **Sim events become animation:**

| Event | On screen |
|---|---|
| `kickoff` | Ball at centre; players in kick-off shape, holding still for a moment; KICK-OFF! bubble |
| `advance` | Ball travels to the carrier in the next zone; the team shifts up |
| `possession_change` | Ball goes to the new carrier; both teams reshape, nearest the ball first |
| `shot` | Ball flies toward goal |
| `save` | Keeper dives; held or parried; SAVE! bubble |
| `block` | Ball deflects off a defender; BLOCK! bubble |
| `miss` | Ball goes wide; MISS! bubble |
| `goal` | Ball hits the net; caption; scorebug updates |
| `burn` | Ball flashes white-hot, then the turnover; BURN! bubble over the referee (at the ball until 2026-10-18) |
| `foul` / `free_shot` | Short pause pose; yellow FOUL! bubble; free shot from the mark |
| `substitution` / `injury` | Caption; sprite swaps |

- **Callouts** (`src/channel/callouts.js`, pure; drawn by `overlays/callout.js`): a small speech bubble over the player involved, for `captions.calloutMs` (1.2 s), edged in the colour of the shirt their team wears in this match. **The referee's calls** (Task 64) go over the referee instead: KICK-OFF!, the yellow FOUL!, a bold **GOAL!** (edged in the scoring team's colour, while their arm is up and the whistle goes) and SUB ON, for `captions.subCalloutMs` (2.5 s), once for the moment's substitutions and alongside any other bubble, stacked above a call of theirs showing at the same time (Task 41 had it over the player coming on, one per team). TACKLE! (every logged tackle wins the ball), STEAL! (a tackle the sim marks as a pass cut out, Task 33), BREAKAWAY! (as the player breaks through), SAVE!, BLOCK!, MISS!, BURN! (over the referee from 2026-10-18, Tom: at the ball it travelled with it to the keeper). If several share a moment, the highest-ranked shows (goal, foul, burn, save, block, miss, then tackle, steal or breakaway), and a newer moment replaces an older bubble. They show in highlights too, but not in shootouts.

- **Heat:** the ball's colour follows heat continuously, from white through yellow and orange to red, using the elapsed game-time since possession changed (sport.md §8). The heat meter on the scorebug shows the same value.

## 6. Screen layout

The screen is 16:9.

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────┐
│ [LIVE] GUL 2–1 STO  31:04  ▓▓▓▓▓░ heat        │  TABLE       │
│                                               │  1 Royale 24 │
│              PITCH (canvas)                   │  2 Gulls  22 │
│                                               │  ...         │
│   ── GOAL! R. Tamsin (assist K. Orrin) ──     │              │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────┤
│ TICKER  FT Royale 4–3 Oasis · Next: Anvils v Rime 14:10       │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- **Scorebug (top left):** team abbreviations in kit colours, score, game clock, half or overtime marker, heat meter.
- **LIVE / HIGHLIGHTS bug:** always visible, so highlights are never mistaken for a live match.
- **Table:** position, team, played, points and goal difference, now the TABLE tab of the side panel (Task 32).
  - **Standings only change at full time**, including in the final round (Tom, 2026-10-03: no live tables; the live table with ▲▼ arrows was tried and removed). Teams playing right now are highlighted. FINAL TABLE once the season's last round has finished on screen. The season beside it, as the record books write it: TABLE - S1, FINAL TABLE - S1 (Tom, 2026-10-08: nowhere else said which season it is).
  - When the order changes, each row glides to its new place over `table.slideMs` (0.7 s).
- **Lower thirds:** goal (scorer and assist), scorcher (with a special treatment), burn, substitution, injury, half-time and full-time.
- **Ticker (bottom):** see §8.

**[Match day] New layout direction** (details are settled in the presentation tasks, with Tom reviewing on screen):
- **A bigger pitch** (done in Task 28: layout D from the `pitches.html` mock-ups, end boards in team colours, keeper trim): the 104 px table panel makes way for an 80 px stats panel, so the pitch can grow. Players, nets and lines are bigger, and the ball is easier to see.
- **Sidelines** at the top and bottom, with the substitutes idling on benches. The walls can read like hockey boards. Crowds, stands and a home-team look come later.
- **One broadcast frame** (Task 31, layout C from the `frame.html` mock-ups, with B's full-width line; Tom, 2026-10-03): plates on the screen edges, nothing over the pitch or the dugouts.
  - **Top left, one row:** the scorebug (LIVE / HIGHLIGHTS / REPLAY bug, teams, score, clock) flush to the corner, then in the final round the match picker as its own plate, stretching to the edge of the pitch area: equal chips with both kits, abbreviations and score (no numbers: keys 1–4 follow them left to right, and the tooltip names the teams and key), the shown match underlined, the channel's pick marked with a small red dot, and the chip of a match whose goal is alerting flashing red.
  - **The heat strip** runs along the scorebug's bottom edge, as wide as the scorebug (a full-width line was tried; a circular gauge by LIVE is an idea for later).
  - **Bottom, resting on the ticker:** the caption plate flush left, and goal alerts from other live matches as a plate on the right (ending at the stats column), with the scoring team's colour stripe and "press N to watch".
  - **Right: the side panel** (Task 32; `sidePanel.js` and `matchStats.js`, pure; drawn by `overlays/sidePanel.js`): a tabs plate at the top (STATS, TABLE, TOP, GAMES (WEEK until 2026-10-27, Tom); keys S, T, G, W or a click, on the viewer's screen only, starting on STATS every load) and the view below, 86 px wide.
    - **STATS:** the match on screen with its kits, state (LIVE 29', REPLAY or S1 REPLAY, HIGHLIGHTS, FULL TIME, UP NEXT), shots, on goal (ON GOAL, was ON TGT), saves, possession, blocks (shots each side blocked, Fixes, Task 183; the defenders' award will count them), steals, tackles (the balls won that weren't steals, so each counts once), scorchers, burns and fouls (Task 33), then the goals so far with minutes (scorchers in orange) in their own area that scrolls with a thin scrollbar once it's too long; it stays on the newest goal unless the viewer has scrolled up. Counted from the match's events up to the moment shown, so re-airs and highlights never spoil; at the final whistle the counts equal the sim's own totals (possession within a few seconds, from whole-second event clocks). Around a match it shows that match at full time; before kick-off the coming match at zero; in the gaps the last match to finish.
    - **STATS gets the score** (Groundwork, Tom 2026-10-07): between matches the panel keeps showing the last match (FULL TIME) until the next pre-match card, but said how it ended only through the goals list; now a GOALS row comes first, in amber, each team's goals under its name (always, live too), and the list below is headed SCORERS.
    - **TABLE** (back to TABLE in Groundwork, Tom 2026-10-07: TABLE is the word everywhere else; it was STANDINGS for a while): the league table (the old panel, with sliding rows), fitted to the column.
    - **TOP:** the top ten scorers: position, kit (the team), name, goals. **Task 141 (Tom):** the season's leaders count the regular season only, here and everywhere on screen (the ticker's TOP SCORER, the TOP SCORERS and SEASON LEADERS cards, the desk's scorers clip and tee-up, the champions card), as the season's record does (`onScreen.js` `leadersAt`, its `part`: regular by default, `playoffs`, `all`; Foundry Fuel's scorchers so far count every match). Once a playoff match has finished on screen, TOP shows the season's top five and a gold PLAYOFFS list of five under them; the studio rotation's PLAYOFF RACE (settled by then) becomes PLAYOFF SCORERS · SEASON N until the new season; the champions card names the playoffs' top scorer under the season's. Tom's picks from four mock-ups (a ticker line was left out).
    - **GAMES:** live now, the last four results, then the next six fixtures under a small header for each day ("13:55 LIS v PCG"; results "FT SKA 5-5 KES"; a re-air "REP" with its score hidden; SF and F for the playoffs). Tooltips give full names.
    - Everything fits the 86 px column; nothing covers the pitch (a wider panel over the pitch for TOP and GAMES was tried first).
- **A live-stats panel with tabs, instead of the table panel** (Tom, 2026-10-03): the 80 px panel on the right shows the match on screen's live stats by default (shots, on target, saves, possession, tackles, scorchers, burns, fouls, counted from its events up to the moment shown). Tabs above it switch to other views (STANDINGS, LEADERS, SCHEDULE), which can slide out wider over the pitch since they need team names. Viewers open and close them with keys or clicks, on their own screen only (Tom, 2026-10-03). They start closed on every load and aren't remembered between visits. The channel doesn't open them itself; the full-time and studio cards already show the table.

## 7. Segments

The programme is a pure function of the calendar and "now". It decides which segment is on screen. All durations are in `settings.segments` and `settings.highlights`.

| Segment | When | Shows |
|---|---|---|
| **Pre-match** | 3 min, ending 2 min before kick-off | Both teams, crests (Task 166), starting lineups, form, table positions, countdown |
| **Walk-out** | 2 min before kick-off | The teams on the pitch (§7a) |
| **Live match** | Kick-off to final whistle | §5 and §6 |
| **Handshakes** | 2 min after the whistle | The teams on the pitch (§7a) |
| **Full time** | 2 min, opening the post-match show | Result, scorers, scorchers, updated table |
| **Highlights** | In the post-match show, and in the gaps | A package of a recent match's goals and big moments (`src/channel/highlights.js`), at `highlights.speed` (1.5×, Tom 2026-10-03; it was 2×), with the blue HIGHLIGHTS bug. About 2 min each, showing about 3 min of the match. |
| **Re-airs** | The rest of each gap | Full matches at 1× with a REPLAY bug (§7a) |
| **Studio cards** | After each re-air and highlights package, and whenever neither fits | Rotating cards: table, latest results, next fixture with countdown, season top scorers. 20 s each **[Default]**. **[Between]** Over the scene, restyled, in studio blocks with talk clips and ad breaks (§16.4, §16.9). |
| **Final round** | Day 6 20:00 | One featured match plus live ticker scores and goal alerts cutting in from the other matches; the table updates as each match finishes |
| **Playoffs** | Day 7 | As a live match, with the stage named (Semifinal, Final), plus overtime and shootout presentation |
| **Champion** | After the final | Champion and Pennant winner, final score, the season's top scorers |
| **Off-season summary** | After the champion card | Retirements of note, draft lottery result, top draft picks |
| **New-season countdown** | Until day 1 00:00 | Countdown, last season's champion, the season's first fixture |

## 7a. [Match day] The hour slot and re-airs

Decided 2026-10-03 (Tom): the season keeps its 56 matches. Each live match gets an hour slot, and **full re-airs** of earlier matches fill the time until the next one (vision §6). Durations below are **[Default]** and live in settings.

**The hour slot (live matches):**

| Part | Real time | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| Pre-match | 3 min | The pre-match card: both teams, line-ups, form, table positions, countdown |
| Walk-out | 2 min | On the pitch: teams walk out and line up, a short anthem caption for each team, then a warm-up |
| First half | 10 min + pauses | As §5 |
| Half-time | 1 min | Players walk to their sideline with the subs; keepers jog to the other ends |
| Second half | 10 min + pauses | As §5 (overtime and a shootout follow in knockouts) |
| Handshakes | 2 min | Handshakes at halfway, a salute to the home crowd after a home win, then the teams leave slowly |
| Post-match show | 12 min | Full-time card, **player of the match** (stat line, plus a short canned quote in a speech bubble, standing in until voices arrive), this match's highlights package, studio cards |

**[Between]** The post-match show gains match talk at a desk, the filmed interview in place of the POTM card, and a studio block with an ad break (§16.2); each gap ends with a 45 s tee-up talk before the pre-match (§16.5).

That's about 40 minutes plus pauses. The pre-match and walk-out together start 5 minutes before kick-off, as the pre-match alone used to, so kick-offs don't move.

**Built in Tasks 23 and 25** (`programme.js`): segments `pre_match` → `walk_out` → `live` → `handshakes` → the post-match show (`full_time`, `potm`, then `highlights` of this match, then `studio`, each with `show: true`) → the gap.
- In the simultaneous final round, the show has each match's package, biggest stakes first, while they fit.
- After the final, the full-time card is followed by the champions and the off-season; the final's highlights then open the gap.
- In the gap, the matches just shown in the post-match show go to the back of the highlights queue.
- **Ceremonies** (`ceremonies.js`, Tasks 23 and 26; timings in `settings.ceremonies`; every leg starts from wherever a player is, so nothing jumps):
  - **Walk-out (1.5 min; 2 until Task 73):** players walk on in pairs, side by side, one from each team (`walkOnGapMs` apart; Task 63, one by one before) from the tunnel (the top touchline at halfway) to a line across halfway, home on the left, keepers at the ends (`walkOnSeconds`, 12); the substitutes follow them out and walk to their seats in the dugouts. The **home anthem**, then the **away anthem** (`anthemSeconds`, 16 each: one play of the tune; 25 until Task 73) while they stand, captioned in that team's colour. A **warm-up** (`warmUpSeconds`, 24): each player jogs to a new seeded spot in their own half every `warmUpMoveSeconds` (5), keepers in their goals. `breakLineSeconds` (18) before kick-off everyone jogs to their exact kick-off spot, the same spot the match's first frame uses (tested). Captions: THE TEAMS WALK OUT, HOME ANTHEM, AWAY ANTHEM, WARMING UP, TAKING POSITIONS.
  - **Goal celebrations:** during the goal pause the scorer runs to the corner and the two teammates nearest them run over to join in. **Task 65 (Tom):** the scorer goes above the centre circle, in line with the dugout on the side they scored (not the corner, not dead centre), the two nearest teammates either side; only the scoring side joins (anyone else whose spot is close waits just outside); the scoring keeper throws their arms up like the manager; the scoring bench is on its feet, arms up and hopping, with one or two pairs of neighbours turning to high-five (`sidelines.js` `celebrationAt`, `benchCheer`), all for `animation.goalCelebrateMs`.
  - **Half-time and the break before overtime:** outfield players walk to their bench and gather in front of it; keepers jog straight to their end for the next period. `breakLineSeconds` before the restart everyone heads for their kick-off spot.
  - **Half-time:** the substitutes stay in their dugouts.
  - **Handshakes (1.5 min, 2 until Task 73; 30 s in a re-air; shorter without a home win, Task 74):** the whole squads take part, substitutes and players who came off included (the dugouts empty). Parts by `handshakeParts` (fractions of the whole): each team gathers in a huddle just off its end of the lines, home left of halfway and away right, idling gently (0.2); then the players peel off one at a time into their line and walk along it past every opponent, the two lines just touching (Task 74), in one procession at `handshakeLineSpeed` (0.035 pitch lengths a second, about three times the old average; quicker only if a short re-air needs it), so every pair meets and nobody stops to set up; both lines reach the line in step, so opposing pairs meet at the same moments, and everyone holds for `handshakePauseMs` (220 ms) at each, a handshake's beat (Task 74) (0.25; Task 43, which replaced lines that formed, then stepped past each other with a pause at each meeting); as each player comes off the end of the line, after a home win (shootouts count) the home team lines up along the bottom touchline and hops to salute the fans while the away team goes to its bench (0.25), then everyone walks off down the tunnel and out of sight, in pairs (Task 63: each pair meets in its lanes just below the gate, or at the mouth for anyone by the bench, and goes in side by side). **Without a home win** (Task 74) there's no salute and no waiting by the dugouts: both teams head straight off in pairs from the end of the line, and the handshakes last only the huddle, the procession and 0.15 of the full length for the walk off (`ceremonies.js` `handshakeLength`; the programme uses it, live and in re-airs). Captions: FULL TIME · HANDSHAKES with the score, then "<HOME> SALUTE THE FANS" (or FULL TIME), then THE TEAMS HEAD OFF.
  - **After a home win** (Tom, 2026-10-16): the home end cheers the salute as it cheers a home goal (Tom: only that, no chant or applause; the goal's run of roars, each a little smaller, at `ceremonies.saluteCheer.power` 0.75; `crowd.js` `ceremonyCues`), and the losers go to their bench each their own way (a seeded bow off the straight, their own pace, the deadline shared between the legs so a re-air never sprints) and stand about by it, spread and swaying, not in a row (`ceremonies.js` LOOSE).
  - **Re-airs** keep the line-up short: from the line straight to the kick-off spots. Their half-time runs twice as fast, so the gather is quicker too.
- **Player of the match** (Task 25; `potm.js`, data in `data/interviews.json`): `segments.potmSeconds` (45) after the full-time card, before the champions after the final.
  - **The pick:** each player's match stats times `potm.weights` (goals 3, a scorcher 1 more, assists 2, saves 0.6, tackles 0.05 and shots on target 0.1 (0.3 each until 2026-10-18, Tom: players make 7 to 21 tackles a match, so tackles decided it over goals; a big scorer should usually win, not always), burns −0.5, fouls −0.3), plus `winBonus` (1.5) for the winning side, shootouts included. Ties go to the winners, then a seeded pick. In the final round it's the featured match's.
  - **The card:** name, position and team with a big kit swatch, the stat line ("6 goals (3 scorchers) · 1 assist · 18 tackles"), and their quote in a speech bubble, typed out at `captions.quoteCharsPerSecond` (25) as if spoken.
  - **Quotes:** canned lines by role (scorer, keeper, other) and result (win, draw, loss), with placeholders for the team, opponent, city, goals, saves and assists, picked with a seeded roll. A voice or AI-written quote can replace the text later without changing the segment (vision §12).
- **Dev jumps:** Next walk-out, plus Last handshakes and Last post-match show for the most recent finished match (a show's start depends on how long its match ran).

**Re-airs (the rest of each gap; built in Task 24):** **[Between]** they gain a 30 s full-time card and the recorded interview, then a studio block (§16.3).
- **Full matches at 1×,** with a REPLAY bug (S1 REPLAY for an earlier season's match, on the bug and at the head of STATS: Task 277b, Tom). The game clock pauses as it did live.
- **Trimmed ceremonies:**
  - a 20-second intro card instead of the pre-match: FULL MATCH REPLAY, the teams, when it was played and the round, never the score
  - a 20-second line-up instead of the walk-out: players go from the line across halfway to their kick-off spots, with a "FULL MATCH REPLAY · played MON 08:33" caption
  - half-time runs in 30 seconds instead of 60: it plays twice as fast rather than being cut, so the jog to the other end still shows
  - 30 seconds of handshakes
  - No post-match show; one rotation of studio cards follows instead. About 25 minutes each.
- **Code:** a `reair` segment in `programme.js` with a `part` (intro, lineup, match, handshakes); in the match part, `elapsed` is the moment of the match, as for a live one. `showsMatch(seg)` says whether a segment shows a match being played.
- **Filling the gap:** a re-air starts only if it ends before the next pre-match. Whatever is left (usually 10–25 minutes) goes to studio cards and highlights packages, as gaps are filled today. So trimmed or not, the time always fills exactly.
- **Which matches:** in a regular gap after the live match in play position k, the re-airs are the matches at positions k−1, k−3, k−5 and k−9, newest first (`reairs.offsets`). Each match then re-airs about 3, 8, 13 and 24 hours after it was live, so viewers in every time zone get a chance at it.
  - Longer gaps (after the final round, between knockouts, after the final, before a new season) cycle through the last `reairs.poolSize` matches, newest first, so the final round's other three matches get shown in full.
  - The first gaps of a season can reach back into the previous season. Before season 1 there's nothing to re-air, so the countdown runs.
- **Spoilers:** while a match re-airs, the ticker leaves out its result. The table already includes it (standings change at full time), which is accepted.
- **Early in season 1** there are fewer earlier matches to re-air (after match 5 only the matches 1 and 3 back exist), so highlights and studio cards fill more of those gaps.
- **Share of airtime** (default league): a match is on screen **67.4%** of season 1's week and **70.9%** of season 2's; live matches alone are about 13%. A test checks a season stays above 60%, and the dev panel's **Match share** link works it out for the current season. Since AROUND THE GROUNDS (Tom, 2026-10-19; §32) the 60% floor counts highlights as play (`showsPlay`): a match alone is about 55%, play about 67% of season 1; `npm run rundown` prints both.

**Highlights** (replaced full replays on 2026-10-03, when matches went to 60 minutes; a 2× replay would have taken 30):
- **A package** has every goal and any shootout, plus the biggest other moments (hot shots, saves, burns, blocks, misses) up to `highlights.maxClips` (8) clips in all, in match order. Each clip shows `leadMs` (10 s) before its moment and `tailMs` (3 s) after, on screen, and never reaches back across half-time. Overlapping clips merge.
- **The rundown:** the pool is the last `highlights.poolSize` (8) finished matches, newest first, starting with the match just played. It cycles, with a studio rotation after each package, so in a ~90-minute gap each package airs 3–4 times and a missed match can still be caught.
- A package starts only if it ends before the next pre-match. Otherwise studio cards fill the time.

**Flipping between live matches** (decided 2026-10-02):
- During the final round, a viewer can put any of the live matches on their own screen with keys 1–4 or a small selector. 0 or Auto returns to the channel's pick.
- It's instant and stays in sync, because every match is already simulated and drawn purely from "which match, what moment".
- The channel's own featured match stays the default, and later the Director or votes can steer it.

**Featured match in the final round:**
- Each match scores 1 point per team that could still move across a key line (first place, or the playoff cut between 4th and 5th) by winning or losing.
- The highest score wins. Ties go to the higher combined points, then home team order.
- Later, the Director takes this over (vision §8).

## 8. Ticker

A crawl across the bottom of the screen.

- **Usually:** the latest results (`FT Royale 4–3 Oasis`), then upcoming fixtures with kick-off times, then the leader and top scorer, each with its team's kit before the abbreviation (Tom, 2026-10-18: TOP ▮ VAR 34 PTS · TOP SCORER J. LAITY (▮ PCG)).
- **During the final round:** live scores for the other matches, with a flash on each goal. Goal alerts also pop as a lower third.
- It never shows anything ahead of "now".
- **Movement** (Task 29) is a browser animation the compositor runs, so it stays smooth when the page is busy; the content refreshes four times a second.
- While a match is re-aired, its result leaves the ticker (an older one takes its place), so the re-air isn't spoiled.

## 9. Team colours and kit clashes

- Players wear their team's primary colour, with the secondary colour as trim.
- **Clash rule:** if the two primaries are too close, the away team switches to its secondary colour, with its primary as trim. It only switches if that's actually further from the home shirt. "Too close" is an RGB distance below `kits.clashDistance` (100, so about one match in seven gets a change kit). Keepers wear a fixed contrasting shirt (lime at home, pink away) with sleeves and shorts in their team's shirt colour.
  - On the pitch, the scorebug, the caption stripe and the final-round chips, teams wear the kit of that match. The table, cards and goal alerts keep each team's home colours as its identity.
  - Code: `matchKits` in `src/channel/kits.js` (pure, tested).
- **Swatches** have a faint light outline, so a dark kit (the Stormcrows) still shows on the dark panels.
- **Kit review sheet:** `kits.html` (dev page) draws every team's sprites on the turf and every matchup with the rule applied. `?clash=N` previews another threshold.
- **Review:** Tom reviews all 8 teams on screen, so that none reads as a copy of a real team (vision §11). Changes go in `data/teams.json`.

## 10. Dev tools

- **Server:** `npm run channel` starts a tiny static server (`node:http`, no dependencies), because ES modules won't load from `file://`. Open the URL it prints.
- **`?speed=N`** runs league time N× faster from the moment the page loads. For example, `?speed=60` makes a match last 16 seconds.
- **`?at=<ISO time>`** starts the page at that moment, to jump straight to the final round or day 7.
- **`?seed=<seed>`** overrides the live seed, to try other leagues.
- **`?hl=<speed>`** plays highlights at another speed on this screen only, to compare speeds (Task 27). The setting is `highlights.speed`.
- **`?layout=broadcast`** starts in Broadcast mode, and **`?sound=on`** shows a small CLICK FOR SOUND plate under the scorebug (browsers need a click before sound can start). The dev panel's jump links add both to match the page as it is (Task 62), so a jump keeps the layout and offers the sound back.
- **Sound after a jump** (Task 62): turning sound on, or any jump while it's on, starts whatever is still ringing at that moment (an anthem, a goal's cheer, applause) from where it would be by now, instead of leaving it out.
- Any other parameter, or a bad value, stops the page with a readable error, so typos like `?sped=` don't fail silently.
- **Dev panel** (the ` key):
  - **Status:** the current moment in league time (in the form `?at=` accepts), speed, FPS, season, phase, segment, what's on air (match, score, time in, live or highlights with the clip number, and in the final round whether it's the viewer's or the channel's pick), the next kick-off, and the seed with build time.
  - **Jumps:** links to handy moments for the current seed and season: this moment (a shareable link), the next match, the opening match, the final day card, the final round, each playoff match, the champions, the off-season, and next season's countdown and opening match. Links keep `?seed` and `?speed`. Past ones are dimmed.
  - The champions and off-season moments depend on how long the final lasts. If it hasn't been played yet in this league, clicking builds a scratch league up to the final's kick-off to find out (about as long as the page's own build).
  - **Help** (H while the panel is open, or the Help link): a plain-language refresher beside the panel on every URL option, status line, jump link and key, with which ones suit Tasks 18–20. Its text lives in `index.html`; update it when the panel changes.
  - Pure parts (URL parameters, league-time stamps, the jump list) are in `src/channel/dev.js`; the panel is `src/channel/overlays/devPanel.js`.

## 11. Code layout

| Module | Pure? | Job |
|---|---|---|
| `src/channel/calendar.js` | Yes | Kick-off time of every fixture; any moment → season, phase, current or next match |
| `src/channel/liveLeague.js` | Yes | Build the league from the seed up to "now"; simulate each match at kick-off; keep the event window |
| `src/channel/timeline.js` | Yes | One match's events → state at any moment (score, clock, heat, zone, possession, carrier, players on pitch, last action) |
| `src/channel/programme.js`, `highlights.js` | Yes | Calendar + now → which segment is on screen, which highlights package and clip, which featured match |
| `src/channel/sidelines.js` | Yes | Who sits on each bench and who walks between the bench and the pitch |
| `src/channel/potm.js` | Yes | Player of the match: the stat line and quote (the pick is the sim's, `src/sim/potm.js`, from Task 214, and logged) |
| `src/channel/ceremonies.js` | Yes | Where players stand in the walk-out and handshakes, and their captions |
| `src/channel/formation.js`, `motion.js` | Yes | Where players and the ball are at any moment |
| `src/channel/onScreen.js` | Yes | What the viewer knows: finished/live matches, table, top scorers, upcoming fixtures |
| `src/channel/captions.js`, `callouts.js`, `ticker.js`, `cards.js` | Yes | What the captions, on-pitch callouts, ticker and cards say |
| `src/channel/pitch.js` | No (canvas) | Draws the pitch, players and ball |
| `src/channel/overlays/*.js` | No (DOM) | Scorebug, caption, table panel, ticker crawl, card panel |
| `src/channel/main.js` | No | Boot: load config with `fetch`, build the league, run the animation loop |
| `index.html` (repo root) | No | The page shell |
| `src/cli/serve.js` | No (Node) | Dev static server for `npm run channel` |

`loadConfig(readText)` in `src/config.js` already takes a reader function, so the browser passes a `fetch`-based one.

## 12. Settings

`config/settings.json` holds real-time timing, pacing and the live league's identity (Task 3). Every key has a schema in `src/config.js`, plus the calendar cross-checks in §4.

```json
{
  "live":     { "seed": "flareball", "firstSeasonStarts": "2026-10-05", "timeZone": "America/New_York" },
  "match":    { "realMinutes": 20, "halfTimeRealMinutes": 1, "goalPauseSeconds": 7, "subPauseSeconds": 5, "pauseAllowanceMinutes": 4,
                "overtimeBreakRealMinutes": 1, "shootoutKickRealSeconds": 8 },
  "calendar": { "seasonDays": 7, "firstKickoff": "day 1 00:30", "finalRoundKickoff": "day 6 20:00", "finalRoundSimultaneous": true },
  "playoffs": { "kickoffs": ["day 7 14:00", "day 7 17:00", "day 7 20:00"] },
  "segments": { "preMatchMinutes": 3, "walkOutMinutes": 2, "handshakeMinutes": 2, "postMatchMinutes": 12, "fullTimeMinutes": 2, "potmSeconds": 45,
                "studioCardSeconds": 20, "championMinutes": 10, "offseasonSummaryMinutes": 15 },
  "highlights": { "speed": 1.5, "poolSize": 8, "maxClips": 8, "leadMs": 10000, "tailMs": 3000 },
  "reairs":   { "offsets": [1, 3, 5, 9], "poolSize": 8, "introSeconds": 20, "lineUpSeconds": 20, "halfTimeSeconds": 30, "handshakeSeconds": 30 },
  "ceremonies": { "walkOnGapMs": 400, "walkOnSeconds": 12, "anthemSeconds": 25, "warmUpSeconds": 30, "warmUpMoveSeconds": 6,
                  "breakLineSeconds": 20, "handshakeParts": { "huddle": 0.2, "pass": 0.25, "salute": 0.25 }, "handshakeLineSpeed": 0.035 },
  "kits":     { "clashDistance": 100 }
}
```

- **Removed:** `calendar.regularSeasonDays`, which the final round's day now implies, and `playoffs.gapBetweenMatchesRealMinutes`, which the fixed kick-offs replace.
- **The seed lives in `settings.json`** (decided 2026-10-02), in the `live` block.
