## 14. [Stadium] Sound, Broadcast mode and pitchside life

Milestone 3, Stadium and sound (`docs/stadium-tasks.md`, Tasks 45–59; vision §12). Written in Task 45 (2026-10-04). **[Default]** values are starting points, judged on screen or by ear. Nothing here changes the sim: every person and sound illustrates events the sim already logged. All randomness comes from the seeded RNG (seeds built from the match id and the moment, or from the team), so every screen and every re-air looks and sounds the same.

### 14.1 The button row and the viewer's choices

Tom (2026-10-04): the icon buttons from the backlog can arrive now, even though most have nothing behind them yet.

- **A row of square icon buttons** (pixel icons, about 12 px, 1 px apart), in this order: **Settings**, **Rulebook**, **Record book**, **Audience**, **About**. Only Settings works in this milestone. The others are dimmed with a "coming later" tooltip: Rulebook and About need writing, Record book comes with It remembers, Audience with It responds.
  - **Where [Default]:** in Full, a plate at the bottom of the side column, above the ticker (the panel gives up about 16 px; the goals list scrolls anyway). In Broadcast, a small plate in the top-right corner over the stands. Settled with the Broadcast mock-ups (Task 54).
  - **Tom's thought (2026-10-04), for Task 54:** Broadcast mode might hide the whole button row, since that area becomes crowd, as on TV with the scorebug sitting over the stands. The layout toggle (and perhaps sound) would then live in the scorebug instead. Not decided yet; try it in the mock-ups.
- **Settings** opens a small panel: **Sound** on or off, **Layout** Full or Broadcast. Volume sliders (master, crowd, effects) wait for the UI rework.
- **Keys:** **M** turns sound on and off; **B** switches between Full and Broadcast. The help panel lists them.
- **Defaults (Tom, 2026-10-04):** every load starts in **Full** (today's layout, named Full for now) and silent. Nothing is remembered between visits, like the side panel's tabs.
- **Why silent:** browsers only allow audio after the viewer clicks or presses a key on the page. The audio starts the first time sound is turned on; until then nothing is computed for it.
- A small **speaker mark** on the Settings button shows whether sound is on.
- **Since Task 110 (Tom, 2026-10-05):** Settings is gone (it only held Sound): the **speaker button beside the scorebug** turns sound on and off in both layouts (lit when on), and the row's first button is a dimmed **TV guide** (coming later; backlog), so all five are "coming later" for now.

### 14.2 Sound

**Synthesized, no files** (Tom, 2026-10-04): every sound is built in the browser with the Web Audio API from oscillators, filtered noise and envelopes. No recordings, no licences, no dependencies. Tuned by ear on the sound board (`sounds.html`, Task 50).

**Heavy sounds rendered ahead** (Task 278b; Tom: every sound stuttered on his phone, in Firefox and Chrome): a phone can't run the crowd's sounds live (130–200 nodes each, the murmur three at once all through play: 500–600 filters) or the music (about 800 nodes a pass). A sound needing `sound.prerender.minNodes` (40) or more (`recipeNodes`: the crowd, the music, the anthems, the ident's fire, the lottery, the ads' effects) is rendered once in the background (an `OfflineAudioContext`, the same graph, one at a time) and played as a recording; it plays once ready, joining part-way if that took a moment, kept up to `cacheMB` (64), the oldest dropped. The crowd's sounds come from `variants` (6) takes per kind, picked by the cue's seed (`takeOf`; alike on every screen), a roar in `roarSizes` (3) sizes; anything else exactly as before. Every crowd take (54, `crowdTakes`) is rendered as sound is switched on, before anything else, at `crowdRate` (24,000 samples a second: half the work), and a crowd reaction ready more than `lateSeconds` (0.3) late is left out (Tom, on a smart TV: reactions rendered on demand came late, and joined part-way played only their quiet tails). Measured on a desktop: a murmur take in about 0.15 s, the music's pass (34 s) in 2.9 s; a phone perhaps several times slower, so the first pass of music comes in late once. The small sounds (whistle, kicks, the net) stay live. `sound.latency` `"playback"`: a larger audio buffer. The music's passes join by the audio clock (Tom: rare pops): each queued pass (`loop`, its length) starts exactly where the last ends, not a frame or more off by the frame clock.

**Two layers:**
- **Cues:** short sounds tied to a moment on the match timeline (a whistle at a kick-off, a kick at a pass, the net at a goal). A pure module (`sounds.js` **[Default name]**) lists the cues between two moments of a match, each with its kind, moment, loudness and seed; the audio module plays them. The list comes from the match's events and the motion plan, so it plays the same live, in highlights and in re-airs. A substitution gets a short whistle, and another as its pause ends and play restarts (Tom, 2026-10-20).
- **Beds:** continuous sounds whose level follows the match (the crowd). A pure function gives the level at any moment; the audio module eases toward it.

**No sound from the past:** each frame asks only for the cues since the last frame. After a jump (loading mid-match, `?at=`, waking from sleep, switching matches in the final round, a new segment) no earlier cues play and the beds go straight to their current level. A frame gap of more than about half a second counts as a jump.

**Speed:** in highlights (1.5×) cues keep their own pitch and length and only their timing follows the clip (judge by ear whether pitching up sounds better). Above `?speed=2` cues are muted and only the beds play.

**The cues [Default]:**

| Cue | When | Sound |
|---|---|---|
| Whistle | Kick-offs, half-time, full time, a goal, a foul, a substitution (the referee's moments, Task 47; substitutions from Task 61) | Two close oscillators with a pea trill; long for half-time, three blasts at full time |
| Kick | Each pass the motion shows | A short low thump, a little louder and brighter for long passes |
| Shot | Each shot as it's struck | A harder, louder kick |
| Net | A goal, as the ball lands | A soft swish of filtered noise |
| Save | A save | A dull glove thud, louder when held |
| Post | A miss the motion sends just outside a post (Task 61: a narrow band beside the post, about 1 miss in 10; the sim only says it missed, the picture picks where) | A metallic ring |
| Block | A block | A short dull thud |
| Burn | A burn | A low fizz |
| Sub | Sound being turned on (substitutions got a short whistle instead, Task 61) | A soft two-note chime |

**The murmur** (Tom, 2026-10-04): a continuous bed was built (filtered noise, then voices) and dropped, since it sounded like violins playing constantly. Instead the murmur is a small roar played quietly, a new take every 0.6–1.1 s so they overlap, a little louder as the level below rises. It dips at every stoppage (a goal, a substitution, a miss, a held save, a foul, a burn) and comes back a few seconds into the next play, so it isn't always on; the dip is to 0.6 of it, never silent (Task 60; half until Task 75). **Quiet spells** (Task 60): once no crowd reaction has come for about 5 s, the murmur eases down (to 0.7 of itself over 4 s; 0.6 until Task 75) until the next reaction, then comes back within a second. One share, `liveliness` in crowd.js (the lower of the stoppage dip and the quiet spell), drives both the murmur and the stands, so the fans bob less while it's quiet and more as it returns. A goal's cheer is a run of overlapping roars that lasts the celebration: big for a home goal; for an away goal the same shape but smaller (the away end), with two rounds of boos from the home crowd under it (Task 61; drop the boos if they sound wrong, the sound board has both). (An olé chant was tried and set aside for now.)
- **Level:** rises as the ball nears a goal, with the heat, and on breakaways. Higher in the final round and the playoffs; lower in re-airs (judge by ear).
- **Home bias:** the crowd is mostly home fans, so a home attack lifts it more than an away one.
- **Reactions** (cues over the bed): a roar at a goal (bigger for a home goal, a scorcher, a late winner or equaliser, and in the playoffs), a groan at a miss near goal, an "ooh" at a save (a smaller one at a steal, a soft one at a tackle or block; Tom, 2026-10-04), a small cheer at the walk-out, after each anthem and at the handshakes (Tom, 2026-10-20: cheers in place of the applause everywhere it played; `crowd.js` `SMALL_CHEER`, the small roar's takes), a roar with the full-time whistle after a home win (Tom, 2026-10-04; bigger in the playoffs or after a late winner) and a small cheer after any other result (applause until 2026-10-20), boos at the away side's fouls (low voices; Tom, 2026-10-04). The crowd's sounds are made of voices (a buzz through vowel formants), not filtered noise, which sounded like surf.
- **Cheers of other kinds** (Tom, 2026-10-20, picked on the sound board): each roar size's six takes mix cheers in with the plain roar (`soundRecipes.js` `roarCheer`, `CHEERS`), each on top of the roar: the small roar half plain, then whoops (high voices whooping up), whistles (fingers in the mouth) and clapping (hands under it); the bigger roars a third plain, then those three and hey (a shout as one, the roar swelling behind it; not on a small one). A roar plays the take its seed picks, so goals, home wins, the salute, the lift and the small cheers all hear them.
- **Final round:** the crowd of the match on screen; switching match switches it at once.
- **Between matches:** no crowd in cards and studio segments; highlights and re-airs carry their match's crowd.

**Anthems (Task 53):** each team has a short tune of 6–10 notes built from its seed (a scale, a rhythm and an instrument from small seeded tables), the same every time. It plays during that team's anthem caption in the walk-out, with the crowd hushed. A re-air's trimmed walk-out plays a shorter version or none (decide by ear).

**Channel stings:** a short sting as the post-match show opens, at each highlights package and at a re-air's intro card. Studio segments get a quiet bed or silence (decide by ear).

**Volumes and lengths** live in a new `sound` settings block (a master volume and a level per cue kind and bed), so tuning never touches code.

### 14.3 Broadcast mode

**The scorebug steps aside** (Task 278d, Tom: for immersion, and against screen burn-in): in Broadcast only, the scorebug's row (the scorebug, its layout and sound controls) fades out for each ad break and the opening (the new season's countdown and the ident) and back after (`scorebugAside` in `main.js`), held off through a transition that follows so it never returns over a sweep; a mouse moving over the picture brings it back for `broadcast.peekMs` (2.5 s), and it stays while the levels pane is open. The desk's welcome after the ident keeps it.

Two layouts, the viewer's choice (§14.1):
- **Full** (default): today's layout, with the side panel, the ticker and the button row.
- **Broadcast** (Tom picked layout B, the bowl, from the `broadcast.html` mock-ups, 2026-10-04): the side panel, ticker and button row go, and **stands go all the way round**: a strip above the dugouts, a strip below the pitch, and down both sides.
  - **The home name on the turf** (Task 72, Tom; it replaced Task 68's strip along the very bottom, a misreading): under the bottom boards, the dark gap and the stand's top two rows, as wide as the ground, are the apron's darker green carried on, like the turf round the dugouts, with the **home team's name** as one string centred on halfway (Task 109, Tom: split either side of the line, names like Port Calder Gulls sat off centre; Task 72 had the city left and the nickname right), in a small built-in block font (5×6 capitals at double size), in the team's first colour with a shadow in its second (Tom's pick B from `turf.html`; swapped, or white, if the first wouldn't show on grass). The corners keep their full stand. The caption and goal alerts sit below it, over the stand (y 250); the replay inset just above it, over the left stand and the pitch's corner.
  - **The pitch moves to the middle** (Tom): everything drawn on the canvas (pitch, dugouts, tunnel, people, ball) shifts right by `broadcast.shiftPx` (43, which centres the 394 px ground on the 480 px stage), leaving room for a stand on each side. It's never a hard cut: switching layouts slides the picture over `broadcast.slideMs` while the stands and the panels fade across. The overlays that sit on the pitch (callouts, cards) move with it.
  - **The scorebug stays top left**, where it has always been, floating over the top stand with the heat line under it. It ends with two small controls: the layout switch (back to Full) and sound, since the button row is gone (Tom's idea). The final-round picker floats beside it. **Task 70 (Tom: the best spot):** the layout switch is in the scorebug in **both** layouts and nowhere else (it left the Settings panel, which now holds only sound); its icon shows the layout it switches to (a framed block for Broadcast, a framed side panel for Full) and swaps once the slide finishes. B still switches.
  - **The caption** is a lower third floating over the bottom stand, at its left; **goal alerts** from other live matches float over it at its right (the side stands are only 43 px wide once the pitch is centred, too narrow for a plate). So they stand out against a moving crowd, the goal caption and goal alerts have a darker solid backing and a border pulsing in the scoring team's colour (Tom agreed, 2026-10-04).
  - **Cards and studio segments** show over the pitch as in Full (shifted with it); the stands stay, with a calm crowd.

The mock-ups also tried stands on the right and bottom only with the plates as in Full (A), and the scorebug in the top-right corner (C).

**The crowd in the stands (Task 56):** rows of small pixel fans (`stands.js`), mostly in home colours, with the away supporters' main block in the corner nearest the away dugout and a few small groups of them (a row, a small section) elsewhere (Tom, 2026-10-04), and a sprinkling of neutrals. **The away end by the match** (Tom, 2026-10-16; `settings.broadcast.awayEnd`, `stands.js` `awaySteps`): a step bigger when the two teams share a region, and a step when the away team tops the table as aired before kick-off (once it has played 4); each step spreads the corner 50 px further left along the top stand and 50 px further down the right-hand stand. Each has a seeded look (shirt or scarf in a team colour, skin and hair from small tables) and idles. Their activity follows the same crowd level as the sound (§14.2), so the two match: a stir as an attack builds, standing and jumping at goals (the scoring side's fans), arms up at near misses, flags and scarves at the walk-out and handshakes. The stands are drawn on the canvas, in Broadcast only.

### 14.4 Pitchside life

All presentation, in pure tested modules, drawn by `pitch.js`:
- **The tunnel (Task 46):** a tunnel mouth at the top of the off-pitch turf, in the open stretch of touchline between the dugouts. The walk-out starts there: players, subs, managers and the referee come out of it; after the handshakes everyone leaves through it. Re-airs' trimmed ceremonies use it too. **Task 63:** wide enough for two side by side (a lane each, home on the left); walkers fade only once well inside (feet past `TUNNEL.fadeFrom`) and are clipped to it, so heads go under its roof; a **gate** in the gap in the boards (two leaves, white with the yellow kick plate like the boards) is shut during play and opens over `ceremonies.gateMs` for the walk-out (until the teams head for their kick-off spots), the breaks (half-time, before overtime; the timeline's `breaks`) and from the final whistle through the handshakes.
- **The referee (Task 47):** in a kit that isn't close to any team's kits or keeper kits (checked with the clash distance, §9): black with yellow trim, or a change kit (orange, purple, grey) when that clashes (Task 47). Follows play a little behind and to the side of the ball, at most at a jog; never in a pass's lane, in a crease or on top of a player. Leads the teams out, stands by the centre spot at kick-offs, raises an arm after goals and fouls, stands still during pauses, and walks off at full time. The whistle cues come from the referee's moments.
- **Managers (Task 48):** one per team, in a black suit with a tie in the team's colours (Tom, 2026-10-04), on the touchline in the open stretch between their dugout and the tunnel (Task 48). They pace slowly with the ball along it (like keepers tracking it, with more lag), throw their arms up at their team's goals and put their hands on their head when conceding. They come out of the tunnel before the teams, shake hands at the tunnel mouth while the teams huddle in the handshakes, and leave early through the tunnel. **No names** (Task 278i, Tom, 2026-10-13): the pre-match card named them (generated from the team's region), one per team for the league's whole history while players came and went; dropped. A manager system (hired, fired, retiring, coaching records) is on the backlog.
- **Dugout props (Task 49):** balls, water bottles, kit bags, a cool box; a few per dugout. **Task 69:** cones and a tactics board too, in six arrangements (`sidelines.js` `ARRANGEMENTS`), one picked per match for each dugout (never the same for both), seeded by the match, so the sidelines look a little different each match.
- **Task 140 (Tom saw players standing in front of the team equipment):** an injured player walked to a new seat past the bench's end, and the dugout was drawn only as long as the count of those seated, so after an injury and its substitution (6 of 59 matches in season 1) one sat out among the props. Now the injured player takes the seat their substitute left (`sidelinesAt`), and the dugout always reaches the furthest seat taken (an injury with no substitutions left still adds a seat, and the dugout grows to it).
- **The injured in the dugout** (Fixes, Task 185, Tom): the five who don't start (a roster of 10) are all in the dugout, so an injured player takes a bench place: a bench of four in kit means one injured. They sit in the **seats nearest the tunnel** (Tom), in a **dark tracksuit** (`PALETTE.tracksuit`) trimmed in their team's main colour (Tom picked B of three on `bench.html`), walk out with the substitutes in the walk-out, stay seated at the breaks (the substitutes step out) and through the handshakes. The sim's `lineup` event now lists them (`injured`; `selectLineup` returns them, and the pre-match card for the walk-out), extra data only: results unchanged. `benchesAtKickoff` gives the dugout in seat order, the injured first, with `injured`; `sidelinesAt` and `benchWalkOn` mark them. A player injured during a match comes off in their kit and takes their substitute's seat, so the dugout stays at five (six only when no substitution is left).
- **Home mark (Task 49):** the centre circle filled out to its line in the home team's main colour, one flat shade (Tom's pick from the `grounds.html` mock-ups, 2026-10-04; painted letters, home boards and mown patterns were also tried). A main colour close to the turf's green is darkened instead. The final has one too: it's at the higher seed's ground (Tom, 2026-10-05; until then a neutral ground with none).

### 14.5 Instant goal replays (Task 57)

During each goal pause, after a short beat, a **small TV inset** bottom left, above the caption, replays the last few seconds before the goal (**[Default]** about 4 s of match time), zoomed **[Default] 2×** on the area between the scorer's run and the goal, with a REPLAY mark. **Task 71 (Tom's pick F from `replays.html`):** the camera follows the ball, smoothed over ±0.6 s, at 1.5×, and the replay plays at 0.75× (`replays.zoom`, `speed`, `followMs`). **Task 88:** the smoothing is weighted toward the moment shown (a triangle over the window), since wide play's fast crosses left the even average up to 0.8 s behind; the ball now leaves the shot for at most about 0.25 s, during a fast cross. It's drawn from the same pure motion as the match, so it shows exactly what happened. On in live matches and re-airs; not in highlights (they already show the goal) or for the final round's other matches. If it doesn't fit the 11-second pause alongside the celebration, the pause grows (`goalPauseSeconds`) and the calendar checks re-run with `pauseAllowanceMinutes`. New `replays` settings block (lead, zoom, delay, size).

### 14.6 The studio, a proof of concept (Task 58)

Not the priority (Tom, 2026-10-04). One studio scene: a desk with two pixel presenters (generated names, seeded looks) in front of a backdrop in the channel's colours. It replaces **one** kind of studio card **[Default: the latest results card]**. The presenters take turns with speech bubbles typing out short lines built from real facts with a seeded grammar (the last result, a scorer, the table, the next match): like the player-of-the-match quotes, but assembled from fragments rather than whole lines. Built so other studio cards, pundits, the filmed player-of-the-match interview and, later, voices can follow without changing the segment.

### 14.7 Code and settings

- **New pure modules [Default names]:** `sounds.js` (cues and bed levels), `crowd.js` (the fans and their activity, shared with the crowd bed), `pitchside.js` (referee and manager positions), `replay.js` (the inset's window and zoom), `studio.js` (presenters and lines). Tested in Node like the rest.
- **Browser-only modules:** `audio.js` (the Web Audio graph and synthesis; never imported by tests or the sim), `overlays/buttons.js` (the row and the Settings panel); the stands, tunnel and people drawn in `pitch.js` or new drawing files.
- **New settings blocks:** `sound`, `crowd`, `replays`, and the tunnel and manager timings in `ceremonies`, each with its schema in `src/config.js`.
- **New dev pages:** `sounds.html` (Task 50) and a Broadcast mock-up page (Task 54).
- **The dev panel** shows the layout and whether sound is on; its help lists M, B and the new pages.

## 15. [Gameplay] Width, loose balls, the tether, bubbles and the crowd filling

Milestone 3½, Gameplay pass 2 (`docs/gameplay-tasks.md`, Tasks 77–90; vision §12). Written in Task 77 (2026-10-05). **[Default]** values are starting points, judged on screen and against the pitch map. Like the rest of the channel, everything here is a pure function of "which match, what moment", seeded from the match id and the moment, so every screen and every re-air shows the same picture. **No sim changes** except Task 88's safer keeper restarts (simulation.md §4.2): these illustrate what the sim already decided.

### 15.1 The baseline (2026-10-05)

Measured before any change: the first 12 matches of a season-1 league (seed `baseline`), sampled every 250 ms of on-screen play (not in pauses, breaks or ceremonies). Pitch fractions as in `formation.js` (x along, y across); "pitch lengths" as in §5.

| Measure | Baseline |
|---|---|
| Ball in the wide quarters (y < 0.25 or > 0.75) | 12.5% of play (0.9% in the outer 15%) |
| Ball by thirds across (top / middle / bottom) | 17% / 67% / 15% |
| Ball by sixths along (end to end) | 7 / 18 / 25 / 26 / 18 / 7% |
| Ball in a **corner area** (the last sixth at either end, in a wide quarter) | 0.2%; in the **attacking** corner area (the end being attacked) 0.0% |
| Outfield players across (5th to 95th percentile) | y 0.27 to 0.75: nobody uses the outer quarters |
| The **gap**: furthest-forward outfield attacker beyond the last outfield defender | beyond the line 8.5% of the time, more than 0.1 pitch lengths 1.4%, at worst 0.29 (breakaways included) |
| Outfield players more than 0.35 pitch lengths from the ball | 13.4% |

So the picture Tom saw is real: play runs down a middle band about half the pitch wide, the corner areas see nothing, and now and then an attacker stands well beyond the defence.

**Loose balls are already in the sim:** after every **block** (9.8 a match) and **parried save** (5.9) the sim runs a race on pace between an attacker and a defender (`looseBall`, `rules.contests.looseBall`). The defender wins about 8.4 a match (a `possession_change` with reason `loose_ball`), the attacker about 7.3 (no event: the attacking team keeps the ball). The channel doesn't show them as races yet. Tackles: 41 a match win the ball off the carrier, and 29 more are steals.

### 15.2 The pitch map (Task 78)

- **Built in Task 78.** **`src/channel/pitchmap.js`** (pure, tested): samples a match's motion every 250 ms of on-screen play and adds it up: a grid of **6 along × 4 across** (sixths and quarters, so the corner areas and the top and bottom show) for the ball and for each position (by side, with and without the ball), plus the measures in §15.1, with breakaways reported separately, and (from Tasks 82 and 83) loose balls and races per match and bubble mismatches.
- **In Node:** `npm run pitchmap [-- --matches 20 --seed S]` prints the numbers (a few seconds).
- **`pitchmap.html`** (dev page): the same numbers, and heat maps drawn over the pitch (the ball, then each position), for a chosen number of matches.
- **First-time finishes (Task 170):** for each open-play goal, how long the scorer had the ball (as drawn) before it left their foot; under 1 s is first time (target 40%–60%, Tom: about half), and under 0.5 s is shown too.

**Targets** for the done-when (**[Default]**, from the baseline):

| Measure | Target |
|---|---|
| Ball in the wide quarters | at least 30% of play |
| Ball in the middle third across | at most 50% |
| Ball in an attacking corner area | 5% to 7.5% (about 1 to 1½ minutes a match; Tom, 2026-10-05: up to 7.5%), from several kinds of play (§15.3) |
| Outfield players across | 5th to 95th percentile at least 0.15 to 0.85 |
| The gap, outside breakaways | beyond `play.tether` + 0.02 in under 1% of play |
| Breakaways | unchanged: the runner and the trailing defence as today |
| Loose balls | every block and parried save shown as a race, plus some tackles (§15.5) |

### 15.3 Using the width and the corner areas (Task 79)

Tom (2026-10-05): not the exact corners, but the corner areas and the top and bottom of the pitch, which today almost never see action; **a few different kinds of play** that go there. Presentation only: where the ball goes between sim events, and the shape around it. Each kind is chosen with a seeded roll per possession or per action, never changes who the sim credits, and keeps every existing rule (passes never through players, nobody in a crease, the shooter shoots from the shooting spot).

**Built in Task 79** (`formation.js` takes a `wide` option: the flank, whether the player on the ball is in the corner area, and who that is; `motion.js` decides it per possession and carries it through the phases):
- **Width in the shape:** the formation spots spread out (`SPOTS`). With the ball, the defenders go out toward the touchlines (y 0.17–0.2 / 0.8–0.83), the midfielder sits a little above the middle (0.32–0.38) and the striker below it (0.64–0.66); without it, the defenders sit at 0.22–0.32 / 0.68–0.78, still more compact. The seeded jitter across stays.
- **Down the flank:** a possession goes down a flank with chance `play.flankChance` (0.45; seeded per possession spell, top or bottom): the carrier takes a lane 0.15 ± 0.05 from that touchline, whoever has the ball until the next pass keeps it out there too, the team on the ball leans that way by 0.08 and the other team by 0.06 (a strong side). It lasts the whole possession, until a switch.
- **To the corner area, then a cut-back or a cut-in:** on a flank, as the ball reaches the attacking zone (the advance into it, or a later phase there), with chance `play.cornerChance` (0.65), the player on the ball goes to the corner area (0.91 ± 0.025 from their own goal line, 0.09 from the touchline). It's kept until the next shot: in the phase before it, a teammate who will shoot waits in a cut-in lane (0.3 from that touchline) for the pass back across (a **cut-back**), or, if the player in the corner is the shooter, they **cut in** along the byline to shoot from that lane. Never on a breakaway.
- **A switch of play:** on a flank, a pass in a long spell goes across to the most open teammate at least 0.08 past the middle on the far side with chance `play.switchChance` (0.25), and play goes down that flank (bent or chipped if someone is in the way, as any pass).
- **The keeper plays it wide:** a possession that starts with the keeper (after a save, miss or goal) goes down a flank with chance `play.wideOutletChance` (0.6) instead, so the first pass out goes to a wide player.
- **Measured** (`npm run pitchmap`, 20 matches; baseline in §15.1): the ball in the wide quarters 44–47% of play (12.5%), the middle third across 33–35% (67%), an attacking corner area 6.3–6.7% (0.0%), outfield players across 0.15 to 0.85 (5th to 95th percentile, right at the target's edge on three seeds; 0.27 to 0.75). In 10 matches: about 38 visits to an attacking corner area a match, 12 of them ending in a shot (9 cut-backs, 3 cut-ins), and about 30 switches of play. No kick-off, breakaway, crease or pass-lane test changed.
- **Breakaways** stay as they are (straight at goal), though they may now start from a wide lane (the shooter's cut-in lane).
- **The keeper's outlet** (Task 88): when the sim turns the ball over straight after a restart (goal, miss, held save), before the team has got it out, the keeper first plays it out to a defender (on the flank side if the possession has one) a second after collecting it, and the tackle happens on that defender, so the keeper is never seen passing straight to an opponent. The sim makes these rare (`tactics.restartPressFactor`, simulation.md §4.2): about 1.6 a match.
- **If presentation can't do it** (the width reads as fake because the sim's next action ignores it), Task 81 gives events a side; decided after Task 79.

### 15.4 The soft offside: a tether (Task 80)

No offside rule (sport.md is unchanged); the picture keeps attackers from standing far beyond the defence, unused (Tom, 2026-10-05: so nobody is obviously open for a breakaway and never passed to).

- **The line:** the last outfield defender of the side without the ball (keepers don't count), measured along the attack.
- **The tether:** no outfield attacker's spot may be more than `play.tether` (**[Default]** 0.04 pitch lengths, about 14 px) beyond the line, or beyond the ball if the ball is further forward. It applies to the spots players head for, not to where they stand, so they drift back onside at their usual speed and never jump.
- **Both sides give:** a gap beyond the tether is closed partly by the last defender dropping back (`play.lineDrop`, **[Default]** half of it, never into their crease) and the rest by the attacker checking back. The striker stays high and the defence stays honest.
- **Never applied:**
  - during a **breakaway**, from the advance the sim calls a breakaway through its shot (Tom, 2026-10-05: breakaways must not be affected), so the runner goes and the beaten defence trails as today;
  - to the player on the ball (the ball is never offside);
  - at kick-offs (their own shape, Task 76), in goal pauses, celebrations, breaks and ceremonies.
- **Turnovers:** players far from the ball still react late (§5), so for a moment after a turnover someone may be beyond the tether. That's measured (§15.2) and accepted while it's brief.
- **Built in Task 80:** `formation.js` `tetherLine` (`play.tether` 0.04, `play.lineDrop` 0.5), and in `motion.js` outfield defenders dropping back toward their own goal **sprint** (`runSpeed`), not just the ones near the ball, except on a breakaway: the spots rarely broke the line, but a striker running near the ball used to outrun a defence that only jogged back. An attacker off the ball more than 0.06 beyond the line or the ball: 0.8–0.9% of play (1.1% at the baseline). Breakaways measured the same before and after.

### 15.5 Loose balls (Task 82)

Presentation only (Tom, 2026-10-05). The ball breaks loose into open space, players from both sides race onto it, and **the player the sim gave the ball to always gets there first**.

- **Which moments:**
  - **Every block** (about 10 a match) and **every parried save** (about 6): these are already loose balls in the sim (§15.1). The ball deflects off the blocker or spills off the keeper into space, `play.looseReach` away (**[Default]** 0.06–0.14 pitch lengths, away from goal and toward the side the shot came from, never into a crease or off the pitch). If the defender won (`possession_change`, reason `loose_ball`), they win the race; if the attacker kept it, the attacking player shown on the ball for the next action wins it.
  - **Some tackles that aren't steals:** with chance `play.looseTackleChance` (**[Default]** 0.2, about 8 a match), the tackle knocks the ball a short way loose (`play.tackleReach`, **[Default]** 0.04–0.08) instead of the tackler taking it cleanly; the tackler wins the race. Steals stay clean interceptions.
- **The race:** the winner and the nearest opponent (the shooter, or the old carrier, when they're close) run for the ball at sprint speed (`animation.runSpeed`); the loser arrives a step behind and pulls up. Everyone else reacts as after any turnover.
- **Timing:** the ball rolls for `play.looseMs` (**[Default]** 700–1100 ms, seeded) and the winner reaches it as it stops, inside the time the timeline already has before the next event, so nothing in the timing moves. If there isn't time, the race is shortened (the ball goes less far), never skipped for blocks and parries.
- **Bubbles:** BLOCK! and SAVE! show as now; a loose tackle still shows TACKLE! over the tackler.
- **Built in Task 82** (`motion.js` `looseLength`, `runLoose`): a loose ball gets a phase of its own at the start of its moment. The ball slows as it runs and ends exactly at the winner's feet; when the attackers keep it after a block or parry, the sim doesn't say who, so their nearest outfield player wins it. A spot out of the winner's reach (at up to 1.5 times `runSpeed`) comes closer. Poses mark the ball `loose`. About 22 a match (9.6 blocks, 5 parried saves, 7.3 tackles), rolling about 45 px.

### 15.6 The bubble attribution check (Task 83)

Each bubble names the sim's player; the check makes sure the picture agrees. Measured at the moment the bubble appears, over many matches (in the pitch map and a test):

| Bubble | Over | The picture should show them |
|---|---|---|
| TACKLE! | the tackler | the nearest opponent to the ball as they win it (within 0.04 pitch lengths) |
| STEAL! | the player who cut out the pass | on the pass's path, or the nearest opponent to its receiver |
| BLOCK! | the blocker | between the shooter and the goal, near the shot's line |
| SAVE! | the keeper | on the ball (already so) |
| MISS! | the shooter | the one who struck it (already so) |
| BREAKAWAY! | the runner | on the ball (already so) |

- **Fixes, in order of preference:** make the picture lead up to the sim's player (in the last phase before a tackle, the sim's tackler is the one closing the ball down; the blocker steps across into the shot's line), so the bubble stays over a player, as Tom likes. Only if that isn't possible within the look-ahead rules (§5), move the bubble to the player the picture shows doing it. The sim's credit never changes (stats count the sim's player).
- **Target:** at least 95% of TACKLE!, STEAL! and BLOCK! bubbles pass (the baseline is measured at the start of Task 83).
- **Built in Task 83** (`motion.js` `toTheAction`, `tackleSoon`): the baseline was 12% for TACKLE! (median 0.19 pitch lengths from the ball) and 25% for BLOCK!. In the last 5 s before the sim's tackle, steal or block, that player heads for the action, sprinting if needed: the tackler to just goal-side of the player on the ball, a player stealing into the passing lane to the passer's nearest teammate, a blocker into the shot's path. Nobody passes it about just before a tackle, and if the tackler was the last defender the attackers check back to the new line. Now 99% TACKLE!, 96–97% STEAL!, 99–100% BLOCK!.

### 15.7 The crowd fills and empties (Task 84)

So Broadcast can stay on all the time without a full, idle crowd round a studio card. A **fullness** (0–1) for the stands at every moment of the programme, from a pure function in `crowd.js`. Each fan gets a seeded arrival and leaving moment (groups and neighbours close together, the away end arriving as a block a little later), so seats fill and empty a few at a time.

| Segment | Fullness |
|---|---|
| Studio cards, countdowns, champions, off-season | empty |
| Pre-match (3 min) | fills from about 10% to about 70% |
| Walk-out | full by the anthems |
| The match, half-time, the handshakes | full (the usual few empty seats) |
| Post-match show | drains: the losing side's fans leave first during the full-time card (after a draw, evenly); about half gone by the end of player of the match; empty by the studio cards |
| Highlights | that match's full crowd **[Default]** (they're moments from it) |
| Re-airs | fills through the 20 s intro and the 20 s line-up, full through the match, drains through the 30 s handshakes (**[Between]** then the new 30 s full-time card, §16.13) |

- **Drawing:** the stands' cached base (seats and steps) stays; fans are drawn only once they've arrived and until they've left. Empty seats are the bare stand.
- **Sound:** the murmur scales with fullness (silent when empty), and so do the crowd's reactions (the walk-out's applause comes from whoever is there).
- **Settings:** a `crowd.fill` block (the fractions and times above) with its schema.
- **Task 89 (Tom, at the check):** the fans leave from the final whistle: through the handshakes the losers' end to 60% and the other to 85%, then through the full-time card to nobody (the losers' end by 70% of it), so the ground is empty before the show moves on (`crowd.fill.handshakeLosers`, `handshakeOthers`, `losersGoneAt`). Each change of crowd dissolves over `fadeMs` (600 ms) instead of popping, like a time-lapse.
- **Built in Task 84:** `crowd.js` `standsFullness` (the table above, with the full-time card taking the losers to 35% and the others to 85%, player of the match to 15% and 60%, and a re-air's intro to 60%); `stands.js` gives each fan a seeded arrival and leaving order (blocks of 24 × 12 px together, the away end from 15%) from a stream of its own, so the crowd looks as before, and `presentFans` picks who's in, in 40 steps, so the stands' cached pictures are remade only every few seconds. The murmur in the walk-out and handshakes thins with the crowd. **From Task 278j (Tom):** it rises from silence under the pre-match card too, its share the fullness times how far through the pre-match, so it meets the walk-out's as that begins.

### 15.8 Code and settings

- **New pure module:** `pitchmap.js` (§15.2), with a CLI `src/cli/pitchmap.js` (`npm run pitchmap`) and a dev page `pitchmap.html`.
- **Changed:** `formation.js` (the wider spots, flanks, the tether), `motion.js` (wide play, switches, cut-backs, loose balls and races), `motion.js` or `callouts.js` (attribution fixes), `crowd.js` and `stands.js` (fullness), `audio.js` (the murmur follows fullness).
- **New settings:** a `play` block (`flankChance`, `cornerChance`, `switchChance`, `wideOutletChance` from Task 79; then `tether`, `lineDrop`, `looseReach`, `looseTackleChance`, `tackleReach`, `looseMs`) and `crowd.fill`, each with its schema in `src/config.js`. Presentation only, so nothing goes in `data/rules.json`.
- **Tests:** each feature's own (§15.3–§15.7), the pitch map's targets on a few matches, and the same-seed and continuity tests as always (no jumps, nobody in a crease, passes clear of players).

## 16. [Between] Between matches: the scene, talking heads, the interview, transitions and ads

Milestone 3¾, Between matches (`docs/tasks.md`, Tasks 91–108; vision §12). Written in Task 91 (2026-10-05), from Tom's answers of the same day. **[Default]** values are starting points, judged on screen; **[Mock-up]** marks what Tasks 92–94 settle. **No sim changes:** talking heads, the interview, cards and ads only read facts the sim already logged. The programme stays a pure function of the calendar and "now", and every choice (a clip's lines, a card's order) is seeded from the slot, the match id or the segment's start, so every screen shows the same programme.

**Renamed for ad blockers (the launch, 2026-10-11):** the ads' files are `spots.js`, `spotArt.js`, `spotBrands.js`, `spotKit.js`, `spotLeague.js`, `spotsSheet.js` and `data/spots.json` (they were `ads.js`, `adArt.js`… and `data/ads.json`; older task docs keep the old names). A blocker that stops one module stops the whole channel, so `npm run publish` refuses any name that looks like an ad or a tracker (`blockedLooking`). The config's key is still `ads`, and `ads.html` (not published) keeps its name.

### 16.1 The scene and where it's used

**The scene** is an abstract, animated pixel backdrop, like the glassy motion graphics sports channels run behind their studio graphics. **Tom's pick (Task 92, 2026-10-05): A, light bars:** slow diagonal bars of light on deep blue (a glint across every few seconds was taken out, Tom, after Task 98), an orange line low down with a bright run along it, and a few twinkling pixels (`scene.html`). **In Broadcast it fills the whole screen** (Tom, 2026-10-05: the empty stands as a border looked off), with the scorebug floating over its top-left as always; **in Full** it covers the whole ground (the pitch, the dugouts, the tunnel, the apron), beside the side panel and ticker as now. Either way the home name on the turf is hidden. **The same goes for everything between matches** (Tom): the studio set, the interview, the studio cards, ad breaks, the opening ident, the champions and the off-season all fill the screen in Broadcast and the ground in Full (`scene.html`, `studio.html` and `ads.html` show both). Seeded and looping, the same on every screen, and calm enough to sit under text.

**Built in Task 96:** `scene.js`: `sceneState` (pure: each bar's position, the run along the line, the twinkles, seeded and from the league's clock, so every screen draws the same), `sceneRect` (the ground in Full, the whole screen in Broadcast, sliding between them with the layout), `showsScene` (the segment's picture is the scene), and `drawScene` (canvas, a row at a time so the bars' edges stay crisp; the backdrop's bands drawn once per size). Its numbers are in a `scene` settings block (bands, slope, the six bars' widths, speeds, offsets and opacities, the line and its run, the twinkles) and its colours in `palette.js`. In `main.js` it's drawn over the ground and stands on every scene segment; Task 58's desk (without its old backdrop) stands in for the talk on top of it until the studio set (Task 98), and the champions' confetti goes on top. **The studio bed:** a soft pad (four chords in turn, each swelling over about six seconds, a new one every `sound.bedEverySeconds`, 4.5) under the scene; its level `sound.levels.bed` is 0, so it's off until Tom turns it up on the sound board.

**The scene is behind everything that isn't the pre-match, a match, or the post-match's full-time card and highlights** (Tom, 2026-10-05):

| Over the stadium (as today) | Over the scene (new) | Its own set |
|---|---|---|
| Pre-match card (fans arriving), walk-out, live matches, handshakes, the full-time card (fans leaving), highlights (their crowd back), re-airs (intro, line-up, match, handshakes, the new full-time card) | Studio cards (all of them, restyled, §16.9), ad breaks, the champions, the off-season, the new-season countdown | The studio set, for all talk (§16.7, its screens showing the scene); the player-of-the-match interview (§16.8); the channel's opening ident (§16.5a) |

The scene replaces Task 58's studio, which turned the whole ground into a studio for one card; the latest-results card goes back to being a card. **The desk is the exception** (Tom, 2026-10-05: the desk straight in front of the scene looks a little off): talk happens on a **studio set**, a sportscaster set with the desk in front and the light bars playing on screens behind it (§16.7). **Tom's pick (Task 93): B**, one big curved screen with the light bars, two upright side screens with live graphics (the table, the next match), a lighting rig with spotlights, and a curved desk (`studio.html`). Cards and ads sit over the scene itself.

### 16.2 The post-match show (live matches)

Still `segments.postMatchMinutes` (12) in all, so kick-offs and the calendar don't move:

| From | Part | Length **[Default]** | Over |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0:00 | **Full-time card**, the fans leaving (empty by its end, §15.7) | `fullTimeMinutes` 2 (as now) | the stadium |
| 2:00 | **Match talk:** the presenters on the match (§16.7) | as its lines need, `studio.matchTalkSeconds` 40 at most (Task 187: about 12 s) | the desk |
| ~2:12 | **Interview tee-up:** one line ("Let's hear from…"), then a cut | as needed, `studio.interviewTeeUpSeconds` 10 at most (about 5 s) | the desk |
| ~2:17 | **The player-of-the-match interview** (§16.8) | the nod, the quote, a moment to read; `segments.potmSeconds` 45 at most (about 9 s) | the interview set |
| ~2:26 | **Wrap-up:** a line or two, then "the highlights, next" | as needed, `studio.wrapUpSeconds` 20 at most (about 6 s) | the desk |
| ~2:32 | **This match's highlights:** an opening sting naming the match, then the package with a sting between clips (§16.10) | ~2 min (1–3 by match) | the stadium, its crowd back |
| ~4:30 | **A studio block** (§16.4): an ad break, a talk clip on something else, studio cards to the end of the show | ~7½ min | the scene |

Since Task 187 (§18.13) every talk and the interview run only as long as their words; the time saved goes to the block's cards, so the show still ends at 12 minutes.

- **The final round:** the match talk covers the round (who made the playoffs, the Pennant race, the featured match) for `studio.roundTalkSeconds` (70) instead of 40; the interview is the featured match's; the packages follow, biggest stakes first, while they fit (as now). If no room is left for the ad break, it moves to the gap's first block.
- **After the final:** full-time card, match talk, interview tee-up, interview, wrap-up ("the champions, next"), then the champions and the off-season over the scene, each opening with a talk clip (`studio.championTalkSeconds`, `offseasonTalkSeconds`, 30 each) inside its own length; the final's highlights then open the gap (as now).
- **Segments:** the show's parts become `full_time`, `talk` (with a `topic`: `match`, `round`, `interview_tee_up`, `wrap_up`), `potm` (now the interview), `highlights`, then the block's `ads`, `talk` and `studio` parts, each with `show: true`.

### 16.3 Re-airs

Re-airs keep their trimmed ceremonies (§7a) and their REPLAY bug, and gain a short ending (Tom, 2026-10-05: no desk talk, so the post-match talk never repeats):
- **Handshakes** 30 s (as now), then a **full-time card** (`reairs.fullTimeSeconds` 30) over the stadium while the crowd drains (§16.13).
- **The recorded interview** (`reairs.interviewSeconds` 45 at most; since Task 187 as long as the live one, about 9 s): the same interview as on the day, marked **RECORDED · MON 08:33** (when the match was played).
- **Then a studio block** (§16.4), replacing today's one rotation of cards.

A **re-air unit** is about 27½ minutes: intro 20 s, line-up 20 s, the match (about 23½ min with half-time and pauses), handshakes 30 s, full-time card 30 s, interview 45 s, then a studio block of about 2 min (ads if due, a talk clip, a rotation of cards).

### 16.4 The gap: its blocks and how it fills

A gap runs from the end of a post-match show (or the off-season) to the start of the **tee-up talk** before the next pre-match (§16.5). It's built in order from these blocks:

| Block | Length | What |
|---|---|---|
| **Studio block** | flexible | the transition into the scene; an **ad break** if one is due (§16.11); a **talk clip** (`studio.clipSeconds` 45 at most, about 12 s since Task 187, §16.7); **studio cards** (`studioCardSeconds` 20 each), at least one rotation |
| **Re-air unit** | ~27½ min | §16.3, its studio block included |
| **Highlights unit** | ~3–4 min | the transition out of the scene, the opening sting, a recent match's package, the transition back, then a short studio block: an ad break if due and one rotation of cards (no talk clip) |

**Filling a gap:**
1. **Re-air units** as today (`reairs.offsets` after a regular match, the `reairs.poolSize` pool in longer gaps), each only if it ends before the tee-up talk.
2. **Highlights units** from the pool, as today (matches just shown go to the back), each only if it ends before the tee-up talk.
3. **The last studio block** stretches with cards to end exactly at the tee-up talk; it gets a talk clip only if it's at least `studio.minBlockForTalkSeconds` (90) long. So the time always fills exactly. A card that would leave less than half a card after it runs on to the end instead (Task 175: a 0.7 s card flashed up before the opening), so a block's last card lasts 10–30 s.

**Card order:** cards continue from where the last block stopped rather than restarting (`segments.studioCards`, in order), so short blocks don't keep showing the first card. The spotlight still moves on each rotation.

**A regular gap** is about 118 minutes after the show (kick-offs about 2h41m apart; the match and handshakes take about 26 min, the show 12, the tee-up talk ¾, the pre-match and walk-out 4½). Four re-air units take about 110, so on most days all four still fit; on a long-match day the fourth drops and highlights units fill the time, as today.

**Match share** drops a little (each re-air gains about 1¾ min of non-match time, and the fourth re-air drops more often). Task 95 re-measures it; the test's floor stays 60%.

**Longer gaps** (after the final round, between knockouts, before a new season) use the same blocks. Between the final and the new season, the countdown is a studio card over the scene, as today.

### 16.5 The run-in to a live match

Tom (2026-10-05): the presenters introduce the match, then a transition to the still-empty ground and the pre-match card, then the walk-out. No separate UP NEXT card, so two cards never sit back to back.

| Before kick-off | Part | Over |
|---|---|---|
| 5:15 | **Tee-up talk** (`studio.teeUpSeconds` 45): the teams, their form and table places, a player to watch, the stage (a semifinal, the final, the final round's four matches at once), "kick-off in five minutes" | the desk |
| 4:30 | Transition: the scene lifts off the next home ground, empty, its turf name showing | |
| 4:30 → 1:30 | **Pre-match card** (as now), the fans arriving | the stadium |
| 1:30 → 0:00 | **Walk-out** (as now) | the stadium |

**Built in Task 103:** the ground under the pre-match card is already the next home ground (`programme.groundOf`): its end boards, the home mark (the final too, at the higher seed's ground) and the empty dugouts, so the scene lifts off it; the substitutes come out with the walk-out as before.

- The tee-up talk ends every gap that leads to a live match, including the season's first (after the countdown). No ad break comes right before it.
- **Calendar checks:** the slot now starts `teeUpSeconds` before the pre-match, so `src/config.js` checks that the tee-up, pre-match, walk-out, match with pauses, handshakes and post-match show fit between regular kick-offs and before each knockout.

### 16.5a The channel's opening, once a day

Tom (2026-10-05): a "start of the channel" moment, like a station signing on. **[Default]** once a day, 40 s in all:
- **The ident** (`opening.identSeconds` 10; Tom's pick from `ads.html`, Task 94: **C, the O**), over the light bars, dimmed (the whole screen in Broadcast, the ground in Full): the screen empty (Task 277b, Tom: SCORCHBALL used to sit dim from the start); a ball wrapped in fire (flames streaming off its back as it moves, licking upward when it's still, white-hot to red) comes in from the left, makes one full turn of an oval round where the name will be, carries on over the top to just above the O's place and drops straight down into it as the O (Tom: never sliding across the S and C); only then do SCORCHBALL's letters (the block font at 5×) come in, lighting up outward from it, each row a shade from white-hot to red. A comet and an ignition were also tried.
- **The welcome** (`opening.talkSeconds` 30): the presenters at the desk welcome viewers in: the day, the season's state (round N, the final round tonight, playoff day, the off-season), the day's live matches and the first kick-off, the leader. Then the normal flow carries on.
- **When (Task 95):** right before the tee-up talk of the first live match whose tee-up starts at or after `opening.at` ("06:00", in `live.timeZone`), so it runs ident → welcome (today's matches, the first kick-off) → tee-up → pre-match. With the default calendar that's about 08:36 on most days (the 05:55 tee-up comes just before 06:00). It takes its 40 s from the end of that gap's last studio block, so the calendar doesn't move. (First specced as the first studio block after 06:00; placing it in the run-in keeps it within one slot and makes the welcome lead straight into the day's first match.)
- **Why once a day:** it marks the broadcast day rather than each match, and with about nine live matches a day, more often would make it routine. If it should come round more (a morning and an evening opening, say), `opening.at` can become a list; Tom to judge once it's on screen.
- **The season's first match** also gets it, before its tee-up.

**Built in Task 106:** the ident is `spotArt.js` `drawIdent` (Tom's pick C) over the channel's own light bars, its own picture (`pictureOf` → `ident`) so the diagonal band cuts into it and out to the welcome; a whoosh as the comet comes in and the `ident` sound (a bright chord and a crackle) as it lands (`identLanding`). The welcome is `talk.js`'s `welcome` topic: the day, the season's state (season N starts today, round N, the final round, semifinal day, the final), today's live matches and the first kick-off, the leader; greetings are time-neutral (the season's first opening is just after midnight).

**Built in Task 95** (`programme.js`): each slot's running order (from the end of the handshakes to the next pre-match) is built once as a list of timed pieces and cached by the match it follows; `at(t)` looks the piece up. New segment kinds `talk` (with a `topic`: match, round, interview_tee_up, wrap_up, clip, tee_up, welcome, champions, offseason; and the clip's kind), `ads` (bumper_in, ad, bumper_out) and `ident`; re-airs gain the parts `full_time` and `interview`; highlights segments carry `from`, `clipFrom` and `clipUntil`. Until the visuals arrive the channel shows placeholders: Task 58's desk for every talk segment (the latest-results card is a card again), a plain card for ads and the opening, the full-time card and the player-of-the-match card (marked RECORDED) in re-airs. Settings blocks `studio`, `ads`, `transitions` and `opening`, and `reairs.fullTimeSeconds` and `interviewSeconds`, with their schemas; the calendar checks count the run-in (the tee-up and the opening) and the show's talk.

### 16.6 Over the scene: the bug, the panel and the ticker

- **The scorebug** shows what it shows on today's studio cards (the NEXT countdown to the next live match). A match picture always carries its LIVE, HIGHLIGHTS or REPLAY bug, as now; the bug follows the picture across a transition, switching at its midpoint.
- **The side panel** behaves as now: in the gaps STATS shows the last match to finish; during a highlights unit or a re-air, that match.
- **The ticker** is unchanged, and still leaves out a match's result while it re-airs.

### 16.7 Talking heads

**The desk** (Task 98): Task 58's two presenters (same names, jackets and skins, plus seeded hair and ties) on set B (§16.1), drawn as a new half-length figure, bigger and more detailed than the player sprite. They take turns; the one talking gestures (making a point, pointing at the screens, laughing, both arms up), chosen by the line's kind, and the listener turns to them and nods now and then (Tom approved the gestures, Task 93), and their speech bubble types the line at `captions.quoteCharsPerSecond`, as now. No voices yet.

**Built in Task 98:** `studioSet.js`: the half-length figure (`drawFigure`, from `studio.html`; the interview will use it too), set B's layout as a pure function of its rect (`setLayout`: 3× over the ground in Full, 4× on the whole screen in Broadcast, so the presenters don't look small; the desk centred, the big curved screen, the side screens, the rig's lights), `figurePoses` (pure: the one talking makes their line's gesture for its first `studio.gestureSeconds` (2.6), then talks, mouth moving while the line types, facing their partner, or turned to the screens when pointing; the other faces them and nods every third `studio.nodEverySeconds`, 1.4), and the drawing (`drawSetBack`: the curved screen showing the live light bars, the side screens with the table's top six and the next match, the rig and spotlights; `drawDesk`). The presenters keep their names, jackets and skins, with seeded ties and hair (from their own stream, so no name changed). Task 58's desk drawing is gone from `pitch.js`. The speech bubble is placed in stage px over the speaker (no longer shifted with the ground).

**Lines are data:** the grammar moves from `studio.js` into **`data/studio.json`**, validated in `src/config.js`: for each clip kind, lists of fragments keyed by the fact they need (a big win, a draw, a narrow lead at the top), with `{placeholders}` filled from real facts and a seeded pick. A line whose fact is missing is skipped, never left with a blank. Up to four lines a clip, sharing its length so each has time to type and be read.

**The clips:**

| Clip | Airs | Facts it uses |
|---|---|---|
| **Match talk** | the live post-match show only, once, never again | the result and its size; a scorcher, a late winner or equaliser (the last 15% of normal time, or overtime), a comeback, a hat-trick, burns, a keeper's saves, a shootout; the table effect (top, the top-four cut, a streak) |
| **Round talk** | the final round's show | each match's result in a phrase, who made the playoffs, the Pennant winner |
| **Interview tee-up**, **wrap-up** | the post-match show | the player of the match's name and team; "highlights next" (after the final, "the champions next") |
| **Standings** | gap studio blocks | the leader and their margin, the race for the top-four cut, the bottom |
| **Top scorers** | gap studio blocks | the leader and their goals, who's closing, scorchers |
| **Upcoming** | gap studio blocks | the next two or three fixtures and when |
| **Welcome** | the channel's opening, once a day (§16.5a) | the day, the season's state, today's live matches and the first kick-off, the leader |
| **Tee-up** | the run-in only | the next live match: teams, form (last five), table places, a player to watch (the top scorer of either side, or a recent player of the match), the stage |
| **Champions**, **off-season** | opening those segments | the champion, the final's score, the Pennant winner; notable retirements, the lottery winner, the top pick |

**Built in Task 99** (`talk.js`, pure): `matchFacts` (the result and its size, golden goal or shootout, a comeback from two or more down, the goal that put the winner ahead for good if it came in the last 15% of normal time, a late equaliser, a hat-trick (three or more), three or more scorchers in the match, six or more saves by one player, the burns, and what it did to the table: going top, into the top four, out of it, a streak of three or more, staying top) and `talkScript` for the topics `match` (the result, the story of the game (the first of: comeback, late winner, late equaliser, hat-trick, scorchers, saves, burns, or a plain line), a reaction, then the table effect or, in the knockouts, "into the final" / "the champions"), `round` (an intro, each match's result, the playoff four, the Pennant), `interview_tee_up` and `wrap_up` (the player of the match by full name; then "highlights next", or "the champions next" after the final), `champions` and `offseason` (the champion, the final, first title or how many; a notable retirement, the lottery, the first pick). The lines are in `data/studio.json` (`matchWin` to `offseasonPick`), each kind with its gesture, picked by the segment's seed. **No repeats in a talk** (Fixes, Task 184, Tom: after the final round the desk said who won four times and the lines repeated): each kind's ways are dealt from a seeded shuffle (`sayer`), so none comes twice in a talk until all the others have, and `matchWin` has six ways (three added: take the win, victory for, come out on top) and `matchDraw` five (all square, no winner), so a final round of four never repeats. Their 52 clips (16 per winning line, by team and presenter; 2 per draw line) were made in one paid run, about 1,300 characters. The welcome keeps the stand-in lines until Task 106.

**Built in Task 101** (`talk.js`): `talkScript` for the gap clips (topic `clip`) and the tee-up (`tee_up`). **Standings:** the leader and their margin or a level top, fourth against fifth with the games left, the bottom, a reaction; after the regular season, the final table. **Top scorers:** the leader or a shared lead, the next one down, the most scorchers, a reaction; once the final has kicked off, who finished top. **Upcoming:** the next three fixtures and when (spoken "Mon 21:58"; playoff teams to be decided until known on screen), or when the next season starts. Before a season's first goal, standings and scorers fall back to the next two games. **The tee-up:** the match and its stage (or the final round, all at once, and the featured match), table places and wins in the last five, a player to watch (either side's top scorer this season), kick-off in five minutes. **No spoilers by construction:** gap clips never name a result or a score. Lines `standingsLeader` to `teeUpKickoff` in `data/studio.json`; `onScreen.leadersAt` gives a season's leaders in any stat.

**Rules:**
- **The post-match talk never repeats:** match talk airs only in its live show; re-airs get none.
- **Gap clips rotate** through `studio.clipKinds` (`standings`, `scorers`, `upcoming`) in a seeded order per gap, never the same kind twice in a row; lines vary by a seed from the clip's start, so a kind that comes round again still says something new where the facts allow.
- **No spoilers:** no clip names the result of a match that re-airs later in the same gap or is re-airing now (as the ticker). The table already includes it, which is accepted (§7a).
- **Early in season 1,** with no table to speak of, clips fall back to the upcoming games and the first results.
- **Lines that know where the season stands** (Tom, 2026-10-18: after the final round the desk still asked who'd finish top scorer): `talk.js` `talkStage` says where the season stands as a talk airs, by what has finished on screen: `season` (the regular season running), `settled` (it's over, no semifinal finished yet), `semis` (one finished), `finalSet` (both), `done` (the final finished). A way in `data/studio.json` can name the stages it's never said in (`not`), and the sayer skips it; a kind with no way left says nothing. Kept out after the regular season: the scoring race's ways (scorersLeader's "leading the scoring", "the goals keep coming", "leading the way"; scorersShared's "share the lead"; seven of scorersChasing's eight; six of scorersReact's eight); in the knockouts, the points (matchWin's "Three points for…", reactWin's "Good points for…"); after the first semifinal, playoffsReact's "Everything comes down to one match"; after the final, playoffsReact and scorersPlayoffs. Once the regular season is over the scorers clip says how it finished (scorersFinal or scorersShared, then "And second in the scoring.", the most scorchers, a reaction), with the playoffs' scorer while there's one and the final still to play. Before this, about 33 wrong lines aired a season's end (24 overnight before the semifinals, 7 after the final); none now (`tests/talk.test.js`).

### 16.8 The player-of-the-match interview

**Tom's pick (Task 93): A, the board:** the player in front of a board printed with the ball mark and the channel's orange (pitchside and a tunnel close-up were also tried). It replaces the POTM card for the same 45 s and the same pick (`potm.js`, §7a):
- The player large (the presenters' half-length figure), in their kit, breathing, alone: **no reporter's arm**, the interviewer is out of frame (Tom). **The kit in detail** (Tom: not a gown): short sleeves with cuffs, a collar and a small crest in the second colour, darker side panels, a waistband, then below the waist about a third shorts in the second colour (a stripe down each side) and two thirds bare legs (Tom). **Framed lower** (Task 189, Tom's framing B on `offseason.html`: the legs felt long): the figure stands so 19 rows show below the shirt, the shorts and the legs to just below the knee (`interview.js` `LEG_ROWS`; it was about 24), 19 px lower in Full and 25 in Broadcast. The off-season show's award plinth is framed the same.
- The **stat line** as a lower third ("6 goals (3 scorchers) · 1 assist · 18 tackles"), with the name, position and team.
- The **quote** in a speech bubble, typed out as now: today's canned lines from `data/interviews.json`. Generated quotes wait for voices (Tom, 2026-10-05; backlog).
- **Brands stay in the ads** for now (Tom): the board carries the channel's mark, not an advertiser.
- In re-airs, the same interview with the **RECORDED** mark and when it was played.

**Built in Task 100:** `interview.js`: `interviewLayout` (where the player, the bubble, the badge, the caption bar and RECORDED go; the player at 5× on the whole screen, 4× on the ground), `interviewPose` (the player listens and nods for `studio.interviewQuoteDelaySeconds`, 1.5, then talks while the quote types at `captions.quoteCharsPerSecond`, then rests, nodding now and then), `playerLook` (their sprite's skin, seeded hair from `studio.looks`, the kit they wore that day, clash kits included; a keeper in the keeper's shirt), `recordedMark`, `drawBoard`, `drawInterview`. The caption bar is dark with a thick edge in the team's colour (Tom, 2026-10-05: as in the mock-up; a filled bar reads badly on light kits), with PLAYER OF THE MATCH above it; RECORDED sits top right. The overlay is HTML (`#interview` in `index.html`), following the layout's slide. The card is gone; `potmCard` still gives the pick, the stat line and the quote.

### 16.9 Studio cards

All studio cards sit over the scene and are **restyled to match it** (Tom, 2026-10-05). **Tom's pick (Task 93): Glass:** see-through over the light bars, a light edge, a slanted orange header, the top-right corner cut on the bars' diagonal (Today's opaque card and slanted plates per row were also tried). It applies to today's results, upcoming, scorers and spotlight cards, the champions, the off-season's cards and the countdown. New cards (shown in `studio.html` with real numbers; **Tom keeps all four**, 2026-10-05):
- **Form guide:** each team's last five results.
- **The playoff race:** the top-four cut and the points between places, with the games left.
- **Other leaders:** assists, saves, tackles, burns (blocks wait for It remembers).
- **Head-to-head:** the next live match's teams, their meetings this season and last (the events kept in memory, §3).

`segments.studioCards` lists the cards in rotation order; picked cards are added there. **Until 2026-10-19:** replaced by the decks below.

**The decks by phase** (Tom's note 11, 2026-10-19; `docs/studio-cards.md`, mock-ups on `newcards.html`): `segments.studioDecks` holds a deck per phase, **early** (the regular season's first `earlyDays`, 2), **late** (the rest of it), **finalRound** (the final round to the first semifinal), **playoffs** (from a semifinal to the final) and **afterFinal** (the final to the new season; before the first match ever, the early deck in order). Each studio block deals `cardsPerBlock` cards from its phase's deck (`programme.js` `deal`, `phaseOf`): a shuffle seeded by the gap (`deck|<last match>|<phase>|<pass>`), every card in the pool before any comes round again, a new shuffle never opening with the card just dealt, never one card twice running; a card with nothing to say is skipped (`cards.js` `cardShows`: no rows, no next match for HEAD TO HEAD, THE PLAYOFF RACE only once no team has more than `raceFromGamesLeft` games left, and the new cards' own rules). Only what the block's time fixes counts (what's on screen by then, the seasons before, the sim's events), so every screen deals the same whenever it was loaded (a test). **Fixed slots:** LATEST RESULTS first in the post-match show's block, the promo leading each block before a big match. 'Coming up' still becomes the new-season countdown after the final. Until then two fixed rotations (`studioCards`, `afterFinalCards`), each block carrying on from where the last stopped, with HEAD TO HEAD and THE PLAYOFF RACE giving way to other cards.

**The eleven new cards** (`moreCards.js`, drawn in `cardPanel.js`; Tom's picks over three rounds): **LAST SEASON** (last season's whole final table, the cut dashed, CUP, RUNNERS-UP and PENNANT beside their teams, the table 13 parts to 7, its gaps tighter; AWARDS beside it), **ROLL OF HONOUR** (every season's Cup, runners-up and Pennant, the latest eight; MOST CUPS once a team has two), **BIGGEST WINS** (this season's biggest margins, then the most goals: the round, the teams, the score, the winner lit), **SCORCHERS** (Tom, 2026-10-20, in place of STREAKS, which was too niche: the top two scorcher-scorers with their goals, then every team's scorchers for and against in two columns of four; skipped until the first scorcher), **AWARD RACE** (each award's leader and runner-up by its own rule, each stat beside its player), **RECORDS WATCH** (this season's leader against the season record from the seasons before, when within `recordsWatchShare`, 75%: the chaser on the left, the record and its holder on the right), **ROOKIES** (the best five rookies by goals, assists and stops; rookie keepers with `rookieKeeperStarts`, 3, starts below with SV and SV%), **FINAL TABLE** (the regular season's, once over), **PLAYOFF STATS** (the playoffs' ASSISTS, STOPS, KEEPERS with save % and SCORCHERS), **THE DRAFT** (round 1 just aired: pick, team, name, position, age, projection) and **FAREWELLS** (the notable retirements, at most `farewells`, 6: position, age, seasons, awards).

**Built in Task 102:** every card over the scene (studio cards, the champions, the off-season, the countdown, and the placeholders for ads and the opening) is **Glass** (`#card.glass` in `index.html`; `cardPanel.show(card, t, { glass: true })`); cards over the stadium (pre-match, full time, the re-air intro) keep their look. Results, upcoming and scorers became grids like the new cards (names beside their kits, the score in the middle). The new cards in `cards.js` (`studioCard`): `form` (every team's last five, in table order), `race` (points, games left, and how far clear of fifth or off fourth, a dashed line at the cut; SETTLED once the regular season is over), `leaders` (the top three in assists, saves, tackles and burns, from `onScreen.leadersAt`) and `head_to_head` (the next match's teams, table places and form, and their last four meetings this season and last, finished on screen; in the off-season, with no next match, the programme shows `leaders` instead). **The rotation** is now eight cards: results, the race, upcoming, head-to-head, scorers, leaders, form, the spotlight. A block shows **`segments.cardsPerBlock`** (4) of them, carrying on round the rotation, rather than a whole rotation (which would have doubled every block and squeezed out re-airs); so the day's timings are unchanged (day 2: a match on screen 72.2%, 44 ad breaks). **Card to card:** a soft dissolve, `transitions.cardDissolveMs` (400): the old card is copied on top and fades away (a CSS animation, so a page loaded mid-dissolve just shows the new card). **A swatch in a line of text** (Fixes, Task 182, Tom: a grey line beside the Pennant on the FINAL DAY card): `.kit` was only sized as a flex or grid item, so in a plain line (PENNANT: on FINAL DAY and the champions card, a retirement's team, the defending champions and the opener on the season card) it collapsed to its outline; on cards it's now an inline block.

### 16.10 Transitions

All worked out from segment edges by a pure `transitionAt(programme, t)`: a transition straddles an edge (half before, half after) and the cut happens while the screen is covered, so the programme's times don't change and a load mid-transition draws the same frame as every other screen.

**Tom's picks (Task 92, 2026-10-05):** into and out of the scene, the **glass sweep**: a band of glass edged white and orange crossing on a diagonal, left to right, the same both ways, matching the light bars (a lift upward for out was tried). Between clips and opening a package, the **diagonal band**: a wide band in the channel's colours (an orange and white leading edge, a dark middle, a blue tail) sweeping across on the same diagonal, with no mark or name in it, quicker between clips. Blinds, a pixel dissolve, a flip, a pixel burst and team stripes were also tried.

| Transition | Where | Length **[Default]** |
|---|---|---|
| **Into the scene** | stadium → scene: after the full-time card, after highlights, after a re-air's full-time card | `transitions.sceneMs` 800 |
| **Out of the scene** | scene → stadium: before a highlights unit, a re-air's intro card, the pre-match card (the scene sweeps off the empty ground) | `sceneMs` |
| **Cards under the sweep** (Tom, during the off-season show's Task 187: the UP NEXT card sat in front of the glass lifting off the studio, in both layouts) | a card is HTML over the canvas, so while either sweep is on it's hidden (`cardPanel.cover`), kept as it is: the full-time card before the sweep in, the pre-match card and a re-air's intro card after the sweep out | within `sceneMs` |
| **The crowd's exit** | highlights → scene: the full crowd goes as the wipe passes (in Broadcast the scene then covers the stands too), not at once | within `sceneMs` |
| **Clip sting** | between highlight clips, centred on each cut | `transitions.clipStingMs` 800 (Tom, after Task 98: the 400 first built felt too quick; now as long as the sweep) |
| **Opening sting** | the start of each highlights package: the band covers, holds on a plate with the two teams and when it was played (no score), then uncovers the first clip | `transitions.openStingSeconds` 2, inside the package's time |
| **Desk ↔ interview** | the diagonal band | `transitions.cutMs` 800 (Tom, after Task 98: as long as the sweep) |
| **Card to card** | over the scene: a soft dissolve (Tom, after Task 101: a sweep would be overdoing it), the old card fading off the new | `transitions.cardDissolveMs` 400 |

**Built in Task 97.** The sweeps run on **the stadium side of the edge** rather than straddling it: going into the scene, over the stadium's last `sceneMs`, the glass laying the scene over everything it has passed; coming out, over the stadium's first `sceneMs`, the scene left only where it hasn't reached yet. The stadium is drawn as usual and only the scene (cheap) is drawn a second time, offscreen; the look is the mock-up's. The diagonal band straddles its edge (fully down at the cut), between clips (`clipStingMs` 400) and between the desk and the interview (`cutMs`); into a package it comes down in the last half clip sting before it, holds with the package's name (HIGHLIGHTS, the teams with their kits, when it was played, no score) and lifts `openStingSeconds` after the package starts. Both lean like the light bars ("/"), over the scene's rect (the ground in Full, the whole screen in Broadcast). `transitions.js` gives a sweep's progress `p` and a band's `q` (0.5 fully down) and `title`; `scene.js` draws them (`drawSweep`, `drawBand`, `drawStingTitle`); settings `transitions.glassPx` (26). **Sounds:** a whoosh with each sweep and a swish with each band (`sound.levels.whoosh`, `swish`, 0.3; on the sound board), played as a transition starts, not when loading into one. The glass lays down (or lifts off) what's on the scene side (`drawSweep`'s `picture`): the recorded interview's board and player (Task 100), and set B with the presenters for talk (Tom, after Task 101: the bare light bars, then the set cutting in at the edge, was jarring; it happened after every full-time card and before every pre-match). The bug and HTML cards switch at the edge (the end of a sweep in, the start of a sweep out). The HTML laid over the picture (the match's bubbles and caption, the presenters' bubble, the interview's overlay) is hidden while any transition is on screen (Tom, after Task 102: a MISS! showed over the band into a package). **Never from the walk-out** (fixed after Task 100, Tom): `transitionAt` doesn't look past a kick-off, since on the channel the league hasn't played that match yet and the programme would answer with the scene after it; matches and their ceremonies never get a transition.

### 16.11 Ads

**A proof of concept** (Tom, 2026-10-05): a small fixed set of in-universe pixel ads for invented products, played in the same order every break; a seeded rotation comes later (backlog).
- **A break:** a bumper in ("WE'LL BE RIGHT BACK"), `ads.perBreak` (3) ads of `ads.adSeconds` (9), a bumper out ("BACK TO THE STUDIO"), `ads.bumperSeconds` (1.5) each: 30 s **[Default]**.
- **When:** at the start of a studio block, if the last break ended at least `ads.minGapMinutes` (20) earlier in this slot (the post-match show's block always has one). Never in the run-in, a match, a highlights package or a re-air. About 5 breaks, 2½ minutes, in a regular 2h41m slot.
- **Where:** the whole screen in Broadcast, the ground in Full (the scene hidden); the scorebug as on studio cards.
- **Data:** `data/spots.json`, validated in `src/config.js`: per ad an `id`, `brand`, `product`, `tagline`, `colours`, a `scene` (which drawn ad it is) and a `jingle` (a few notes, built like the anthems). Brands are invented, local to the league's regions, evoke no real brand and aren't named after a team's city.
- **The set (Tom's picks from `ads.html`, Task 94: all as shown):** Caravan Cola (Sunreach; a caravan at sunset, a can rising; "The taste that crossed the desert."), Tideline Sardines (the Gullcoast; a gull mascot dives for a tin and winks; "Fresh off the Gullcoast.", one sentence, Tom), Foundry Fuel (the Kettle; a sponsored stat, the season's scorchers counting up; "Fuel for the hot ball."), Ironvale Boots (the Kettle; a billboard, a boot strikes a ball that heats white to red; "Forged in the Kettle. Made to last."), Velours (Varenne; a bottle pours into a glass; "Terribly refined. Slightly fizzy."), and a channel promo for the playoffs. More brands wait in `ads.html`'s list (Red Dune Sun Cream, Gullwing Ferries, Maison Doré, Steamvale Crisps, Ember & Co.).
- **The bumpers:** WE'LL BE RIGHT BACK and BACK TO THE STUDIO over the light bars, a small fireball crossing, 1.5 s each.
- **Brands only in ads** for now (Tom): not on the pitch boards or the interview board.
