**Built in Task 105:** `data/spots.json` (the six in order, each with its drawn `scene`, end-card `colours` and a `jingle`), `spotArt.js` (the drawing, shared with `ads.html`: each ad a function of the moment into it, the brand and tagline from the data), `spots.js` (pure: `adIndex`, `playoffTimes`, `adFacts`). **Order (Tom, 2026-10-05):** each break carries on through the ads where the last stopped, 1-3, 4-6, 1-3, counted within the slot (`brk` on the ads segments), to revisit when there are many more (now in the slot's own shuffled order, Task 158, below). The bumpers and the promo sit on the channel's own light bars. Foundry Fuel counts up the season's real scorchers; the promo's times come from the calendar.

**Between ads (Task 118, Tom):** a darker pixel dissolve over the new ad's first `ads.dissolveMs` (0.5 s): the old ad, held as it ended, breaks up into near-black cell by cell in an ordered dither, then the new one appears out of it (`drawAdDissolve`). It was a straight cut. The ad still above the ground changes the same way (Task 119, Tom: that's the one he meant), over the new still's first `dissolveMs` of the league's clock.

**The promo's window (Task 111, Tom, 2026-10-05):** the playoffs promo is only in the order from `ads.promoFromShare` (0.5) of the way through the regular season, by kick-offs, until the final kicks off; outside that (the first half of the season, the off-season) `adsFor` leaves it out and each break carries on through the other five. It has two versions (`promoStage`): **the playoffs** (PLAYOFFS, the semifinals' and the final's times: "Semis Sun 14:00 and 17:00", "Finals Sun 20:00"; Task 138, Tom: PLAYOFFS, not THE PLAYOFFS) until the first semifinal kicks off, then **the final** (THE FINAL, its time, and the finalists, better seed first, each after a block of its kit colour, once both semifinals have finished on screen; "Two semifinals. One final." until then). Worked out at each break's start, so every screen shows the same. `ads.html` shows all three.
**The league's own ads (Task 146; the slate in `docs/backlog.md`, Ad break):** two more in `data/spots.json` before the promo, so the rotation is eight (seven outside the promo's window). **Tickets**: a turnstile clicks, a fan climbs the steps out of the dark into a full stand that rises cheering; the end card: TICKETS, Louder in person., ON SALE NOW (Tom) and the next live match (`adFacts` `tickets`: its time and, once known, its teams). **Season tickets**: a wall calendar flips through a team's seven home matches while a scarf in its colours grows a stripe for each, then lies along a seat's back; the end card: SEASON TICKETS, Your seat. All season., SEASON N ON SALE NOW, N this season until the first semifinal kicks off, then the next (Tom; `calendar.at(t).phase`). The team is seeded by the moment (each break and each still its own, the same on every screen). Each has a still (TICKETS ON SALE NOW and the next match; the scarf, SEASON TICKETS and the season on sale) and a jingle (brass; bells). The league's ads share an end card (`leagueCard`) with room for a call to action, as the slate's later ones (the Almanac, the League Shop) will need. Season tickets' brand was light blue, too close to some teams' colours (Tom): it now shares Tickets' gold on dark red (Fixes, Task 182; `data/spots.json` `colours`, the still and the end card).

**Clear of the scorebug in Broadcast (Groundwork, Tom 2026-10-07):** an ad fills the whole screen there and the scorebug stays over its top left (about 186–210 × 20 px), so four end cards that put the brand in the top 20 px were cut off: Orrery Bank (the name lower in its frieze, y 14 → 24), Velours, Gullwing Ferries and Maison Doré (their plates, names and taglines 10–12 px lower). Every ad's top strip was checked at 2, 5 and 8 s; the rest keep their text clear. The Orrery Bank still's name went back to the usual size (Tom: at twice the size it drew the eye), with a soft marble shadow so the gold still reads.

**The order, shuffled (Task 158, Ad break; Tom, 2026-10-06):** with the slate's 18 ads a fixed order would keep the same ads together, and since the count restarts in every gap (about 5 breaks, 15 ads) the last few would hardly ever air. So each gap between live matches shuffles all the ads into its own order (`adOrder`: seeded by the league's seed and the gap, the id of the match it follows, `gap` on the ads segments; the same on every screen), and its breaks take them `ads.perBreak` at a time from the start, carrying on from break to break (1-3, 4-6, …, wrapping round in a long gap). Every ad plays once before any repeats, and the next gap has a fresh order. The promo is shuffled in with the rest and dropped outside its window, so the others keep their places if the window opens or closes in the gap. **The stills** take a fresh shuffle each time round (`stillOrder`, pass N of the league's clock, `ads.stillMinutes` × the number of ads long), each ad once a pass; a pass never opens with the ad the last ended on (the first two swap). `npm run rundown` names each ad; `ads.html` shows a sample gap's break and the first five breaks of six gaps.

**Batch 1 of the slate (Task 159, Ad break; Tom's picks):** four brands' ads, one from each region, in `spotBrands.js` with stills, so 12 ads in the rotation (11 outside the promo's window). **Red Dune Sun Cream** (Sunreach, a mascot; organ): a red lizard dances on the hot sand under a swelling, pulsing sun; a tube flies in and squeezes a blob of cream onto its back; it cools to green, a striped parasol opens and it lounges in sunglasses. **Gullwing Ferries** (the Gullcoast, a journey; brass): the ferry chugs along a dotted route from the lighthouse quay to the harbour houses, smoke puffing, gulls trailing, its wake only on open water (Tom); as it docks the tide sweeps in over the flats behind it. **Maison Doré** (Varenne, a product shot; bells): a croissant on a silver plate under a spotlight, lifted by a white-gloved hand, shatters into slow-motion golden flakes that settle on the plate and the velvet. **Steamvale Crisps** (the Kettle, the crowd; brass, Tom: a rising triplet then two chords above it, unlike Maison Doré's): in a pool of light in the stand a fan's packet puffs and pops, CRUNCH! shakes the steaming valley walls and echoes off both sides, and the whole row in front turns round. The block font gained É and ! for them. `ads.html` shows the four at the top, each with a button to play it from the start with its jingle.

**Batch 2 (Task 160, Ad break; Tom's picks):** three more brands' ads, so 15 in the rotation (14 outside the promo's window). Their jingles are **slower and lower** than the others (Tom: a different series of sounds), 80–88 bpm and about an octave down. **Sandglass Watches** (Sunreach, a product shot; bells, a tick-tock then a low chord): in a desert market, awnings fluttering, sand pours through an hourglass on a rug-covered table against the stalls' shade; in a swirl of sand it becomes a gold watch on a leather strap, its second hand ticking. **Brinestone Oilskins** (the Gullcoast, a little story; brass, a low shanty phrase): a fisher in yellow oilskins on a pitching boat in a storm (rain, lightning); two waves crash over them in clouds of foam; the storm breaks, light falls through, and they're bone dry, grinning, a thumb up; the end card in a band over the sea, clear of the fisher. **Orrery Bank** (Varenne, a billboard; organ, slow low chords): a marble hall; a gold coin rolls along a ledge past the columns and drops into an open vault, whose round door, an orrery turning on it, swings shut with a clunk that shakes dust off the ledge; the name is cut into the frieze in gold. Its still draws the name at twice the size with a 1 px shadow, like the frieze (Tom: at 1× the shadow swamped the gold).

**Batch 3, the league's own (Task 161, Ad break; Tom's picks):** the slate is complete: 18 ads in the rotation (17 outside the promo's window), three brands from each region and six of the league's own. In `spotLeague.js`, on the league's end card (its name at 3×, or 2× when that won't fit: The Scorchball Almanac in Full). Their jingles are low and quick, running up and down like fire (Tom), 138–150 bpm, nothing above middle C, each ending on a short low chord. **Ember & Co.** (the official match ball, a sponsored stat; brass): over the light bars, the ball on a pedestal heats from Cool (white) to Warm to Hot, the ident's fire licking round it from Warm, while COOL, WARM, HOT light in turn and this season's burns count up; "The official match ball". **The Scorchball Almanac** (the record book, a trail for It remembers; organ): a red book (ALMANAC, SEASON N) thumps onto a desk in a dark study, dust puffing, opens, its pages riffling past as blurs of tiny results, and settles on this season's goals; **Buy yours today**. **The League Shop** (replica shirts, a showcase; low bells): a carousel rack spins through all eight teams' kits (each shirt its first colour with collar, cuffs and hem in its second, its abbreviation on the chest) and slows to a stop on one, which is lifted off and held up with its team's name; **Find a shop at your local stadium**. The facts (`adFacts`, worked out at each break's start, the same on every screen): `season`; `burns` and `goals`, every team's in every finished match this season, the playoffs' too (goals from the scores, not shootouts); `shop`, the eight kits and the one the rack stops on: the next live match's home team (Tom), or a team seeded by the break's moment while that isn't known (a playoff waiting on the round before, the off-season). **Its still** shows the home team of the match on screen instead, live, re-air or highlights (`shopWith`, applied as the still is drawn, so it follows the picture). Found on the way: `upcomingAt` named the final's teams in the semifinals' order, though the sim has hosted it at the better seed's ground since 2026-10-05; now by seed, so Coming up, the side panel, the ticker, the desk and Tickets name the final's home team right.

### 16.12 Sound

All synthesized, as §14.2, tuned on the sound board:
- **A soft studio bed** under the scene **[Default: quiet, or none; decide by ear]**.
- **A whoosh** with each transition into or out of the scene; a short **clip sting** between highlight clips; the existing channel stings stay (the show opening, each package, a re-air's intro).
- **Speech blips** while a presenter's line types (soft, like an old game's dialogue) **[decide by ear]**.
- **Ad jingles** from each ad's notes, and a bumper sound. **Built in Task 105:** each ad's jingle (`data/spots.json`: notes on one of the anthems' instruments) plays as its brand comes up (`sound.levels.jingle` 0.4); the bumpers have no sound of their own yet.
- New levels in `sound.levels` (`studioBed`, `whoosh`, `clipSting`, `blip`, `jingle`, `bumper`), with their schema.
- No crowd over the scene or in the interview; highlights bring their crowd back as now.

### 16.13 The crowd

As §15.7, with two changes:
- **Re-airs drain like live matches:** through the 30 s handshakes the losers' end to 60% and the other to 85%, then through the new full-time card to nobody.
- **Highlights → scene:** the crowd leaves under the wipe (§16.10), not at once.

### 16.14 Dev tools

- **`npm run rundown [-- --day 2 --season 1 --seed S --all]`** (`src/cli/rundown.js`, Task 95): a day's running order, every part with its start time and length (each ad break, each talk clip's kind; studio cards in a row folded into one line unless `--all`), then the day's totals: live matches, re-airs, highlights, talk, cards, ads, the opening, the share with a match on screen, and the number of ad breaks. Under a second. Built on `programme.pieceAt(t)`.
  - **Day 2 of season 1, the live seed (Task 95):** a match on screen 72.2% of the day (live 14.8%, re-airs 57.4%), 44 ad breaks (22 minutes, 1.5%), talk 3.2%, studio cards 8.3%, highlights 4.1%; the opening at 08:36.
- **Dev-panel jumps (Task 95):** Next match talk, Next ad break, Next tee-up, Next re-air interview, Next highlights unit (Task 104), Next opening, each searching the running order up to 30 hours ahead on a scratch league; they replace Next studio desk.
- **Mock-up pages:** `scene.html` (scenes and transitions, Task 92), `studio.html` (the desk, the interview, the cards, Task 93), `ads.html` (Task 94). The help panel lists them.

### 16.15 Code and settings

- **Changed:** `programme.js` (the show's parts, the blocks, ad breaks, the tee-up talk), `studio.js` (clip kinds and their facts, the grammar read from data), `cards.js` (restyled and new cards), `crowd.js` (re-airs drain), `main.js`, `dev.js` and `overlays/devPanel.js` (jumps), `soundRecipes.js` and `audio.js` (the new sounds), `src/config.js` (schemas and calendar checks).
- **New pure modules [Default names]:** `scene.js` (the scene's animation as a function of time), `transitions.js` (Task 95: `pictureOf`, `transitionBetween`, `transitionAt`, worked out from segment edges and the highlights segment's `from`, `clipFrom` and `clipUntil`), `interview.js` (the set's layout), `spots.js` (a break's running order and the ad on screen). Drawing in `pitch.js` or a new `sceneDraw.js`.
- **New data:** `data/studio.json` (talk lines), `data/spots.json` (the ads).
- **New and changed settings [Default]:**

```json
"studio":      { "matchTalkSeconds": 40, "roundTalkSeconds": 70, "interviewTeeUpSeconds": 10, "wrapUpSeconds": 20,
                 "clipSeconds": 45, "teeUpSeconds": 45, "championTalkSeconds": 30, "offseasonTalkSeconds": 30,
                 "minBlockForTalkSeconds": 90, "clipKinds": ["standings", "scorers", "upcoming"] },
"reairs":      { "...": "as now", "fullTimeSeconds": 30, "interviewSeconds": 45 },
"ads":         { "perBreak": 3, "adSeconds": 9, "bumperSeconds": 1.5, "minGapMinutes": 20 },
"transitions": { "sceneMs": 800, "clipStingMs": 400, "openStingSeconds": 2, "cutMs": 300 },
"scene":       { "...": "the light bars' values, Task 96" },
"opening":     { "at": "06:00", "identSeconds": 10, "talkSeconds": 30 }
```

  Plus `crowd.fill` for the re-air's full-time card, `segments.studioCards` gaining the picked new cards, and the new `sound.levels`, each with its schema in `src/config.js`.
- **Tests:** every gap still fills exactly and ends with the tee-up talk at the pre-match; the post-match show still totals 12 minutes; ad breaks keep their spacing and never touch a match, a package, a re-air or the run-in; match talk appears once per live match and never in a re-air; gap clips never repeat a kind back to back and never name a result re-airing in the same gap; transitions cover every cut and never move a segment's time; the same seed gives the same running order; match share stays above 60%; the calendar checks include the tee-up talk.

## 17. [Polish] Full mode's empty areas, pop-up panes, playoff turf, livelier players

### 17.1 Full mode's empty areas (Tasks 112–113)

In Full, two strips of the stage sit empty: **above the ground** (y 0–23, the scorebug over its left end; Broadcast's top stand goes there) and **between the ground and the ticker** (y 236–256; Broadcast's turf and bottom stand). Tom (2026-10-05): four areas to fill, the two strips for **match pictures** (live, re-airs, highlights, and the cards over the ground) and for **everything else** (the scene, the desk, the interview, ads, the ident), the top strip left to the match picker in the final round. A dimmed crowd may suit matches but not the studio; the bottom strip could simply be the turf with the home team's name (lower thirds briefly over it are fine).

**The mock-ups (Task 112):** `empty.html` holds the real channel in a frame, with every option drawn by `strips.js` behind `?strips=groundTop,groundBottom,sceneTop,sceneBottom` (switched on the fly, no reload) and jumps to one moment of each kind. Options: above the ground in matches, a crowd (full or dimmed), the stand's roof with floodlights and the home name, or LED ad boards with the in-universe brands; above the ticker in matches, the turf with the home name, the turf then a row of crowd, or ad boards; everything else, the scene carried into the strips, a quiet band in the scene's blues, the band with the wordmark (top), or the empty ground dimmed. Whatever is picked fades out as Broadcast's stands slide in.

**Tom's picks (2026-10-05):**
1. **Match pictures, above the ground: one ad** filling the area right of the scorebug, in that ad's own look: a still, a snapshot of its animated ad (Caravan Cola's dunes at sunset and the can, Tideline's gull and tin, Foundry Fuel's stat, Ironvale's bulbs and boot, Velours' bottle), with its brand. It changes every few minutes (about 5; not too fast), from the league's clock so every screen shows the same ad. Mock-ups of the stills first (Tom: "a snapshot of the larger animated ad"). Tom on the idea: "ads are such a good idea".
2. **Match pictures, above the ticker: the turf with the home name**, looking exactly like Broadcast's: in the mock-up a dark band was left between the bottom boards and the strip (the turf was clipped at y 236, while Broadcast's starts at 231, just under the boards), cutting into the letters, and another below it. The turf runs from the boards down to the ticker, unbroken, with the name centred.
3. **Everything else, above the ground: the quiet band.**
4. **Everything else, above the ticker: the quiet band.**

**Built in Task 113:** `strips.js` draws both strips in Full, under the slide to Broadcast (fading out as the stands come in), switching with the picture (`pictureOf`: the stadium, or anything else).
- **Match pictures, above the ground: one ad still** (`spotArt.js` `AD_STILLS`, one per ad and both versions of the promo; `ads.html` shows them). A still is a snapshot of its animated ad: the scenery stretches to fill from 4 px past the scorebug (measured each frame, since its width changes with its label: LIVE, HIGHLIGHTS, REPLAY, NEXT) to the side panel, the brand pinned left and the product right; at least 180 px wide. It's 20 px tall, ending level with the heat meter under the scorebug (Tom: at 23 px it ran past the scorebug's bottom edge). `spots.js` `stillAt` picks it: the next every `ads.stillMinutes` (5) of the league's clock, in `data/spots.json`'s order, the promo only in its window (Task 111), its facts (the scorchers count, the finalists) from when it went up. Repeats of a brand just seen in a break are fine (Tom). Left out in the final round, where the match picker is.
- **The scorebug narrowed for the still (Tom, after Groundwork):** in the playoffs the SEMI / FINAL label, REPLAY or HIGHLIGHTS and a shootout score made the scorebug end as far as x 252, past where a 180 px still can start (210), so the still slid under it and lost its brand's left end (re-airs and highlights of the semis and final; live playoffs by a few px). Now its cells have 3 px padding a side (was 4), the clock is no wider than "1H 10:42" (31 px, was 40) and the score has no minimum; in a shootout the score stays the match's (2-2) and the clock shows the tally, **SO 4-3** (was "2-2 (4-3)" and SHOOTOUT), on the FT bug too. Measured: the widest, HIGHLIGHTS FINAL, ends at 207; only a team in double figures there (very rare) still runs past.
- **Task 138 (Tom):** the playoffs promo's still names the final on the brand's row and the semis below, each after a label in a tiny 3×5 font (FINALS, SEMIS in the brand's gold; not F and SF), the day by the brand when all three are on one day ("PLAYOFFS SUN  FINALS 20:00" / "SEMIS 14:00 17:00"). **Since 2026-10-11 (Tom):** both on the bottom row, the brand and the day alone on top ("PLAYOFFS SUN" / "SEMIS 14:00 17:00  FINALS 20:00"), placed by the sharp text's own width (`sharpText.js` `sharpBlockWidth`: the block letters' 6 px a character left a gap). Velours' still: the name small (1×) in purple on the sky with no plate, where its large name drew the eye. The animated promo: "Only on" and SCORCHBALL centred as a pair with a 12 px gap.
- **Match pictures, above the ticker: the turf with the home name**, Broadcast's (`drawGroundTurf`), from just under the bottom boards (y 231) to the ticker (y 256) unbroken, the name centred in it. Lower thirds go over it as they come.
- **Everything else: the quiet band** in both strips, the scene's blues brightest by the picture with a thin edge.
- **Task 118 (Tom):** the turf stops 3 px short of the ticker, so a dark line runs above the ticker like the one under the scorebug and the ad still.
- **Task 139 (Tom): the transitions cover the bottom strip in Full.** The glass sweeps into and out of the scene, the bands between highlight clips and opening a package, and the cuts all run from the top of the ground down to the ticker (`main.js` `drawTransition`; in Broadcast the scene's rect is already the whole screen). The sweep lays down the scene side as it really looks: its picture over the ground and the strip's quiet band below (`strips.js` `drawBottomBand`), so nothing jumps when it ends. The sting's title stays centred on the ground.
- **Also (Tom, at the mock-ups):** Broadcast's top stand had its three rows laid out from the top down, leaving a bare 5 px step by the dugouts, like a missing row; its rows now sit against the ground (`rowDrop` in `stands.js`), the spare pixels at its top under the scorebug. The block font gained digits and a colon for the stills. The mock-ups (`empty.html`, `?strips=`) are gone.

### 17.2 Pop-up panes and the TV guide (Tasks 114–115)

Tom (2026-10-05): every button below the side panel opens a **pane over everything else**, like a help pop-up on a website, with its information and choices; the TV guide first, then Rulebook, Record book, Audience and About. **Full only**: none of these buttons are needed in Broadcast. Where it lands: **over the side panel if it can** (Tom's first choice), knowing the column is narrow and a long pane (the Rulebook) would scroll a lot; wider is fine if that reads better.

**The mock-ups (Task 114):** `panes.html` puts a mock pane into the real channel, with the choices: where it lands (over the side panel, 86 px; the side panel widened over the pitch, 226 px; centred over the pitch, 270 × 200), its look (dark plate with an amber title bar, or Glass like the studio cards), what's in it (the TV guide from the real programme, or sample Rulebook text), how much the guide lists (everything; programmes, leaving out studio blocks under 5 minutes; matches only), and dimming the channel behind it. The guide groups the programme the way a TV guide would: a live match from its pre-match card to the end of its post-match show is one line, a re-air from its intro to its interview one, a highlights package one, the talk, cards and ads between them one studio block; now marked, with how far in.

**Tom's picks (2026-10-05):**
- **Over the side panel**, for every button in the row (tabs and panel, 86 px, the buttons still showing below). If a pane truly runs into trouble there, plan B is the side panel widened over the pitch (226 px).
- **Dark plate**, like the side panel and the scorebug, with an amber title bar.
- **The TV guide lists everything** (every studio block, however short). Nice to have: a frozen row of toggles under the TV GUIDE title, ALL / MATCHES, to switch to matches only.
- **All caps** in the guide (STUDIO, OPENING, not Studio): lower case among the channel's capitals looks off (Tom). Whether the Rulebook's long text is in capitals too is for when it's written.
- **The guide keeps its scroll:** it opens at now and stays where the viewer scrolls (the mock-up jumped back to now every few seconds as it redrew).

**Built in Task 115:**
- **The pane** (`overlays/pane.js`, `#pane`): over the side column (x 394, y 0, 86 × 238: the tabs and the panel; the buttons stay showing below), the dark plate, an amber title bar with the button's name, a small line beside it (the guide's day) and ✕; an optional row fixed under the title; the rest scrolls. One pane at a time; ✕, the button again or Esc closes it; switching to Broadcast closes it (and Broadcast hides it). Each button gives a title and its contents, so Rulebook, Record book, Audience and About drop in later. A button is lit (amber) while its pane is open.
- **The TV guide** (`guide.js`, pure; `overlays/guidePane.js`; the first button, or **V**): today, midnight to midnight, grouped as in the mock-ups: a live match from its pre-match card to the end of its post-match show is one line, listed at its **kick-off** (as the ticker and GAMES list it) and in red; the final round's matches at once are one FINAL ROUND line; a re-air (REP, blue) from its intro to its interview; a highlights package (HL); the talk, cards and ads between them one STUDIO line; OPENING, CHAMPIONS, OFF-SEASON. All in capitals, kits before the abbreviations, SF and F for the playoffs. The past is dimmed; now is marked with a thin bar for how far in, moving along once a second in place. **ALL / MATCHES** toggles sit under the title (on this screen only, ALL each load). It opens at now, then stays where the viewer scrolls.
- **Nothing given away:** no scores; the final's teams appear only once both semifinals have finished on screen (THE FINAL until then). The semifinals' teams come from the table the day before, so they always show.
- **Worked out off the drawing thread:** the rest of the day needs matches the channel hasn't reached, so `guideWorker.js` (a module worker) builds the day from a league of its own (`buildDay`: about a quarter of a second, late in a season too) when the guide is first opened each day, and the channel never stutters. LOADING THE DAY… shows meanwhile. The worker gets the channel's own config (with any `?hl=`) and seed, so it lists exactly what the channel will show.
- **Tests** (`tests/guide.test.js`): the grouping; a real day running midnight to midnight with no gaps and every kick-off listed; the same day built twice; the day's edges; now at an entry's edges; the filter; the final round listed once; the final's teams kept back.

**The Rulebook and About (Tasks 163–164).** Mock-ups in Task 163 (`rulebook.html`: tabs, a contents list or one page; capitals or mixed case; 86 or 226 px). **Tom's picks (2026-10-07):** tabs, mixed case, 86 px for both (at full size the 86 px column reads well; each tab is one to three screens).
- **The words** (`data/rulebook.json`, `src/channel/rulebook.js`, pure): the Rulebook is the league's own, in four sections (GAME, HEAT, GOALS, SEASON), each a few rules numbered by section (2.1, 2.2…), the book's one-line intro above the first. Its numbers are `{facts}` worked out from `data/rules.json` and `config/settings.json` (match length, heat thresholds in seconds, squad and subs, teams, regions, the final round's matches, playoff places, overtime, shootout kicks; `{fact|word}` writes a small one as a word), so a rule change rewrites the text. About is out of universe, in Tom's words (modelled on PNN's): what the channel is (upfront about the AI), that it's fictional, that a simulation decides everything, built with Claude, the ElevenLabs voices and the synthesized sound, the font's licence, a version line and a placeholder © line (dimmer, `about.small`). The Oxford comma throughout (Tom).
- **Names** (Tom, 2026-10-07): the table winner takes that season's **Pennant** (it was the Shield; the code keeps `shieldId`), the champion lifts the **Scorch Cup**.
- **Tooltips** (Task 277b, Tom's pick B from `tooltips.html`; `overlays/tooltip.js`): every `title` in the stage becomes the channel's own tooltip, amber like the pane heads, in stage pixels: a moment after hovering, then straight from one to the next; above the bottom row's buttons, under the top's; a key in brackets at the end shown as a dark key cap (Schedule V, Rulebook R, CHRONICLE C, AUDIENCE A, INFO I, sound M, the layout B, the side tabs S, T, G, W). The layout switch reads "Full layout: the stats, the panes and the ticker (B)" in Broadcast (was "Back to the Full layout"). The title moves to `data-tip` as it's set (and to `aria-label`, its name for screen readers), so the browser's own tooltip never shows.
- **Built in Task 164** (`overlays/bookPanes.js`): the Rulebook and About buttons are lit (the second and fifth; Record book and Audience stay dimmed). Each opens its pane like the TV guide: the button again, ✕ or Esc closes it, another button swaps it, Broadcast closes it; the button is lit while its pane is open. The Rulebook's tabs sit in the row under the title (the guide's ALL / MATCHES look); the tab chosen is kept while the channel is open, and each opens at its top. No keys (B, M and V are taken; the buttons are enough). Task 277b (Tom) gave them keys after all: R the Rulebook, C the CHRONICLE, A AUDIENCE, I INFO.
- **Dev page:** `rulebook.html` shows the channel with its own buttons opening the panes, and how many screens each page fills (at 86 px: The game 2.9, Heat 1.4, Goals 1.0, The season 2.4, About 2.2).
- **Tests** (`tests/rulebook.test.js`): every blank filled, the facts from the rules, a changed rule changing the text, numbers as words, short tab labels.
- **About's GUIDE tab** (Task 172, Tom): About has two tabs, ABOUT and GUIDE, like the Record book's mock-up; the guide explains the screen (the scorebug's labels as chips in their colours, its clock, the heat meter; each button beside its own icon, the speaker and layout switch copied from the scorebug; the side panel's tabs and the ticker; the final round's multi-view) and then the channel's rhythm (a day: a live match about every {liveEvery}, worked out from the calendar, its hour, and what fills the gaps, studio and ads included; a week: the season, the final round's day, the playoffs, the off-season). Words in `data/rulebook.json` `guide`, numbers from the settings; no clock times (local times are on the backlog). About 6 screens at 86 px. **The TV guide is now SCHEDULE** (its pane title and button), since GUIDE is About's tab; and the About pane's title is INFO (Tom: its tabs are ABOUT and GUIDE, its button an "i").

**Tomorrow at the foot** (Tom, 2026-10-15, after Fan Coins: you couldn't see the next day until midnight; his pick A on `tomorrow.html`): a TOMORROW divider, then tomorrow's programmes up to and including its first live match, a touch dimmer, built in the worker as the day is (`overlays/guidePane.js` tomorrow). On the live channel tomorrow needs its day key (the daily job publishes it in the late afternoon; a day's request tops the keys up first), so until it's in there's no TOMORROW at all (Tom, 2026-10-16: rather than a stopgap that changes part-way through the day), asking again each minute. At midnight tomorrow becomes the day at once (already built).

### 17.3 Playoff and final turf (Tasks 116–117)

The playoffs should look like the playoffs (Tom's Between matches watch). Semifinals and the final are at the better seed's ground (Tom, 2026-10-05), so it's that team's pitch, marked for the occasion.

**The mock-ups (Task 116):** `playoffs.html` draws the channel's own empty home ground (pitch, end boards, home mark, benches, the turf; in Broadcast the stands too) for any home and away team, with the looks from `playoffArt.js`: the stage's words (SEMIFINAL, THE FINAL) in gold in place of the home name, or small gold tags at both ends of the turf with the name kept; the name in gold; a trophy in the centre circle over the home colour; the white side boards in gold. Semifinals: A (words), B (name with tags), C (gold name with tags). The final: D (words), E (words and trophy), F (words, trophy, gold boards), G (name with tags, trophy), H (all of it). Tom's variant: the stage's words on the pitch either side of the centre circle, running along the stripes (the dark cut on the left, the light cut on the right), each facing its end so the crowd behind that goal reads it: I (gold, the block font), J (gold, a tiny 3×5 alphabet), K (white pitch paint); for the final L, M, N the same with the trophy.

**Tom's picks (2026-10-05):**
- **Semifinals: K**, the words on the pitch in **white pitch paint**, either side of the centre circle, facing the ends; the words **SEMIFINALS** (Tom; not SEMIFINAL). The turf keeps the home name.
- **The final: N plus gold boards**: **FINALS** in white pitch paint the same way, the **trophy** in the centre circle, and the white side boards in **gold** ("a nice touch for the championship; at least we get some gold in there"). The turf keeps the home name.

**Built in Task 117:** `playoffArt.js` (`playoffMarks` picks the marks by stage: none in the regular season; SEMIFINALS; FINALS with gold boards (the trophy dropped after a watch, Tom, Task 118: only the gold boards and FINALS); `drawPlayoffMarks` draws them). In `main.js` they go on right after the home team's centre circle, before the gate, the benches and the players, wherever the ground of a playoff match is drawn: the pre-match card, the walk-out, the match, the handshakes, re-airs, highlights, and the goal replay inset. Each picture takes its own match's stage, so a semifinal re-aired in the off-season still says SEMIFINALS. The words are the block font in white pitch paint (0.8 opaque), centred in the stripes either side of the circle's (3, dark; 6, light). The unpicked looks and `playoffs.html` are gone.

### 17.4 Livelier players (Tasks 119–120)

Tom's Between matches watch: players a touch more alive, visual only (the sim and where players are untouched). Today every player steps every 180 ms whatever their speed and even standing still, the sprite always faces the camera, a held ball always sits on the side of the goal the team attacks, nobody moves a leg to kick, and keepers slide across in their standing pose.

**The mock-ups (Task 119):** `players.html` loops scripted studies on patches of turf, today's sprite beside the new poses from `poses.js` (pixel rows like pitch.js's sprite): 1. the stride following speed (a walk, a jog, a sprint: four steps, a step every 300 ms at a walk easing to 110 ms at a sprint, the free foot kicked further back and the arms pumping when sprinting, both feet down standing still); 2. the ball switching feet only once the carrier has headed the other way for about a fifth of a second (`ballSide`), so wiggles never flick it; 3. a kick for passes (150 ms) and shots (250 ms, arms out), pointing the way the ball goes; 4. a keeper's dive, two ways: A lying full length along the goal line (the dive pose turned), B a leap flung diagonally toward the ball, arms first; then on one knee and up; 5. every pose still, at eight times the size.

Tom (2026-10-05): "These look great." The kicks could be more pronounced (few pixels to work with): the shot now leans the head and shoulders a pixel into it and swings the leg a pixel further. And an effect like the ball's for speed: **turf kicked up** (`drawTurfKick`: a few flecks in the turf's greens and a speck of earth, thrown up and falling within a third of a second, seeded) behind sprinting strides, at a turn, when the ball changes feet, at a kick (more for a shot), and where a diving keeper lands.

**Tom's picks (2026-10-05):** all of it: the stride following speed, the ball switching feet, the kicks (the shot leaning in), the turf kicked up, and **dive B**, the leap flung diagonally toward the ball, arms first (A, lying along the line, read as a 4 px sliver).

**Built in Task 120:** in every match picture (live, re-airs, highlights, the goal replay inset), `drawMatch` gives each player a pose and draws the turf kicked up under them; walk-outs, handshakes and the benches keep their own movements. Settings in a new `poses` block.
- **Speeds and directions** come from where everyone was at the last few 100 ms marks of the match clock (`poses.sampleMs`, cached per match), so every screen, re-air and replay agrees. The run (`stepPhase`) follows the speed between the last two marks.
- **The ball's foot** (`footSide`): the side the carrier has been heading over the last 200 ms, or for a slow drift 600 ms (`footMs`), faster than `footSpeed`; otherwise toward the goal they attack. A ball just kicked is nudged on from the kicking foot over 120 ms, so it no longer jumps to the kicker's middle as it leaves.
- **Kicks and dives** come from the motion's own plan (`motion.actionAt`): a pass as it sets off (`kickMs`), a shot as it's struck (`shotKickMs`, leaning in), and for a shot saved or scored the defending keeper's leap toward the ball's side from `diveLeadMs` before it lands, then `kneeMs` on one knee. Not the dive every time (Tom, 2026-10-20, `saves.html`; `settings.poses.saves`): each shot's seed picks the keeper's move, a held save a standing catch (60%) or the dive, a parried one a standing parry, high or low (40%), or the dive, a goal the dive or the keeper beaten barely moving (40%); a standing save lasts as the leap does, with no knee, and a save's side is seeded (it always dived down before). The motion's positions are untouched: `npm run pitchmap` gives exactly the same numbers.
- **Turf kicked up** (`drawTurfKick`): behind each sprinting stride, at a sharp turn at speed, when the ball changes feet, at a kick (more for a shot) and where a keeper lands.
- `players.html` stays as a dev page (it draws from `poses.js`); dive A is gone. Tests: `tests/poses.test.js` (the run's pace, the foot, every shot a kick by its shooter, every save a dive by that keeper, passes kicking).

### 17.5 Team crests (Groundwork, Tasks 165–166)

Each team's crest is drawn in pixels from `data/teams.json`'s `crest` (a shape: shield, circle, diamond or banner; an icon: gull, crow, crown, grapes, anvil, mountain, hawk, palm) in its two colours, by `src/channel/crests.js`: a 15 × 17 grid, the icon 9 × 9, drawn at a whole-number scale (2 on the cards, 1 in the interview).
- **Styles** (mock-ups in Task 165, `crests.html`): flat (a rim and the icon in the second colour), shaded (a dark outline, a bevel, the icon's shadow), banded (shaded, with the team's three letters on a band, scale 2 and up). **Tom's picks (2026-10-07):** banded on the cards, shaded where it's small, flat on the shirt. The banners are square at the bottom with a small notch (a swallowtail cut the letters off); the crow was redrawn (a rounder head, the body and legs centred).
- **Where they go** (Tom: not the scorebug, the tables or the TV guide, too small): the **pre-match card**, banded, beside each side's name, place and form (above them pushed the line-ups off the card); the **team spotlight**, banded, in place of the kit swatch; the **interview**, shaded at the left of the lower third and flat on the shirt's left chest in place of the small mark (`drawFigure`'s `badge`, `drawInterview` passes it), whatever kit the player wore; the **centre circle** of every home pitch (version D of four: today's filled home colour with the icon on it in the second colour, three times its grid, centred by its pixels, the halfway line and the spot drawn back over it, as centre-ice lines cross a hockey logo; `drawHomeMark`'s `mark`, `centreIcon`), the playoffs' too, the replay inset and the dev pages that draw a home ground. Later: the small crest in the record books (It remembers).
- **Tests** (`tests/crests.test.js`): every team's icon exists and is 9 × 9; a crest stays within its size in every style and scale; the centre icon is in the second colour, centred and inside the circle.

## 18. [Sound] Voices, and the rest of the Sound milestone

Voices are in scope from Sound (vision §12): made **on our side**, never by a speech model in the viewer's page. Viewers only receive small clips.

### 18.1 The voice audition (Task 122)

- **How clips are made:** `npm run voices` (`src/cli/voices.js`) speaks each line with a free, open-source speech model on Tom's machine into `output/voices/`. The sound board's Voices section (`voiceAudition.js`) plays them side by side: even loudness, "on air" treatments (TV band; plus pixel grit), the crowd underneath.
- **Auditioned:** 8 Kokoro voices (`kokoro-js`, a dev dependency, Apache-2.0), 5 Piper voices (its standalone Windows program in `C:\Users\Tom\tools\piper\`, outside the repo; only voices free to use), and the browser's own speech.
- **Tom's verdict (2026-10-06):**
  - **Commentary: Kokoro's George** (`bm_george`, UK male), "best by far": the only voice that follows the exclamation marks, rising with excitement where the others fall away like the end of a normal sentence.
  - **Second commentary voice: Heart** (`af_heart`, US female).
  - **Daniel** (`bm_daniel`, UK male) would be good too, but trails a sound after the word ("GOAL-uh"); worth a fix.
  - **Lewis** (`bm_lewis`, UK male) suits the talking heads (the desk) more than commentary.
  - **UK voices fit the broadcast** best; other accents (Scandinavian, say) are welcome.
  - **Casting for later** (Tom, 2026-10-06): the desk: **Daniel** and **Lewis** (Task 126: Daniel at ×1.15 with no end mark was decent as a commentator, but the desk needs two good voices); the player-of-the-match interview: **Fenrir** (`am_fenrir`, sounds younger), perhaps **Emma** (`bf_emma`; a player's gender may not matter).
  - **Ruled out:** Piper ("fairly weak overall") and the browser's speech ("definitely unusable"). Piper stays installed for now; it may still matter for a cheap server later (Milestone 6), if its quality holds up for long desk lines.

### 18.2 Commentary in matches (Tasks 123–125)

- **What:** short exclamations at a match's key moments, in **live matches and re-airs only**, never highlights (Tom, 2026-10-06).
- **One commentator a match** (Tom): picked by the seed from `data/commentary.json` → `commentators` (George, Heart for now; more if good voices turn up), never two in one match. The same match always has the same commentator, so a re-air sounds like the live match.
- **The lines and moments** live in `data/commentary.json`:
  - Goals, scorchers (a goal while Hot), saves, blocks, off the post, wide, burns and breakaways. Tackles and steals are never called: too many.
  - **A library of 5–6 lines a moment** (Tom, Task 124, to break up the monotony): excited ones end in "!", calmer ones in "." (Kokoro says "Wide." with less force), e.g. a miss: "Wide.", "Just wide.", "Off target.", "And it misses.", "Not this time.", "Pulled wide.". Each moment deals its lines in a seeded shuffle, so none repeats until the rest have had a turn, and never twice running.
- **Pacing** (`settings.commentary`): a call comes `delayMs` (250) after its moment and at least `minGapSeconds` (3) after the last. **A moment on its own is always called** (Tom, Task 124: with chances on everything, too much went by uncalled); only moments within `minGapSeconds` of another are thinned by their chance (saves 0.6, blocks 0.4, wides 0.25, burns 0.5; goals, scorchers, posts and breakaways 1). A goal always comes, and drops a call just before it (whose line goes back on its deck). That's about 55 calls in a 23-minute match, one every 25 s or so: about 15 saves, 15 wides, 10 blocks, 5 goals, 3 burns, 3 breakaways, 2 scorchers, 1 post. (Task 123 had chances on every moment: about 30.)
- **The breakaway one-two** (Tom, Task 125): when the player who broke away shoots, the outcome (goal, save, post, wide) is always called straight after "Breakaway!", at least `followGapSeconds` (1.3) after it. If they pass it off, there's no follow-up. A moment on its own that would land too soon after a delayed call is moved on a little in the same way, never dropped. About 59 calls a match.
- **Never two at once** (Task 179): clips run 1.4–2.5 s (3.2 s for the longest whistle), so a call placed only `followGapSeconds` after the one before (a one-two, a whistle) used to step on its end, by up to 0.7 s (about 3 times a match). The audio knows each clip's length, so it settles it as it plays (`audio.playClip` `queue`): a match's calls **wait** for the voice before to finish plus `commentary.breathMs` (200); passes in a row and holding the ball are **left out** instead (late, they'd be wrong). Passes in a row and holding the ball also keep `minGapSeconds` from each other (they were counted apart, and could start 0.3 s apart: Tom heard two lines at once in season 1's final).
- **Goals are louder** (Tom, Task 125: home goals were too soft under the cheering): goals and scorchers play at `goalLevels` (home 0.6, away 0.5, since the home end cheers louder) instead of the commentary level.
- **Speeds** (Task 125): each commentator has a `speed` in `data/commentary.json` (Kokoro's pace is 1). Tom found George's delivery slow next to Heart's ("Heart is probably better because her lines are a bit faster"), so George is made at 1.15.
- **The mix:** while the voice speaks, every other sound dips to `duckLevel` (0.75) of itself, fading over `duckFadeMs`. Loudness is `sound.levels.commentary` (0.45; it was 0.8 with a 0.6 dip, "a bit jarring" over the murmur and the ball, Tom). After a jump or turning sound on, no call replays (only calls from the moments since the last frame play).
- **The clips:** `npm run voices -- --assets` (about 30 s) has each commentator say each line with Kokoro, trims the silence either side, evens the loudness out and saves 16-bit WAV clips in `assets/voices/<commentator>/<line>.wav` (43 lines, about 2.1 MB a commentator). The channel fetches only the match's own commentator's clips, as the match first plays with sound on. The sound board's Commentary section plays every clip of every moment, at the commentary level and ducking whatever else plays. Adding a commentator or a line is data: an entry in `data/commentary.json`, then rerun the command.
- **Code:** `commentary.js` (pure: the commentator, the calls); `audio.js` (`loadClips`, `playClip`, the duck: every synthesized sound goes through a bus the voice dips); `main.js` (`matchSounds`). Tests: `tests/commentary.test.js`.

### 18.3 ElevenLabs (Task 127)

- **Tom's verdict on the free plan (2026-10-06):** "These are solid"; ElevenLabs is the way to go, a level above Kokoro. **v3 with delivery tags** (`[excited]`) is the real winner; the standard model is "a bit dry". Nearly every voice said "Charged down" robotically: rewordings tried (Task 127), else the line is replaced.
- **Casting (Tom):**
  - **Commentary:** ElevenLabs' **George** and **Daniel** (British male), and ideally a third British male voice (the free plan only has these two; more in the shared Voice Library with a paid month). This replaces the Kokoro commentators once the paid month's clips are made.
  - **The desk:** **Alice** (British female) as one of the two presenters, which needs one presenter drawn as a woman (a visual change, `studio.js` and the scene's sprites); the other presenter to find.
  - **Players:** **Charlie** and **Liam**; eventually at least one unique player voice per team (8 teams).
- **Terms:** clips made during a paid month keep a commercial licence for good; free-plan clips always need attribution, so they stay in `output/` for auditioning. The channel's clips are made in a paid month, in batches (everything new at once).
- **Fact-built lines** (the desk talk, interviews; Tom, 2026-10-06, vision §12): for the launch, everything voiced is made ahead, with no ongoing subscription: no player names (amended in Talk that remembers, Task 244: surnames voiced alone, or inside whole sentences; never joined onto a line's end, except opening "What a scorcher!"), team names fine (pronunciation checked), numbers only where a small set can be made ahead, and only the kinds of lines that can be assembled from such pieces get a voice. Live voices come much later.
- **Only paid-month clips on air** (Tom): even voices already picked on the free plan are made again in the paid month. ElevenLabs' sound effects are left alone for now (backlog).

### 18.4 The levels pane (Task 129)

- **The speaker beside the scorebug** now opens a strip of three on/off switches, **Sound, Crowd, Commentary** (Tom: no sliders), in the scorebug's row: over the ad strip in Full, over the crowd beside the scorebug in Broadcast. **M** still turns sound on and off at once. It closes with the speaker, a click elsewhere, or Escape; the speaker is lit while it's open.
- **Sound** is the old on/off (its click starts the audio, as browsers need). **Crowd** silences the crowd's sounds (roar, groan, ooh, applause, jeer, murmur, chant); **Commentary** the commentator. While sound is off, the other two show dimmed but still switch.
- **Remembered** in the viewer's own browser (`localStorage`, `scorchball.levels`): Crowd and Commentary. Sound itself can't be (browsers need a click first), so every load still starts silent. If storage is blocked, both start on.
- **The look:** each switch has a lamp, a small square lit in the accent when on, and its label struck through when off (Tom picked this, look B, from three mock-ups in the channel itself: text switches with ON / OFF tags, lamps, pixel icons). `levels.html` stays as a dev page showing the pane open in both layouts.
- **Code:** `overlays/levels.js`, `#levels` in `index.html`, `audio.setCrowd` / `audio.setCommentary`; `buttons.js` (the speaker opens the pane).
- **Task 256 (before It responds; Tom's pick A on `levels.html`):** four switches, **Sound, Crowd, Commentary, Studio**, for viewers who want the commentators off but the desk and the interview on (Commentary had become Voices, all three, in Task 135). **Commentary** silences the commentators' calls (`audio.setCommentary`), **Studio** the desk and the interview (`audio.setStudio`, the clips played as kind `speech`). Both remembered (`scorchball.levels`: `crowd`, `commentary`, `studio`); a Voices choice saved before carries to both. B, one Voices switch going round ALL, STUDIO ONLY, OFF, wasn't taken. **No strike-through** on a switch that's off (Tom: the dark lamp and the dimmer label do enough).

### 18.5 More commentary: the whistles and passes in a row (Task 130)

- **The whistles** (Tom): the first kick-off ("And we're underway!"), the second half's ("The second half is underway."), half-time and full time, each with a few lines, plus **level-score forms** ("All square at the break.", "It ends all square."; never after a shootout, which always has a winner). Overtime's kick-offs aren't called. They come `afterWhistleMs` (1 s) after the whistle and are always called: moved on a little if another call is just before, never dropped. Full time is called after the match's playback has ended, so the handshakes (or the trophy lift after the final) play the calls after its whistle (Task 208: until then full time was never heard).
- **Passes in a row** (Tom): "Nice pass.", "Lovely ball movement!", "Look at this!" and so on, when one team strings `passRun` passes together (4; Tom found 6 too rare). The sim doesn't log passes (at most two advances a possession), so they're **counted as the picture draws them**: each pass is a kick (sounds.js). Working that out for a whole match up front takes about 1 s, too long to stall the channel for, so it's counted frame by frame as passes happen (`passCall`). A run is counted only once it's watched from its start (after a jump, or when the match is first heard, counting waits for the next break), and it isn't called when another call is within `minGapSeconds`. At 4, about 27 passing calls a match (at 6 it was about 6; about 11 expected at 5). Lines dealt in a shuffle like the others.
- **Totals:** about 58 precomputed calls a match plus about 27 passing calls, about one every 16 s (Node, the channel's own per-frame path over whole matches).
- **Tom's edits:** the drafts are kept except "Let's get this started!", now "There's the first touch and we're off!"; and "Charged down." (robotic in every ElevenLabs voice, rewordings too) is replaced by "Nice block there!". Kokoro's George and Heart speak all 73 lines until the paid month's batch.

### 18.5a More moments (Touch-ups, Tasks 148–149)
Tom's lines (`docs/tasks.md`, Task 148) as new moments in `data/commentary.json` and `commentary.js`:
- **Tackle and steal**: from the sim's tackles (a pass cut out is a steal), each dealt from its own lines and the "either" ones (Won it back! and the rest). A ball won within `commentary.turnoverGapSeconds` (3) of the last one isn't called (Tom). They're frequent (about 20 tackles and 44 steals a match), so only a share is called at all: `rate`, a new per-moment field applied before the usual crowding (`chance`). Tom (after a watch): every tackle on its own, half the steals (about 43 a match; about 110 event calls a match in all).
- **Holding the ball**: counted as the picture draws it, like passes in a row (`holdCall`, frame by frame, with `motion.holderAt`): when one outfield player (never a keeper) has had it `commentary.holdSeconds` (4.5) without a pass, one call, `rate` 0.4 (Tom, Task 151 and after a watch: of about 330 spells on the ball a match, about 54 last 4.5 s; so about 21 calls a match). Not the spell under way after a jump, and never while play is stopped (Task 179: a substitution, a foul, a free shot being set, a goal, a break between periods): a stop ends the spell, so the count starts again at the restart. A keeper's restart isn't called either (keepers never are; the player they give it to starts a new spell). **"That ball's getting warm."** (a line with `warm`) only once the heat meter is Warm or Hot (`rules.heat.warmAt`, 50; Tom, 2026-10-18: it was said at low heat); while the meter is cool the next line in the deck is said instead.
- **Foul**: every foul, `rate` 1, `chance` 0.7.
- **The playoffs** (the Announcer's alone: lines with `voices`): the semifinal's and the final's full time in place of the usual lines, the golden goal in place of the goal call (Task 147), and golden-goal overtime starting (Tom: until now only its whistle).
- **Off air until their clips are made** (Task 150): each new moment has `onAir: false`, so it isn't called and takes up no room; meanwhile the golden goal and the playoffs' full times stand in with today's lines. `linesFor` gives the lines a commentator needs clips for (on-air moments, their voices), for the clips the channel loads and the clips test.
- **On air (Task 150, 2026-10-06):** the batch made 80 clips on ElevenLabs (17 each for George, Daniel and Zane, 29 for the Announcer), about 1,895 credits, and every new moment went on air once Tom had heard them on the sound board (`sounds.html` now shows each commentator's own lines, the new ones marked until they're on air). Daniel's "Read it perfectly" is spoken from "Red it perfectly!" (he said "reed"), and his "Possession changes hands" was retaken until the pause after "possession" was gone (0.52 s and 0.57 s in two takes; none in the one kept). **The voices tool** (`npm.cmd run voices -- --assets [--count] --paid`) now makes only the lines each commentator speaks and only clips not made yet (`--count` first for the cost): before, a run remade every line, overwriting approved clips (a run stopped partway; George's were restored from git). To remake a clip, delete its file and run it again.
- With everything on air: about 93 calls a match (today about 63), about one every 13 s.

ADEYEMI 88'").

The config rejects a said line with any other blank. 89 kinds (the "how" fragments folded into the win lines); Tom's edit: "And it was won late."

- **The batch:** 919 clips a presenter (103 fixed, 102 team-naming × 8 teams), about 33,500 characters each. **Tom (2026-10-06): both presenters say everything** (about 67,000 characters; top-ups or a month on the bigger plan, settled when the batch is made), rather than fewer team wordings or fixed roles at the desk.
