### 18.10 The desk and the interview speak (Task 135)

- **The clips:** `npm run voices -- --desk --interviews --paid` (vision §12: only in a paid month) makes, on ElevenLabs v3, each desk wording in both presenters' voices (once per team where it names one, spoken as `sayAs` or `spokenTeam`; excited lines tagged), into `assets/voices/desk/<presenter>/<kind>-<way>[-<team>].wav`, and each quote in each region's two voices with its result's mood ([cheerful] after a win, [disappointed] after a loss), into `assets/voices/interview/<region>-<man|woman>/<role>-<result>-<index>.wav`. Clips already made are skipped (a run resumes; new lines cost only themselves); `--count` totals what's left: **2,062 clips, 76,946 characters** (desk 62,634, interview 14,312).
- **Playback** (`speech.js`, `main.js`): a desk line's clip plays in the speaking presenter's voice as the line starts, once, and not when tuning in part-way (`SPEECH_START_MS`, 1 s; a clip still loading is tried again each frame until then, and the current and next lines are fetched every frame while sound is on, so turning sound on during a talk works: Tom found the first lines silent when sound came on after the talk began); the interview's quote plays in the player's voice (their region's, a man's or a woman's by first name) as it starts typing. The typing keeps pace with the voice (`typingSpeed`: the line's length over the clip's). The other sounds dip under it, as under the commentator. Loudness `sound.levels.speech` (0.6). Until a clip exists, its line stays silent.
- **The levels pane:** Commentary is now **Voices** (Tom): it silences every voice, the commentator, the desk and the interview; a choice saved under the old name carries over.

### 18.11 Compressed clips (Task 136)

- **Every voice clip is MP3** (Tom, 2026-10-06; it was the launch item in the backlog): mono at 40 kbps, which keeps speech clear. MP3 because every browser decodes it (OGG/Opus isn't certain on Safari). All 2,354 clips (commentary, desk, interview) went from 229 MB as 16-bit WAV to 25 MB.
- **How:** `ffmpeg-static`, a dev dependency (a copy of ffmpeg inside the project; nothing installed on the machine; `src/cli/mp3.js`). The clip maker writes MP3 directly; `npm run voices -- --compress` turns any WAV left in `assets/voices/` into MP3 and removes it. Try-out clips in `output/` stay WAV (never shipped).
- The channel's clip paths (`clipUrl`, `deskClipUrl`, `interviewClipUrl`) end in `.mp3`; the dev server serves `audio/mpeg`.
- The commentary's earlier WAVs are still in git's history (about 26 MB); the desk and interview clips were only ever committed as MP3.

### 18.12 The studio jingle; the bed dropped (Task 137)

- **The studio bed is gone** (Tom: it sounded "church-organ-ish" even re-voiced; a jingle instead): its recipe, chords, `bedEverySeconds`, its level and its row on the sound board. Speech blips are dropped too: every desk line and quote has a real voice.
- **A trumpet jingle as a STUDIO or OPENING programme begins** (but never over the desk or a bumper, Task 180, below) (Tom: only at the start of the TV guide's STUDIO and OPENING entries, not at every return to the scene; it first played at every sweep into the scene and at every Back to the studio bumper): `guideKind` (guide.js) says which entry a segment belongs to, shared with the guide so they always agree. The opening's comes as the welcome starts, after the ident (which has its own sound). Not when tuning in part-way. About 53 a day (52 studio entries and the opening on day 2; 56 on day 5): roughly every 27 minutes. On the anthems' brass, through the ads' jingle player (level `sound.levels.jingle`); the tune is data, `data/studio.json` `jingle`.
- **Three variants** on the sound board (the jingle row: Studio A, B, C): A a fanfare (da-da-da-DAAA), B a call and answer, C a quick run up to a held note. A is on the channel until Tom picks. **Tom's tune** (2026-10-06): simple two-note chords, two quick ones and a pause, stepping down four times, then C's run up, also in chords, to a held C major. Two readings: **D1** steps down in thirds (G/E, E/C, C/A, A/F), **D2** a tone at a time in parallel thirds (G/E, F/D, Eb/C, Db/Bb); Tom picked **D1**. The jingle player now takes chords (`with`: notes sounded together, each quieter so a chord isn't louder) and rests (`rest: true`), for the ads' jingles too.
- **The bumper sound** (Groundwork, Task 168): two short phrases on the studio jingle's brass at its 150 bpm, one as each bumper card opens: **in** with WE'LL BE RIGHT BACK (the jingle's double chord stabs stepping down, landing on a held chord), **out** with BACK TO THE STUDIO (the jingle's run up to its top chord), so a break is called and answered about 30 s apart, the ads' jingles between. Three takes went on the sound board (A the jingle's own opening and run, B three chords down and up, C a two-chord sign-off); **Tom picked A**, the closest to the jingle. Each ends inside `settings.ads.bumperSeconds` (a test checks); not when tuning in part-way, not above `?speed=2`; the tunes are data, `data/studio.json` `bumpers.in` and `bumpers.out`; the sound board's jingle row plays both.
- **Never over the desk** (Fixes, Task 180; Tom heard it over the champions' and the off-season talk): the jingle runs 3.7 s and the desk's first line came 0.8 s into a talk, so wherever an entry opened with talk the two overlapped by about 3 s: the welcome, the tee-up straight after it, the champions' talk (after the final's show) and the off-season's (after the champions' cards). Now one rule, `studioJingle` (guide.js), shared with a test: of the talks, only those in `JINGLE_TALKS` (talk.js: the welcome, the champions, the off-season) open with it, and their first line waits for it (`timeLines`' `quietMs`, the jingle's length from its tune; the lines still fit with 3 s or more to spare); the tee-up after the welcome has none (the opening's played 30 s before). **Nor with a bumper:** a studio entry that opens with an ad break used to play the jingle on top of the bumper-in phrase (Task 168), running on into the first ad's jingle; the bumper's own sound now stands alone there. So the jingle plays about half as often as before: as the welcome, the champions and the off-season begin, and as the cards after a highlights package start (no voice there).
- **Later** (backlog): variations of each cue; music from Suno perhaps, if a track fits the synthesized look.

### 18.13 The desk's pauses (the off-season show, Task 187)

- **Why** (Tom: long silences between the desk's lines, and in the interview): each talk's fixed length was shared evenly between its lines, so a 2 s clip sat in a 7–8 s slot; the interview held 45 s for a quote of about 4 s. Measured over all 2,130 clips: the desk's run 0.7–5.0 s (median 2.1), the quotes 2.2–5.6 s (median 3.6).
- **The clips' lengths** are a file, `assets/voices/lengths.json` (`config.voiceLengths`, loaded and checked with the config): each desk and interview clip's seconds by its path under `assets/voices/` (`deskClipKey`, `interviewClipKey`). `npm run voices -- --lengths` writes it, reading each MP3's frames (`src/cli/mp3Length.js`, no dependency; within about 25 ms of ffmpeg); a `--desk`/`--interviews` batch rewrites it itself. A line without a clip is timed by its text at `captions.quoteCharsPerSecond`.
- **Each line runs for its clip** (`timeLines`, `lineTiming` in studio.js): the first `studio.lineLeadSeconds` (0.8) in (after the jingle, for the talks it opens), held `lineHoldSeconds` (0.4) after the clip, `lineGapSeconds` (0.4) before the next, the talk ending `talkTailSeconds` (1.2) after its last line. The bubble types at the clip's pace from the list, with or without sound.
- **The graphic under the desk outlasts its line** (Tom, after listening: gone with the voice, it was too quick to read unless you knew to look): it stays up until the next line with a graphic, or the talk's end, under whoever said it (the line's `showUntil`), and for at least `studio.showMinSeconds` (3), longer ones at `showCharsPerSecond` (12). So a line with a graphic waits until the one before has been up that long (usually a fraction of a second; a line takes about 2 s plus the 0.8 s beat), a line without one never waits, and the talk runs until its last graphic has been read. 71 of the desk's 93 kinds of line carry a graphic. Day 2's talk: 13:02 → 15:03; talks 5–16 s.
- **The programme sizes each talk to its lines** (`sizedTalk` in talk.js): the talk lengths in settings (and `opening.talkSeconds`) are now the most a talk can run; it works the script out when it builds the slot and puts it on the segment (`script`), so the channel shows exactly the lines it was sized for. The run-in's talks (the welcome and the tee-up) are sized back from the pre-match, their words drawn from where they used to start (`wordsAt`). The interview runs the nod (`interviewQuoteDelaySeconds`), the quote's clip and `interviewHoldSeconds` (4) to read the caption bar, live and in re-airs.
- **Where the time goes:** the post-match show keeps its 12 minutes (its block's cards fill); in the gap, the blocks are shorter, so more fits before the run-in; before a live match, the cards run up to a later tee-up. Day 2 (`npm run rundown`): talk 48:00 → 13:02, cards 2h04 → 2h37, highlights 52 min → 1h20; a talk now averages 5–15 s (the match talk about 12, a clip about 12, the tee-up about 14), the interview about 9.
- **Speed:** the programme works out every talk's lines as it builds a slot; a day's rundown still takes under half a second.

### 18.14 The interview remembers (Talk that remembers, Task 245)

- **Two sentences when there's a fact** (Tom): when the facts module (`src/chronicle/facts.js`, simulation.md §12c) has something about the player of the match or their team that fits the result, the interview is a **reaction** (`interviews.reactions`, four a result) then **the fact** (`interviews.facts`, by kind or kind.variant: a milestone by its stat, an old club let go or left, a late decider by what it decided, a likely last match won as champion, a final's rematch won in revenge), each its own clip in the player's regional voice. Otherwise one of the 28 quotes, as before (Tom: kept, already voiced).
- **Which fact:** the heaviest with a sentence for the result; a player's own kinds (a first goal, a milestone, a record, back from injury, an old club, a likely last match, one more season, a late decider or scorcher they scored, a haul of goals) only by that player, the rest (runs, droughts, the final's rematch, finals and Cups, a comeback, momentum) by anyone on the team. Picked by `interviewTalk` (`src/channel/interviewTalk.js`), seeded by the match. Clip keys: `reaction-<result>-<i>`, `fact-<kind>[-<variant>]-<i>`.
- **The words** (Tom's rules): first person, gender-neutral (no he, she, his, her; no "lads"), no numbers, no team names, so each sentence is one clip a voice; each way lists the results it fits (an old club only after a win; a likely last match after any). The test holds them to it.
- **How often** (`interviews.html`, 20 seasons of the live seed): about half of interviews speak to a fact. The most common: a haul of goals (four or more; three come in most matches, so a hat-trick is no story here; six ways), a comeback, a run against one club ended, a win run, a first win in a while, a late winner; the history-making ones a few times a season between them.
- **The clips:** 91 sentences, 728 clips over the eight voices, about 38,000 characters (about 16,500 credits), made in the milestone's batch (Task 248). Played as two clips back to back in Task 247.
- **On air** (Task 247a): the interview card carries its parts (`potmCard`: `parts`, each `{ text, key }`, and `fact`); `quoteSchedule` (interview.js) lays them out, each as long as its clip (lengths.json, or the usual typing speed until it's made), `studio.interviewPartGapSeconds` (0.35) between; the bubble types each in turn (the player pausing between, mouth still), each clip played as its part starts; the programme sizes the interview by the parts. Until the batch, the new sentences type without a voice, as any line whose clip isn't made.
- **The fact strip** (Task 247a, Tom's look A of three in `factstrip.html`): the fact on screen, a gold strip hanging from the bottom of the name and stat line (`#interview-fact`, inside `#interview-lower`, so it stays anchored in Full and Broadcast), the team's colour on its edge, from the moment the fact sentence starts. Its words are `interviews.strip`, by the same key as the sentences, filled as the desk's graphics are (`fillOf`, deskFacts.js), in capitals; names and numbers are fine ("BEAT GULLS, WHO LET THEM GO · 2 SEASONS THERE", "LAST SEASON'S FINAL · ROYALE BEATEN AGAIN"). `potmCard`'s `factStrip`.
- **Dev page:** `interviews.html` (`interviewsSheet.js`): a fast-forwarded league's interviews (with a fact, all, or by fact), every sentence with how often it's heard. `?seasons=`, `?seed=`.

### 18.15 The desk remembers (Talk that remembers, Task 246a)

- **After a match** (the match talk, `talk.js`): after the result, the story, the reaction and the table (or the knockout line), the heaviest fact the desk can say from `chronicle.facts.deskFrom` (5) in weight: last season's final met again (or avenged), a first win over a club in a while, a first win in a while, a final reached for the first time or after a wait or again, the Cup for the first time or after a wait or again or a dynasty, a milestone, a record, back from injury and decisive, beating an old club (let go, or left), a likely last match, one more season, a late scorcher or momentum that decided it (`deskAfter`, `src/channel/deskFacts.js`). After about a third of matches (20 seasons of the live seed); the rest say nothing more. Lines `factRematch` to `factMomentumDecider` in `data/studio.json`.
- **Before a match** (the tee-up): after the matchup, last season's final met again, one side's run over the other, or a player facing an old club for the first time since leaving (`deskBefore`; `teeUpRematch`, `teeUpRunOver`, `teeUpOldClub`): about 1 tee-up in 10.
- **Said and shown** (as §18.7): the said line is fixed or names one team; the graphic carries the specifics (the player, the numbers, the opponent: "C. PENHALLOW · 100TH GOAL", "GULLS · FIRST WIN OVER ROYALE IN 6 MEETINGS").
- **Player names** (Task 244): a way with `{surname}` says a player's surname inside the sentence, one clip per surname in the pools (280) per presenter, `desk/<presenter>/<kind>-<way>-<surname>`. Dealt only with `studio.playerNames` true (on since the batch, Task 248) and only when the line has a player, from their own deck: **always** for the rare lines (a record, a likely last match, beating the club that let them go, facing an old club), and a kind's `namedShare` of the time for the frequent ones (Tom: mostly named, with some variety: 0.67 for a milestone and for `interviewTeeUpNamed`, which takes `interviewTeeUp`'s place). Six named ways (Tom): 3,360 clips, about 50,000 credits. `npm run voices -- --desk --count` counts them apart.
- **The facts on the channel** (`FactsLog`, `src/chronicle/factsLog.js`): the live league feeds a chronicle of its own as the log is written (not as it airs) and works out each match's facts the moment it's folded in, topping up a final-round match's likely last matches at the seeding; so the talk after a match, built while the match is still airing, says the same on every screen whenever it loads. The aired chronicle (`live.chronicle`) is untouched.
- **Dev page:** `desklines.html` (`deskLinesSheet.js`): every line kind with how often it's said a season, its ways (those naming a surname marked), and the lines as said and shown, with names on or off.

### 18.15a No line twice running (Task 249, Tom)

- **Why** (Tom, watching): the same commentary clip could play twice running when the same kind of event did, most of all turnovers: tackles and steals share three lines ("won it back", "turned over", "changes hands") but deal from decks of their own, so a tackle then a steal could draw the same one.
- **Now** (`commentaryCalls`' deal): a call never says the line of the call just before it, whatever its moment: the next card that's neither that line nor the moment's own last one instead, the skipped card back at the bottom (or, at a round's last card, a fresh round that starts with neither). Lines still come round evenly.

### 18.15b The opening's comet, smooth, and its fire (Task 249, Tom)

- **The path** (`identPath`, spotArt.js; Tom: it made odd turns): in from below the left end on a curve bending up into the oval's leftmost point going its way, at its pace (it came in flat and turned a corner, slow then fast); the same full turn and arc over the top; then, in place of the straight drop, a hook that carries on along the oval and curls down into the gap, easing as it lands, the ball above the letters until it's over the gap (searched: the gentlest such curl, about 10 px at its tightest; the oval's own ends 19). The same timing, so the chord and the letters lighting are where they were.
- **The fire** (sound `fire`, `settings.sound.levels.fire`): from the comet's entry to its landing, after the whoosh and before the chord, following it: short overlapping pieces, one every 80 ms of its course (`identTrack`), each panned with the ball (layers can now pan), louder and brighter across the front of the name and with its speed, fading as the chord comes. Three styles (`settings.sound.identFire`; **Tom picked torch**, level 0.85, fairly loud as nothing else plays then): crackle (crackles and pops over a low rumble), torch (a roaring flame swelling with speed), comet (a fizzing hiss and sparks); on the sound board, and in place with `?fire=` on the channel.

### 18.16 More ways for the lines heard most (Talk that remembers, Task 246b)

- **Measured** (`npm run talk`, `src/channel/talkCount.js`: the programme over a season as it airs, every desk line counted by kind): a season is 7 days, and the gap's clips dominate (the upcoming fixtures about 28 lines a day, each scorers and standings line about 15). With 2–3 ways each, a way came round up to 9 times a day.
- **68 new ways** for the 21 kinds heard most, so each way is heard about twice a day or less (`upcomingThen` 3 → 14 ways, `upcomingIntro` 3 → 9, each scorers line 3 → 8, ...). All name no team (the graphic carries the fixture or the player), so each is one clip per presenter: 136 clips, about 1,500 credits.
- **Dev page:** `desklines.html`'s last section, every kind with how often it's said a day, its ways (the new ones in gold) and how often each is heard.

### 18.17 The commentators name the scorer (Talk that remembers, Task 247b)

- **Who and how often:** George and the Announcer only (Tom: half the cost; Daniel and Zane call matches without names), `commentary.names`: on a goal or a scorcher their call names the scorer `share` (0.5) of the time, by a roll of its own (`commentary|name|<match>|<seq>`), so every other call is dealt exactly as before.
- **The lines** (Task 244: names alone or inside whole lines, never joined onto a line's end): on a goal, the surname alone as the call, in "Goal!"'s place ("Bonnefoy!"), "{surname} scores!" or "Goal for {surname}!"; on a save (Tom), "What a save from {surname}!" naming the keeper; on a scorcher, the surname alone followed with no gap by the commentator's own "What a scorcher!" (the one join that worked: the call's `then`, played with `audio.playClip`'s `joined`, no breath).
- **Clips:** `assets/voices/<commentator>/names/<line>-<surname>.mp3`, one per surname in the pools (280) per commentator and line; `npm run voices -- --names [--count] --paid`: 2,240 clips (with the save line), about 25,100 credits. On since Task 248 (`names.onAir` true); a match loads only its own scorers' clips.

### 18.18 How soon each line comes round again (Task 257, before It responds)

Tom: the opening's desk lines felt repetitive. **`npm run repeats`** (`src/channel/repeats.js`, about 2 s) plays a season of the programme and counts every line the commentators, the desk and the interview say, grouped (a desk kind, a commentary moment, an interview reaction, fact or quote), ranked by how soon the same line is heard again (hours between on average, the soonest, and for the commentary how often each line is heard in a match). Not counted: the passes-in-a-row and holding calls (worked out live from the picture) and the off-season night. **Found** (season 2): the commentators repeat most by far, a tackle line about 4.8 times a match (6 lines, about 29 tackle calls), steals 3.0, wides 2.4, saves 2.3, blocks 2.0; the opening the same four lines in the same order daily, 2–3 ways each, so the same sentence every 2–3 days and sometimes on consecutive days (the desk deals its ways without repeats within a talk, but each talk shuffles afresh); most desk kinds about every 12 h; the interview fine. **Done (Tom's picks):**
- **27 commentary lines** (tackle +8, steal +6, wide +4, save +5, block +4; Tom swapped "CHARGED DOWN!", which read poorly in most voices, for "COULDN'T GET IT THROUGH!"), each `onAir: false` until its clips are made (Task 258): a line off air is never dealt (`commentary.js` `dealt`) nor its clip loaded; `--all` lines (the voices tool) include it.
- **The opening's shape varies** on an ordinary round day: after the hello, one of four (round · today · leader, today · leader, round · today, leader · today; `talk.js` `WELCOME_SHAPES`), dealt in a seeded order a season, a day each. The special days (the season's start, the final round, the semis, the final) say their news as before.
- **The opening deals a day at a time:** `sayer`'s `setTurn`: a talk with a place in a series picks each kind's first way from the series' seeded order by its turn (the season's opener 0, then each day's from its day), so a kind of N ways comes back only after N openings. Soonest repeat now about 3 days (it was 21 h).
- **More ways:** welcomeHello +3, welcomeRound +2, welcomeToday +2, welcomeLeader +2 (team lines, a clip per team), standingsLevel +3 ("It's tight at the top", every 7 h).
- **Voiced in Task 258** (2026-10-11): 194 clips, about 2,500 credits; the commentary lines switched on (each tackle line 4.8 hearings a match to 2.0, steal 3.0 to 1.5, wide 2.4 to 1.4, save 2.3 to 1.4, block 2.0 to 1.1).

## 19. [Off-season] The off-season show

The off-season show (Tom, 2026-10-07; `docs/tasks.md`, tasks from 187): the night after the final as a show of its own. Its mock-ups and Tom's picks are on `offseason.html` (Task 189). The desk's pauses (Task 187) are in §18.13.

**The order after the final** (Tom, 2026-10-11, after Task 258: the champions were crowned twice, and the final had no highlights): the final, the trophy lift, the full-time card; the match talk (it crowns them), the interview's tee-up, the interview; the wrap-up leading into the highlights (`highlightsNext`, as after any match; `championsNext` no longer said); **the final's highlights package** (as after any match, about 1½–2½ minutes); **the champions' cards** (`segments.championMinutes`, no talk before them: the champions' talk and `studio.championTalkSeconds` went, its lines `championsTitle`, `championsHow`, `championsAgain`, `championsFirst` and `championsNext` unused, their clips kept); then the awards' talk and the awards. The show repeated through the night runs from the champions' cards (the final itself is re-aired in full). The sign-off about 20:53–20:57, so `chronicle.offseasonAiredMinutes` went from 60 to 65. The gold boards stop either side of the tunnel's gap (the gate looked shut as the runners-up went off), and the off-season music is pre-rolled silent (§19 sound).

### 19.1 The trophy lift (Task 190)

- **Where:** straight after the final's whistle, in the handshakes' place (the segment is still `handshakes`, marked `lift: true`, so the guide, the ground, the gate, the scorebug and the transitions treat it as the handshakes they replace); the post-match show then runs as after any match (Tom: kept as it is), then the champions, now `segments.championMinutes` **4** (was 10: the lift took most of their time), then the off-season. The final night grows by 2 minutes. `npm run rundown -- --day 7` lists it as `trophy lift`.
- **Its parts** (`settings.trophyLift`, `trophyLift.js`, 6 minutes in all): the celebration (90 s), the guard of honour (90 s), the anthem (60 s), the lift (120 s). Everyone starts from where they were at the whistle, the benches included (not the injured, who stay in their seats), so nothing jumps (a test walks it every 100 ms).
  - **The celebration:** the champions run to their fans by the bottom touchline and jump about together; the runners-up stand about, some with hands on heads. Caption SEASON N CHAMPIONS, "Rime beat Vintners 4-3" (with the free shots after a shootout).
  - **The handshakes, slower than usual:** the runners-up line up across the pitch applauding, a guard of honour; the champions queue at its end and file along it one by one, a step and a handshake at each runner-up, gathering past the far end. Caption A GUARD OF HONOUR. **Task 212** (Tom: they moved as a joined line and rubber-banded): the near end is the champions' side, so celebrating on the right it all runs mirrored, right to left; they leave the boards one after another (nearest the end first), cut across to a spot past the line's end and below it, then up to two loose rows of a queue; along the line a steady walk, a hand out as they pass each runner-up (stepping and stopping at each, with a set-off every step's length, had the whole file stretching and bunching in unison).
  - **The anthem:** the champions line up across halfway; their own tune (the walk-out's, repeated to fill most of the part), the champions' fans waving; the runners-up go to their bench. Caption THE CHAMPIONS' ANTHEM.
  - **The lift:** a gold podium rises on the centre circle and the champions climb onto it, the captain in the middle; after `closeUpAfterSeconds` (12) the picture cuts, with a flash, to the close-up (Tom's pick B, `liftArt.js`): the stands a blur of the champions' colours, spotlights, the five who started the final on a gold podium (SEASON N CHAMPIONS on its front) in their kit with the crest, framed like the interview, the captain (their best outfield starter, `liftFive`) lifting the Scorch Cup (the final's trophy art), the others cheering, confetti in the champions' colours and gold. Over the ground in Full, the whole screen in Broadcast. Caption THE SCORCH CUP. **The second cut** (Tom, 2026-10-20, his pick C on offseason.html; `trophyLift.stageAfterSeconds` 50 into the close-up): a flash and back to the wide shot, a stage where the podium stood (`liftArt.js` `drawLiftStage`), an arch over it in the champions' colours with CHAMPIONS on its banner; the captain holds up a small cup first, then it goes down the line left to right (`stageHolder`), the others waving; fireworks over the stands (`drawFireworks`) and confetti; the caption back.
- **The crowd stays** (`standsFullness`): the champions' fans full until the last tenth, the runners-up's thinning as after the handshakes; the full-time card then empties the ground as usual. Their mood (`liftMood`): the champions' fans jumping as they celebrate and as the cup goes up, everyone waving through the handshakes, the champions' fans through their anthem. Sounds (`liftCues`): a roar for the champions, a small cheer for the guard of honour, the anthem and a small cheer after it, cheering all through the lift (Tom, 2026-10-20): a run of overlapping roars as the cup goes up easing over about 20 s, waves every 6 s, a fresh surge at the cut to the stage, waves to the end (`CUP_CHEER`, `STAGE_CHEER`).
- **Dev panel:** jumps to the trophy lift and to the cup lifted (just before the close-up).
- **Re-airs of the final** keep their short handshakes for now; how the night of repeats shows the lift is Task 195. New desk lines for the lift wait for the voices task (196).

- **Task 197 (Tom's notes):** the celebration takes three ways in turn, a third each (a huddle hopping together; pairs hugging then high-fiving; spread along the bottom boards waving to their fans), REGROUP_MS (3.5 s) to move between them. The champions reach the guard of honour round its near end (along the bottom, then up), never through the runners-up's line. After it the runners-up go off down the tunnel, one after another, and only then (`ANTHEM_GO`, 30% into the anthem's part) do the champions leave the far end for halfway; the anthem plays once they're lined up. The referee and the runners-up's manager leave as after any match, over the celebration; the champions' manager stays on the touchline throughout, clapping and cheering in bursts (`liftCoach`). The close-up's stage runs the whole width of the shot; the lower third goes at the cut to it (it covered SEASON N CHAMPIONS in Broadcast); in Full, the quiet band under it rather than the turf.

- **Task 199 (Tom: the fans' hands under the turf):** in Broadcast the turf under the bottom boards is drawn before the stands, so the bottom stand's front row's raised arms and hands show over its edge (it was drawn after them, covering them).

### 19.2 The awards (Task 191)

- **Where:** after the champions (`segments.championMinutes` 4), before the off-season: the desk's intro (`awardsIntro`, then `awardsList` naming the five in order; `settings.awards.talkSeconds` at most, sized to its lines; it opens with the studio jingle, `JINGLE_TALKS`), then each award for `awardSeconds` (60, Tom), in `settings.awards.order`: the Spark (Task 252, from season 2: the best rookie; its prize a sparkle on a stand, Tom's pick C on `offseason.html`; the card says BEST ROOKIE and the winner's season in their position's stats, `rookieLineText`: a striker's goals and assists, a midfielder's assists and goals, a defender's stops and assists, a keeper's save percentage; never a score; the desk's `awardsListSix` names six, voiced in Task 258), the Blaze, the Compass, the Quell, the Ironclad, the Ember Boot last. An award the sim didn't give is left out. RECORDS lists no "most" for the Spark (won once at most); it counts in MOST AWARDS. About 5¼ minutes in all; the guide lists them as one **AWARDS** entry (the intro with them); the rundown as `talk · awards` and `award · <id>`. The new desk lines are silent (typed in the bubble) until the voices task.
- **What each shows** (`awardShow.js`, from the sim's `award` events, which now carry the two runners-up as `shortlist`; nothing is worked out again): the award's name and what it's for ("MOST STOPS · DEFENDERS · REGULAR SEASON"), the winner with their team and stat ("58 GOALS", "77.6% SAVED · 111 OF 143"), and the next two.
- **The plinth** (Tom's pick B, `awardArt.js`), over the scene (the ground in Full, the whole screen in Broadcast): the prize on a lit plinth with a glint crossing it; the card at the top left (below the scorebug in Broadcast) with the award, then the third-placed named (`shortlistAt`, 6 s), the second (`secondAt`, 12 s); the winner steps in from the right (`winnerAt`, 19 s) in their kit with the crest, framed like the interview (Task 189's framing B), and lifts the prize overhead (`liftAt`, 23 s), the card naming them; they hold it up to the end.
- **The prizes** (pixel art, `PRIZES`): the Ember Boot a gold boot with embers, the Blaze a flame on a gold base, the Compass a gold compass, the Quell a gold breakwater with a wave breaking on it (Tom's pick C), the Ironclad an iron shield in a gold rim.
- **Dev panel:** a jump to the awards; the off-season's jump now comes after them.
- **Task 198 (Tom):** no glint crossing the prize on its plinth. The crest on every shirt (the plinth, the interview, the farewells, the draft, the lift) is the team's mark alone in its second colour (`crests.js` `drawShirtCrest`), no border.

### 19.3 The farewells (Task 192)

- **Where:** after the awards, opening the off-season (inside `segments.offseasonSummaryMinutes`, so the night keeps its length); one segment a retiree (`farewell`, `perPlayerSeconds` 25), then the lottery, then the off-season's talk (moved after the draw, which it names; off the studio jingle's list now, as it no longer opens a programme), then the off-season cards as before. The guide lists them all as OFF-SEASON.
- **Who** (`offseasonShow.js` `notableRetirements`; Tom: long careers and awards): the season's retirements from a team (not out of free agency), ranked by seasons played, each award won counting `awardWeight` (3) seasons more, up to `max` (4); from the sim's `retirement` events and `league.history`'s awards.
- **The gallery** (Tom's pick C, `farewellArt.js`), over the scene: FAREWELLS · SEASON N; framed portraits in their last teams' kits (the crest on the shirt); the one whose turn it is in a gold frame, waving, the others dimmed; their card beneath: FAREWELL · RETIRES AFTER SEASON N, the name, position and age, the crest with "6 SEASONS · LAST WITH ANVILS", and **AWARDS: 2× THE QUELL · THE BLAZE** in gold (Tom: "AWARDS:" so it's clear).

### 19.4 The draft lottery (Task 192)

- **Where:** after the farewells, one `lottery` segment: the odds for `settings.lottery.oddsSeconds` (20), then each pick drawn for `revealSeconds` (25), the last pick first so the No. 1 comes last: 2 minutes for four.
- **What** (`lotteryView`): the teams that missed the playoffs, worst first, with their odds for the No. 1 pick (`rules.draft.lotteryWeights`: 40/30/20/10%) and where they finished; the draw is the sim's own (`draft_lottery`), so the screen shows what was decided.
- **The tumbler** (Tom's pick A, `lotteryArt.js`), over the scene: a glass drum turning with a crest ball for each team still in it, speeding up for each draw; at `dropAt` (6 s) the ball drops down the chute into the tray and its row on the board fills in (the crest, the team, FINISHED 8TH · 40% ODDS in Broadcast). The board: DRAFT LOTTERY · SEASON N, the odds with bars, then PICK 1 to 4.

### 19.5 The draft (Task 193)

- **Where:** after the lottery, before the off-season's talk (which names the No. 1 pick), inside the off-season's 15 minutes: one `draft` segment a pick, every pick in order (`draftPicks`, from the sim's `draft_pick` events). Round 1 at `settings.draft.round1Seconds` (45) a pick, round 2 at `round2Seconds` (15): 8 minutes for sixteen. The off-season cards that follow drop their draft card (it aired in full).
- **Round 1** (Tom's pick: the board, then the close-up; `draftArt.js`): the board of the round's eight (the picks made named with their position (HOME-GROWN too until 2026-10-18), the one on the clock pulsing), "WITH PICK 1 · THE GULLS SELECT…" over it at first; from `walkAt` (4 s) the pick walks up in the board's window in a training tee, named beside it; at `cutAt` (14 s) a cut, with a flash, to the close-up: the team's crest printed across the backdrop, the pick from the chest up framed like the interview, a beat in the tee, the shirt (with the crest) fading on over it from `kitAt` (18 s, a fade, so no grey shows under raised arms), a fist-pump from `cheerAt` (21 s); the card from `cardAt` (23 s).
- **Round 2** on the board only: the slot on the clock, the name up at `round2NameAt` (5 s), the card below.
- **The card:** ROUND · PICK · TEAM, the name, position, age (at the coming season) and region, **PROJECTED** with the role their potential points to (STAR PLAYER 85+, STARTER 75+, SQUAD PLAYER 62+, else LONG-TERM PROJECT) and how soon from their rating now (READY TO PLAY NOW 68+, A SEASON OR TWO AWAY 58+, else THREE SEASONS OR SO AWAY): no ratings on screen (Tom). Tags: HOME-GROWN (from the team's region, Task 176) until 2026-10-18 (Tom: unexplained in the draft, where nothing opens a card; dropped here and on the moves board, the mechanic kept; where viewers learn it is the second half of his note 8). SUITS THE GULLS (a good `styleFit`, Task 177) was dropped in Task 210 (Tom: unclear without team styles on air; back after the launch, backlog).
  - Task 194: a pick sent to the reserves (the sim's `reserve` event, `docs/simulation.md` §9c) is tagged TO THE KINDLING LEAGUE; tags wrap to a second row when they don't fit.
- **Task 198 (Tom):** no "WITH PICK X · THE … SELECT" pane and no name beside the window before the cut; the shirt fades on and the card comes in together at `kitAt` (16 s, two after the cut; `cardAt` is gone), the fist-pump from `cheerAt` (18 s). The board's slots show each team's colour swatch (as the scorebug's) by its abbreviation, not the crest, which sat over the text in Full.

### 19.6 The moves and the sign-off (Task 194)

- **Where:** after the draft, then the sign-off, then the off-season's talk and its cards (those until Task 195 rebuilds the night), all inside the off-season's 15 minutes; `settings.moves`, `studio.signoffTalkSeconds`. Listed as OFF-SEASON in the guide.
- **The moves** (`movesView` in `offseasonShow.js`, from the sim's `signing`, `release` and `call_up` events), in Tom's order: the **free-agent signings** (FROM the team that released them this off-season, or FREE AGENCY), the **veteran releases** (TO FREE AGENCY; most notable first, each award counting `farewells.awardWeight` seasons, then seasons in the league), the **call-ups** (FROM the KINDLING LEAGUE). Not shown: releases of this draft's picks, reserves whose rights ran out (Tom: the weakest in the pool), or a release whose player signed elsewhere the same off-season (the signing tells it). Up to `maxLines` (10); in the live league so far 2–5 a season.
- **The board** (Tom's pick B on `offseason.html`; `movesArt.js`): a departures board over the scene, PLAYER, P, FROM, TO, `perPage` (7) lines a page; after `leadSeconds` (2) each line gets `lineSeconds` (10), its letters flapping in left to right, FROM and TO settling in green (signed), red (released) or blue (called up), a marker on the lit line; its player's card below from `cardAt` (1.5 s): SIGNED / RELEASED / CALLED UP · TEAM, the name, position, age and seasons (a call-up's seasons in the Kindling League), tags: awards, FORMER NO. 1 or 2 PICK, RELEASED BY THE …, a call-up's draft (ROUND 2 PICK 12 · SEASON 3 DRAFT) (HOME-GROWN too until 2026-10-18). No ratings (as on the draft card). Then `tailSeconds` (4); at least `minSeconds` (30) in all.
- **The sign-off** at the desk (talk topic `signoff`, three lines in `data/studio.json`): the season done (SEASON 4 · DONE), the new season's opener ("The new season opens with the Gulls at home", the graphic SEASON 5 · MONDAY 00:30 · GULLS V OASIS: the time from the calendar's first slot, shown, never said, so the clips can be made ahead), goodbye. Silent, typed in the bubble, until Task 196's voices. The desk's NEXT screen falls back to the talk's next fixture, so after the final it shows the new season's opener.
- **Task 198:** the board starts below the scorebug in Broadcast (as the draft's). Every new screen checked against the scorebug in Broadcast: the awards, farewells, lottery, draft, moves and the lift clear it.

### 19.7 The night after the show (Task 195)
- **The playoffs' reels (Task 213, Tom):** the filler is the semis' packages (the later first), then three reels, **PLAYOFF GOALS** (every goal, golden goals included), **PLAYOFF SAVES** (the saves off hot shots: a playoffs has about sixty saves, about thirteen big ones) and **PLAYOFF SCORCHERS** (every scorcher), `highlights.reelPasses` (2) times round, then the semis again, each with its short block and only while the whole unit fits, then a block to fill. A reel is one kind of moment across the two semis and the final, in the order played (`highlights.js` `reelClips`, clips as in a package; shootouts stay in the packages and the final's re-air). It is a `highlights` segment with a `reel` (its kind, title, season), each clip naming its own match, so the pitch, ground, scorebug (SEMI or FINAL) and STATS follow the clip; its opening sting's plate reads HIGHLIGHTS, the reel's name and SEASON N PLAYOFFS; the TV guide lists it as HL and its name. Typical lengths (season 1): goals 5:44, saves 2:23, scorchers 2:10 (about 4, 3 and 1 minutes on average); a reel with nothing in it is left out. Names in `highlights.reels`.

- **Where:** from the sign-off to the new season's run-in (Monday's opening at about 00:25), timed back from the run-in (`programme.js` `night`). Tom: the trophy lift goes with the final's repeat; the night ends on a long run of ads, then the new season.
- **In order:** filler first, recent matches' highlights (the semis first, then the last round; never the final) with short studio blocks, then a block to fill (**Task 213**, Tom: the last round's packages gave way to the playoffs' **reels**, see below); then the **final re-aired** (intro, line-up, the match with half-time shortened, the **trophy lift** as it aired in the handshakes' place (`reair` part `handshakes` with `lift`), the full-time card, the recorded interview) and the **show repeated** (the champions' cards to the sign-off), each followed by a studio block, in turn, as many pairs as fit (three on day 7), so the night ends on the show; then the **closing ads**: the whole slate (less the playoffs promo) `ads.closingSlates` (1) times, in an order of its own (`adOrder`, seeded by the gap `<final id>|closing`), between a bumper in and out; then the **new season's countdown** (**Task 277b**, Tom: in place of a closing studio block, whose cards and talk after midnight were about the wrong season): over the ident's dimmed light bars, THE SCORCH LEAGUE, SEASON N in the ident's block font lighting from the middle, and THE OPENING IN 0:SS ticking down, about `opening.countdownSeconds` (30; the repeats can end a few seconds late), on the ident's picture so it runs straight into the ident with no band.
- **The repeats are the show as it aired:** each piece moved along in time, its words (the talks' scripts), lottery, picks and moves the same, marked `repeat: true`. The scorebug's tag reads **REPEAT** (the countdown to the opener beside it); the schedule lists **REP SKA V LIS F**, **REP CHAMPIONS**, **REP AWARDS**, **REP OFF-SEASON**.
- **After midnight** (Task 277b): the calendar already counts the new season, so until its first kick-off the studio cards read the moment just before it (`wrapUpAt`, calendar.js): SEASON 2's countdown, not SEASON 3's, the playoff scorers, the latest results. The desk's clips still say it's early in the new one and what's coming up. The ads quoting the season's numbers (Foundry Fuel's scorchers, Ember & Co.'s burns, the Almanac's goals) quote the season just finished, LAST SEASON, until the new season's first match has finished (`adFacts`' `lastSeason`).
- **Fitting the night:** the pairs are counted with the most a block can run, then built once to measure and undone, so the time to spare goes to the filler and the countdown stays about its length. With no room for a pair, the filler runs to the closing ads.
- **No longer aired:** the off-season's talk and recap cards after the sign-off (the show has aired all of it).

### 19.8 The off-season's sound (Tasks 203–204)

- **The music** (Tom's pick D, pulse: `musicRecipes.js`, sound kind `music`, `settings.sound.music`): a 34 s loop (E-flat's vi–IV–I–V at 112 bpm: a soft four-on-the-floor, claps, offbeat hats, a pad pumping with the beat, plucked chords, an offbeat bass, a bell hook the second time round) under the awards, the farewells, the draft and the moves, in the repeats too. Each unbroken run of those scenes starts the loop from its top (the programme gives each scene its run's start, `musicFrom`), so every screen hears the same; a pass is queued 1.5 s before the last ends. Out (a fade, `fadeOutMs` 800) whenever the desk talks and for the lottery, the trophy lift and the champions' cards (Tom; music in more places is for a sound update after the launch); in (`fadeInMs` 1200) as the next run starts. **Pre-rolled** (Tom, 2026-10-11: a strange sound at the cut from the desk into the awards, the loop's first kick and chord starting there over the scene's whoosh): each run's loop now starts `preRollSeconds` (3) early, silent, timed to reach its top as the run begins, and is brought up over `fadeInMs` then, so nothing starts at the cut; a pre-roll waits for the last run's fade-out to finish. On its own bus (`audio.fadeMusic`); its loudness `levels.music`, under Sound (no slider of its own, Tom).
- **The lottery** (Tom's pick A, `settings.lottery.sound`: rattle): each draw, as it starts (or joining part-way up to its landing), the balls rattling quicker as the drum spins up over a low rumble, the drop down the chute at `dropAt`, a clunk into the tray, a bell; louder since (`levels.lottery` 0.85, was 0.5). Tom then asked for the music softly under it (`music.lotteryLevel` 0.35), so the awards, farewells, lottery, draft and moves are one run. **Task 211** (Tom): the same loop plays under every run of studio cards, between matches all season and after the off-season (they're long and quiet), each run starting it from its top `music.studioLeadSeconds` (3.5 s) in, after the studio jingle (3.2 s); a run of cards straight after an off-season scene carries that run on. The desk, ads and bumpers, packages, re-airs and live matches fade it out. The odds list shows each team's kit swatch, not its crest (the four sat bunched).
- **The sound board** (`sounds.html`): the music's styles (C mellow synth, D pulse, E chillhop, F tropical) with a Stop (`audio.stop`), the lottery's three takes, the ads' effects.
- **The ads' effects** (Task 205, Tom: the sound board's style, for every ad): each ad in `data/spots.json` lists its effects (`musicRecipes.js` effectLayers: 34 small sounds, from a can's fizz and a gull's cry to a vault's clunk and pages riffling), each at its moment in seconds (timed to the ad's drawing) with a gain to soften one; they play with the jingle as the ad starts (not when loading into the middle of one), under it. `ads.html` opens with every ad playable with its sounds.

### 19.9 The off-season, audited (Task 206)

- **The night's order** (day 7, season 1): the final 20:00, the trophy lift, the post-match show, the champions, the awards, farewells, lottery, draft, moves and sign-off to about 20:54, filler, three pairs of the final and the show from about 21:30, the closing ads and the countdown, the opening at 00:25. Nothing overlaps or runs short.
- **The champions** (Tom): `championMinutes` 1 (was 4): one card (the champions, the final's score, the Pennant, their titles, the playoffs' top scorer); the season's top scorer left to the Ember Boot.
- **After the final** (Tom): its own rotation, `segments.afterFinalCards`: the latest results, the playoffs' scorers, the new season's countdown, the team spotlight and **the season's honours** (new: the champions, the Pennant, the five awards' winners, the No. 1 pick; the latest finished season, even after midnight). The form guide, the leaders and the top scorers dropped (stale, or the awards again).
- **Highlights** (Tom): no package more than `highlights.perGap` (2) times in a gap (the channel's first gap played its one match about 40 times). A long stretch of studio fills in whole blocks (ads when due, a desk clip, four cards), the last stretched (`fillTo`), not one long run of cards: day 1's first gap now about 2 hours of blocks with six ad breaks; an ordinary day unchanged.

## 20. [It remembers] The chronicle on screen

Milestone 4 (vision §9, §12; the hand-off in `docs/tasks.md`). The chronicle itself (`src/chronicle/chronicle.js`, `records.js`) is in `docs/simulation.md` §12b.

### 20.1 Only what has aired (Task 219)

The live league simulates a match at its kick-off and the whole off-season at the final, so the log runs ahead of the screen. `LiveLeague.chronicle` therefore takes each event only once it has aired (`liveLeague.js`, a queue released in `advanceTo`): a match's events, and the league events logged with it (the table after its round, the playoff seeding, the season's end), at its **final whistle on screen** (kick-off plus its timeline's length; a match kicked off over three hours before the moment being reached needs no timeline); the **awards, the off-season** (retirements, development, the draft, reserves, moves) and the new season's start **when the next season starts** (Monday 00:00): the off-season show airs them the night before, and the chronicle catches up by morning. So everything that reads the chronicle (the CHRONICLE pane, the cards, LATEST, a record broken on air) can trust it never gets ahead of the picture. Tests: `tests/chronicle.test.js` (not while a match is played, not a second before its whistle, at it; the final's result at its whistle, its awards and draft only once season 2 starts).

### 20.2 The CHRONICLE pane (Task 219)

**The button:** the third in the row, in the Record book's place, with **the Rulebook's open book** (Tom, Task 218); the Rulebook now has **a referee's whistle**. Lit while open; Full only like the other panes (Broadcast closes it). About's GUIDE lists it ("Chronicle: the latest, the League's history and every past season, and the records."). `chronicle.html` (Task 218's mock-ups) shows both icons large beside the old trophy.

**The pane** (`overlays/chroniclePane.js`; look B from Task 218's mock-ups): three tabs under the CHRONICLE title, kept while the channel is open:
- **LATEST:** "coming soon" until Task 220.
- **HISTORY:** **PAST SEASONS**, newest first, under the note "Click a season for its table, playoffs and leaders." (Tom: clicking wasn't obvious), one row each (the Cup and the Pennant), each opening its page in the pane (the eras' CUP WINNERS rows were dropped, Tom: the list already shows each season's Cup): the Cup with the final's score and the Pennant with its points (shaded crests), the table (the playoff line), the playoffs (the final first), the awards (Task 252, Tom: in the show's order, the Spark first from season 2; each winner's name opens their card), the leaders (goals, assists, playoff goals). **‹ HISTORY** goes back: one level only (Tom). Before any season has finished: "The first season is under way." ### 20.3 LATEST and HISTORY (Task 220)

What's notable is picked by `src/chronicle/notable.js` (pure, ids and numbers; the seed of Talk that remembers' facts module) with the bars in `data/chronicle.json` (`latest`, `history`, `legends`); the pane words it (`overlays/chroniclePane.js`).

**LATEST** (Tom: the whole week, choosier, by day): the season shown (the latest with a match on screen), newest first, under a sub-heading for each day (MON…SUN, in the league's time zone; Tom: not THU on every line), each line the pane's full width. A **season's story** (`seasonStory`): milestones (games only from the 200th), a match of 5 goals, 4 assists or 18 saves (at most one line a player a match, the biggest), a run of 5 wins or 6 without a win (each run once, at its longest), the Pennant and the playoff places at the regular season's end, the Cup. About 25 lines a season (50 seasons measured: 5 goals 6.6 a season, 4 goals would be 17). **The off-season leads** (Tom): its awards (in the show's order), legends retiring, the Kindling call-ups and the No. 1 pick, labelled with the final's day, from the moment the off-season show has aired them (`settings.chronicle.offseasonAiredMinutes`, 65 after the final's kick-off, 60 until the final's highlights came in; the show signs off by about 20:53–20:57, a test checks) through the new season's first day (`latestOffseasonDays`). Before any match: "The first match is still to come."

**HISTORY:** **ON THIS DAY** (up to three things from earlier seasons on today's day of the week, a season being a week: Cups first, then milestones, big matches, runs; labelled S14…), then PAST SEASONS ("Click a season for its table, playoffs, leaders and highlights."). Each season's **landmarks** are at the foot of its page, under HIGHLIGHTS (Tom: the past seasons are the heart of the pane; eras of landmarks would have grown to many screens). Landmarks (`landmarks`, past tense): the first season; a team's first Cup, Cups in a row (back to back, three…), a Cup ending a drought of 8 seasons or more, each "and the Pennant" when the table was won too, else "won the Pennant and the Cup" (one line a season, Tom); an all-time record changing hands (goals, assists, saves, stops; not games, which changed hands 9 times in 45 seasons) from season 5; legends retiring with what made them one ("646 goals", "7 Cups").

**Legends** (`isLegend`, `legendWhy`; Tom: by rules, at retirement): a career of 450 goals, 225 assists, 2,500 saves or 3,500 stops; or 5 awards; or 7 Cups as a regular (half the team's regular-season games): about 21 in 50 seasons, one every two or three weeks (Tom: about one a season, from 350/175/2,000/2,800, 3 awards or 6 Cups, was a touch too often). Ranking against the all-time lists made a young league's first long careers legends wholesale (22 in 20 seasons); these fixed bars make about 1 a season after the first ten (three seeds, 50 seasons: 35 to 43), long careers being most of it. 4 Cups made every starter of a four-in-a-row dynasty a legend (Tom expected a dynasty's star to win 3 or 4), so 6. Tighter if they feel too common: higher bars or 4 awards.

### 20.4 A record broken, on air (Task 220b)

Tom's pick **A** on `recordbroken.html`: when a record falls in a match, the caption bar is its own for `captions.recordMs` (7 s), gold (the golden goal's), with a gold stripe: **ALL-TIME RECORD** "N. Yarosh · 134 career goals, passing D. Santoro" or **SEASON RECORD** "C. Sabrier · 52 goals this season, past V. Bonnefoy's 51 (S12)". It comes at the event that passed the record: the goal (or the assist's goal) after the goal's own caption (`goalMs`), a save or a stop at once; two in a match one after the other. Live and in re-airs; not in highlights (Tom: no lower thirds there, and too quick).

**How the channel knows in time:** the chronicle only takes a match at its final whistle (§20.1), so the live league also keeps a `RecordWatch` (`src/chronicle/chronicle.js`; the chronicle has one too) fed ahead of the screen: `live.records.inMatch(matchId)` from kick-off. `recordCaptions` (`captions.js`, pure) finds the event that passed each record, counting the player's total back from the end of the match; `captionAt` takes them (`ctx.records`) and gives them the bar over anything else. Records count all-time (the regular season and playoffs together) and single-season (regular season) goals, assists, saves and stops, someone else's best passed, from season 5 (`history.recordsFrom`): about one a season.

**In the CHRONICLE** (the same log, `chronicle.recordsBroken`): LATEST "N. Yarosh: all-time goals record, 134" on its day; a season page's HIGHLIGHTS "N. Yarosh took the all-time goals record from D. Santoro", "B. Tordal set the season goals record, 47" (the season's final figure), each holder once per record.

Tests: `tests/recordCaptions.test.js` (the live league's record watch agrees with the chronicle and knows a match's records at its kick-off; a goal record's caption after the goal's, gone after recordMs, none without the records), `tests/records.test.js` (each record passes someone else's, rises, from season 5).

Signings come biggest deal first. Two new kinds: **RE-SIGNED** (gold: back with the team that let him go, after testing the market) and **DEAL ENDED** (orange: his deal ran out and nobody signed him), in place of RELEASED for those. `movesView` takes the config for OVER THE MARKET.

Click a team for its contracts.", every team's payroll with a bar against the cap (gold when nearly full, red over); a click opens the team's contracts under its row in a block of their own, a line under them (Tom: room to breathe) (name and wage, biggest first; the name opens the player card, ‹ MONEY · ABBR back). ## 22. [Momentum] momentum on screen (Task 238)

Mock-ups on `momentum.html` (the real channel frozen just after a real scorcher and a real burn); Tom's picks 2026-10-09: **B** and **C**, no commentary (the scorcher already says it).
- **Who has it** (`momentum.js` `momentumAt`, pure): from the match's `momentum` events (Task 237): the latest one revealed, with how much is left by the game clock against its `until` (rules.momentum.seconds, 90: about 50 s of picture), none after a kick-off, half time, full time, the start of overtime or in a shootout.
- **B, the scorebug:** the team's kit swatch glows heat orange, as strong as the momentum left, fading out (`overlays/scorebug.js` `glow`; cleared at FT and NEXT). Tom: fine that its length isn't spelled out; no flame or bar (A was mocked up and dropped).
- **C, the players:** as it's gained, a heat-orange ring pulses out round each of the team's players on the pitch, centred on the body and **under the sprites** (Tom), growing and fading over a second (`pitch.js` `drawMomentumRings`, drawn just before the players). **Task 255 (Tom: too subtle to catch):** now three pulses, 2 px, heat orange with a heat-yellow core, each growing by 10 px over 0.7 s, 0.3 s apart (about 1.3 s in all), still under the sprites (Tom's pick B of four on `momentum.html`, drawn there with the channel's own code; the others a glow at the feet, the rings over the players); its look is data, `animation.momentumRing`. **From the goal (Tom, after Task 255):** a scorcher's momentum came in the picture only at the restart (its event follows the goal's pause in the log), a burn's at once; now a scorcher's rings and glow start at the goal itself, through the celebration (`momentum.js` `momentumAt`, timed from the goal so the rings play once). The bonus is unchanged: the sim's, on the game clock, which the pause stops, so it still starts at the restart. A substitute who comes on later has the bonus too (it's the team's) but missed the ring.
- Live, in re-airs and highlights alike (the timeline's events).
- **The Rulebook** (HEAT, Tom's words): "A scorcher gives the scoring team momentum, and a burn gives it to the other team: for a short while they're a little sharper with the ball. Another scorcher or burn refreshes momentum."
- **About's GUIDE** (the scorebug section, after the heat meter; Tom: the scorebug's marks are explained there, the pitch's aren't, so not the rings): "A team's colour on the scorebug glows orange while it has momentum, fading as it wears off (see the Rulebook).", a glowing swatch beside it (`momentum` on the item, `.rb-momentum`).

Tests: `tests/momentumOnScreen.test.js` (full as gained, the right team, fading, none before the first or after the whistle).

### 22.1 Clear chances in front of goal (Task 239)

**Measured** (the live league's season 2, 56 matches, the picture sampled every 0.2 s): an attacker on the ball within a fifth of the pitch of the goal they attack, near the middle, with no outfield defender in the lane to goal, about 9 times a match; about 2 a match end in a shot within 1.5 s (real chances), most of the rest pass in a moment (median 0.6 s), and about 1.9 a match end with the ball lost or burned before any shot, 0.57 a match after a hold of a second or more: the moment Tom saw. The sim has no positions: it shoots on a roll once the team has worked the ball into range, so the picture could show a carrier alone in front of goal when no shot was coming.

**The picture fix** (Tom's pick, after the side-by-side on `clearchances.html`; results unchanged): `motion.js` `stepGoalSide`, each phase of play: if the attacker on the ball (where they stand as the phase starts, where they're headed, or whoever it's passed to) is within `GOAL_SIDE_NEAR` (0.2) of the goal line and `GOAL_SIDE_WIDE` (0.2) of the middle with no outfield defender within `GOAL_SIDE_LANE` (0.06) of the line to goal, and the sim's next moment isn't that team's shot, the nearest outfield defender steps in `GOAL_SIDE_GAP` (0.05) goal-side of them, sprinting if need be. A real chance (a shot next) is left exactly as it was. Wasted holds of a second or more: 0.57 to 0.32 a match (the rest reach the space mid-phase, on a run); real chances kept (98 of 102, the difference knock-on, not the step-in). The pitch map's targets all met (the off-the-ball attacker beyond the line 1.0%, 1.1% before). `createMotion`'s `goalSide` option (on by default) lets the dev page show both.

### 22.2 The big kick (Task 240)

Mock-ups on `bigkick.html` (real keepers' passes side by side, today and kicked); Tom's picks 2026-10-09. A keeper's pass of `BIG_KICK_MIN` (0.35 pitch lengths) or more goes up high, like a goalkeeper's long kick (`motion.js` `choosePath`, the pass path's `kick`): its peak `BIG_KICK_SHARE` (0.15) of its length, at most `BIG_KICK_PEAK` (0.08, about 29 pixels), its shadow on the turf (the chip's drawing, `pitch.js` `drawBall`). **Its flight** (Tom: a straight line up and away from the keeper, then the more rounded arc toward the end; the chip's even arch looked to start high): `kickArc`, a cubic Bézier of height against the way along (`KICK_CURVE`), climbing in a straight line, peaking 70% of the way, then rounding over and dropping to the receiver. **Its speed** `settings.animation.bigKickSpeed` (0.4 pitch lengths a second; 0.3 was a touch slow, Tom), so it hangs about 1.1 s (median; a pass along the turf that long, 0.5 s); squeezed shorter when the sim's next moment comes soon after the restart (a pass lands by then). About 19 a match, half of keepers' passes (Tom kept 0.35 over 0.45, about a quarter). A receiver may play it straight on, first time. Results unchanged; the pitch map's targets all met. `createMotion`'s `bigKick` option (on by default) lets the dev page show both.

### 22.3 Three fixes from getting rid of the ball (Task 241, dropped)

The clearance (dropped, `docs/simulation.md` §10d) changed which matches the sim made, and the new ones turned up three rare faults in the picture, fixed and kept: **the step-in** (§22.1) never just before a breakaway (it left a defender goal-side of the runner); **the keeper's outlet** (Task 88) always along the turf, never a big kick (its slower flight left the keeper on the ball as it was lost); **the referee** stopping short of a crease for a foul right by the goal (`pitchside.js`).

**A long ball that lands too late** (Tom, 2026-10-16: about once every match or two, a keeper's long ball was followed at once by a blur of a pass back to a defender). A keeper holding the ball may pass it to an open teammate; if that's a big kick, it hangs in the air (`bigKickSpeed`), and it could land a few hundred ms before the sim's next moment, so the pass on to the sim's next carrier was squeezed into that. A keeper's hold pass is now a big kick only if it lands at least `passMaxMs` before the next moment (otherwise a nearer teammate, or the keeper keeps it). A season's scan found 6 in 30 matches before, none in 86 after (two seeds); about 14% fewer big kicks; the pitch map's targets all still met. `tests/motion.test.js` checks the ball never moves at 1.6 pitch lengths a second or more in the 1.5 s after a long ball lands.

### 22.4 Breakaways (Task 253, before It responds)

Tom saw two things: a pause before the breakaway, then the runner off; and sometimes a second attacker alone too, like 2 on 0. **Measured** (185 breakaways, 60 matches): the player who breaks stood still 0.65 s on average just before it (107 for half a second or more), either on the ball after passing it about or waiting on their spot for the through ball, then sprinted at once; and in 176 of them another attacker was past the beaten defence (the defenders trail behind the ball, Task 37, while the runner's teammates stayed at their attacking spots). **Built** (Tom's pick B of three on `breakaways.html`, real season-2 breakaways side by side; `settings.play.breakaway`, `motion.js` and `formation.js` `hang`): in the phase before the breakaway nobody passes it about, and the player who breaks sets off goal-ward `leadMs` (1,000) before it at a run, carrying the ball or running onto the through ball, then goes straight on (no reaction delay); on the breakaway the runner's teammates off the ball stay at least `hang` (0.08 pitch lengths) behind the ball, level with the chasing defenders. Now none stands before the break and 1 in 185 has a second attacker past the defence (C, 0.15 behind, kept on the page). The pitch map otherwise unchanged; its tether check went from 1.1% to 0.7% (Task 254). Picture only. Test: `tests/motion.test.js` (Task 253).

### 22.5 The tether and visual offside (Task 254, before It responds)

Tom saw attackers standing well beyond the last defender now and then, though the tether (Task 80, §15.4) should stop it. **Measured** (60 matches, every moment an attacker off the ball stood beyond the last outfield defender, or the ball, by more than `tether` + 0.02, outside breakaways): 472 episodes, about 7 s a match, the longest 9.7 s, 167 more than 0.1 beyond; and 114 passes in about 15,000 played to a teammate standing offside (72 by more than 0.1). **Three causes, fixed** (`motion.js`, `formation.js`; picture only):
1. **Before a breakaway** the whole spell before it was drawn with the defence beaten and no tether (`beaten` from the look-ahead), however long: a third of the offside time, seconds of an attacker loitering beyond the line before the through ball. Now only the last phase before it.
2. **A blocker stepping out** (Task 83's bubble check brings the defender who blocks the next shot into its path, up to 5 s before): the attackers checked back to the new line after a tackle or a foul, not a block. Now a block too.
3. **A teammate on the ball** (a pass about): the formation measured the tether from the sim's carrier and exempted them, so the carrier up ahead waiting for it was never held back. Now, with a teammate on the ball, the line is measured from them and the carrier is held back like anyone else.

**After:** 316 episodes, about 3 s a match, the longest 3.3 s, 53 more than 0.1 beyond; passes to a teammate offside 17 (2 by more than 0.1), about 1 in 900. What's left is mostly the moment after a turnover, the new attacking side's striker still upfield while the other side's defence, caught upfield from its own attack, runs back: a counter-attack's shape (no pass, so no offside). The pitch map's check 0.7% to 0.3% (target 1%). Breakaways (§22.4): 8 in 185 now have a second attacker 0.03–0.05 beyond the trailing defence for part of the 1 s run (tethered before the break, dropping back as fast as the defence trails), against 1 when the pre-break spell was beaten throughout; kept (Claude: a few pixels, against 55 offside passes for drawing it beaten over the last two phases). Test: `tests/motion.test.js` (Task 254, eight matches: under 0.4% of passes to a teammate offside; before, 0.76%).

## 23. [It responds] The AUDIENCE pane

Everything viewer-related lives behind the **Audience** button (the fourth, left of INFO), in the **AUDIENCE** pane: a pop-up over the side column like the others, Full only (vision §8, §12 item 5; `docs/tasks.md` Tasks 259–261). Nothing a viewer sends ever feeds the sim.

### 23.1 The look (Task 259, Tom's picks 2026-10-11; `audience.html`)

One pane, no tabs (redesigned later, when there's more to do):
- **TELL THE LEAGUE** first (a poll matters less once voted): one line ("Spotted a bug, or have an idea? Send a note to the League.", Tom in Task 261) and a full-width **SEND A NOTE** button, which opens the note's form (§23.4).
- **A line** (the panel's edge colour), then **POLLS**: the poll in gold, its status under it (OPEN · CLOSES SUN 21:00, VOTED · CLOSES SUN 21:00, CLOSED · RESULT SOON), then past polls (CLOSED S2 SUN 21:00 · 177 VOTES). With one poll and two past ones, the list fits one view (172 of 218 px).
- **Every poll opens in the pane's place**, as the cards do (‹ AUDIENCE to go back). The poll: the question in gold, one short line ("Closes SUN 21:00"), the options as crests with names, and **VOTE**: a viewer picks one (outlined; they can change their mind) and votes; VOTE is dimmed until something is picked. Once voted, their pick is outlined in gold with YOUR VOTE and the rest dimmed; voted or closed, a plain plate in VOTE's place says **RESULT ON UPDATES** (the same height, so nothing moves). It fits one view: 217 of 218 px with eight teams; a poll with more options scrolls.
- **A past poll with its result** opens to each option's share: crest, letters, a bar and a percentage, newest first by votes, the winner in gold, with when it closed and the votes in all.

### 23.2 Polls as data (Task 260; `data/polls.json`, `src/channel/polls.js`)

A poll is dated data, like a rule change, so every browser shows the same: `id`, `question`, `options` (`"teams"`: every team, so expansion teams join by themselves), `season` with `opens` and `closes` as day times, and `result` (votes by option) once Tom announces it. One poll at a time, in order; checked in `src/config.js` (unique ids, closing after opening, within the season's days, no overlap, a result only for its options). The first: **"Who's your favourite team?"**, season 2, day 1 00:00 to day 7 21:00, a one-off (a standing "your team" choice to explore later).

`polls.js` (pure, shared by the channel, the dev server and the tests): `pollsAt(config, calendar, t)` gives the poll in gold (the newest seen while open or waiting for its result) and the past ones; a poll is seen from when it opens. **Tallies are never shown before the result is in the data** (Tom: few voters at first); the result is announced on INFO's UPDATES (Task 265).

### 23.3 Sending (Task 260; `src/channel/audience.js`, `src/cli/serve.js`)

The **audience module** is the one place the channel sends anything out: votes (and notes, Task 261) go to `config/settings.json`'s `audience.endpoint`. **Until the launch** that's the dev server's `/api/audience`: `POST /api/audience/vote` (`{ pollId, optionId }`) and `POST /api/audience/note` (`{ text, onAir }`, at most `noteMaxChars`) append a line with the time to `output/audience/votes.jsonl` and `notes.jsonl` (bodies over 8 KB refused). The dev server checks a vote is for a known poll and option, but not whether it's open (the channel can be watched at any moment with `?at=`). **At the launch** a small hosted service answers the same calls (docs/live.md, vision §8): only the address changes; it checks the poll is open against the real clock, and adds one vote per connection per poll (a scrambled IP) and a rate limit.

**One vote each:** a vote is remembered in this browser (`localStorage` `scorchball.votes`, by poll) only once the endpoint has taken it; a browser that has voted can't vote again. If sending fails the poll says "Couldn't send your vote. Try again." Where storage is blocked the vote still counts, it just isn't remembered.

### 23.4 Notes (Task 261)

**SEND A NOTE** opens the form rising from the button row over the pane and the pitch's corner (180 × 112, look C from Task 259): its title SEND A NOTE with ✕, a box ("A bug, an idea, anything!"), the line sent with it, then a count (0 / 500, `noteMaxChars`), CANCEL and SEND (dimmed until there's text). Sent, it shows **THANKS** ("Your note is with the League.", SEND ANOTHER). If sending fails: "Couldn't send. Try again." Esc, ✕ or CANCEL closes it; closing the pane or switching to Broadcast closes it too.

- **Typing never reaches the channel's keys:** the box keeps its key presses to itself, so M, V, B and the panes' keys (R, C, A, I; Task 277b) type letters instead of turning sound off, opening the schedule or a pane, or switching the layout.
- **The line sent with a note** (`audience.js` `onAirLine`), shown on the form as it will be sent and kept up to date while it's open: the season, the league's day and time, what's on air (the segment, with a re-air's part; the match's round and teams; while a match is played, its score, period and game clock, as the scorebug shows them) and the layout, then the instant, so the moment can be found again with `?at=`: `S2 WED 06:17 · LIVE R6 VAR 0-1 WRS 1H 16:06 · FULL · 2026-10-14T10:17:14Z`. It's worked out from the channel's clock and programme, not read off the screen (the scorebug's text is drawn on the sharp-text canvas); in a simultaneous final round it's the match the viewer is watching.
- **A wait between notes:** `noteCooldownSeconds` (60) from one browser, shown in the count's place (WAIT 42 S), remembered in `localStorage` (`scorchball.noteAt`), timed by the real clock. The hosted service adds its own rate limit at the launch.
- Notes go to `POST /api/audience/note` and on to `output/audience/notes.jsonl` (§23.3); read on the Director's desk from Task 264. They're never shown on air.

### 23.5 Donations: SUPPORT THE CHANNEL (Tom, 2026-10-18; vision §8; `donate.html`)

**On top of FEEDBACK** (above TELL THE LEAGUE), desktop and phone alike (Tom's pick B on `donate.html`): an amber-edged box with SUPPORT THE CHANNEL in amber, one line ("Scorchball TV is free and always on. Donations help keep the channel going."), and **DONATE ›**, an amber outlined button that fills amber on hover; the amber is the channel's own (`--accent`, the SCORCHBALL tag's; Tom, 2026-10-18: the first build's gold was jarring); then the pane's usual line. No icon (Tom: a cup is too common). DONATE is a plain link to `settings.audience.donateUrl` (`https://ko-fi.com/scorchballtv`) in a new tab (`target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener noreferrer"`). **The channel never handles money:** nothing is loaded from Ko-fi (no widget, script, frame or picture), so the content security policy is unchanged (it governs what a page loads, not where a link goes); `src/config.js` accepts only an https address, since it goes in an `href`. Nothing on air depends on giving, and it's never a sim input. Tested in `tests/donate.test.js`.

## 24. [It responds] Announcements on air (Task 265)

Mock-ups on `updates.html`; Tom's picks 2026-10-11. **What's announced** (vision §8): what changes the channel or how viewers take part: Tom's announcements (roadmap items and their run-up, new ways to take part: `kind: "announcement"` in `data/decisions.json`, a date, a headline, the words, perhaps a roadmap idea), each poll's opening and result (from `data/polls.json`), later perhaps legends inducted. **Upkeep is quiet:** the Director's rule changes are never announced (viewers never see the numbers); the Rulebook reads the season's own rules (`seasonConfig`), so it stays true after a quiet change. Worked out by `src/channel/announcements.js` (`announcementsAt`), only what has aired.

- **INFO's UPDATES tab** (look B, **the last tab**: ABOUT, GUIDE, UPDATES; Tom: rarer news, so it doesn't lead): the newest announcement in full (when and the season, a chip: THE LEAGUE, NEW POLL, POLL RESULT, HALL OF FAME; the words; a poll result's winner with its crest and share; VOTE IN AUDIENCE while a new poll is open), then EARLIER, older ones a line each (the headline, when, the chip) opening in the pane's place (‹ UPDATES). With none yet: "News from the League will appear here."
- **INFO's dot:** a small red dot on the "i" while there's an announcement this browser hasn't seen in UPDATES (the newest seen kept in `localStorage`, `scorchball.updatesSeen`; checked every 10 s, since one can air while the channel is open). While it shows, INFO opens on UPDATES; seeing UPDATES clears it.
- **The ticker** (wording A): each announcement leads the crawl for `ticker.announcementHours` (24) after it airs, its headline after an amber **LEAGUE** tag: "NEW POLL: WHO'S YOUR FAVOURITE TEAM? VOTE IN AUDIENCE", "POLL: WHO'S YOUR FAVOURITE TEAM? RIME, 64%", or Tom's own ("TWO NEW CLUBS JOIN IN SEASON 9").
- **No studio card** (Tom): cards air for a small share of the day; the ticker is constant and UPDATES keeps everything.

**Cards over the pitch ease in** (Task 278k, Tom's pick A on `cardfades.html`): the pre-match card, full time, the final round's cards and a re-air's fade in over `transitions.cardFadeMs` (320), settling up 4 px into place (a transform, since Broadcast shifts the card with translate), once any sweep covering them has passed; the pre-match card fades out as the walk-out begins. The others leave with the sweep into the studio, as before.

**Studio cards and the team card** (Task 278n, Tom): the team card's SQUAD has two views, WAGES (as before) and STATS (this season's numbers on a line under each name, across the card's width, each with its label: G, A and ST, stops, for outfielders; SV and SV% for keepers; Tom: in columns beside the names they were cramped). **Home-grown** (Tom's note 8, his pick C on `homegrown.html`, 2026-10-19, in the card's grey, nothing called out): under SQUAD, in the card's grey, how many are home-grown (from the team's own region; Tom: no mark beside the names, no key) "9 of 10 home-grown, from the Gullcoast"; the player card unchanged but for a lowercase "the" mid-sentence ("Age 27 · from the Gullcoast", as the data files write it); the Rulebook's LEAGUE tab says what it means. The HOME-GROWN tag left the draft and the moves board the same day. The combined SEASON LEADERS card (assists, saves, tackles, burns) is replaced by one card a big stat, TOP ASSISTS, TOP STOPS (tackles and blocks) and TOP KEEPERS (by saves, with the save %), drawn like TOP SCORERS (from 2026-10-18, Tom: no word beside the total, the heading says what it counts) and spread through the rotation (`segments.studioCards`). The playoff race card comes only once no team has more than `segments.raceFromGamesLeft` (5) regular-season games left, Friday on today's calendar; before, a team in the spotlight in its place.

**Celebrations that cheer** (Task 278l, Tom's pick A on `cheers.html`; `cheers.js`, `animation.cheers`): after a goal the scorer and the nearest teammate who can get there in time (half the celebration, at up to twice running speed) meet and cheer on a beat, arms up with a hop, high-fiving in the middle of it, then ease back so play resumes without a jump; who joins is chosen from where everyone was as the goal went in. At full time the winners cheer in their huddle and, after a home win, through the salute. (`?cheer=pair|group|none` on the dev server; group, every outfielder who can get there, was the other option.)

## 25. [MOMENTS] The ticket stubs (Milestone 5¾)

A viewer collects a **moment** for each kind of thing they watched live while the tab showed, kept in their browser, never a sim input (vision §8; `docs/tasks.md`, MOMENTS: the hand-off, the count, Tom's picks on `moments.html`). Passive: nothing to do but watch.

### 25.1 The moments (`data/moments.json`)

Thirteen kinds, one moment each, **levelling up through steps** (set from `npm run moments`, Tom 2026-10-12): IN A MATCH goals (1, 25, 50, 100, 250, 500), scorchers, burns, stops (every tackle and block seen, either team: STATS' blocks, steals and tackles; 1, 100, 500, 1,000, 2,500, 5,000; Tom, 2026-10-13), hat-tricks, early goals (EARLY GOALS from 2026-10-16, Tom: FIRST-MINUTE GOALS was cut off on its stub; a goal before 1:00 on the game clock, id `first_minute`; in place of late winners from 2026-10-16, Tom: about as common, and a found first-minute secret counts as the first), clean sheets (one per side), big wins (by 5 or more); ON AIR records broken on air, interviews, ad breaks; HABITS whole matches (kick-off to the whistle), days tuned in (any days). Nine **secrets** (ten until the first-minute goal became a kind), once and done, a ? until found: a whole 0–0, a last-minute winner, a whole match with 100 stops or more (about 1 in 60), a golden goal, every team in a day, a season's opening and its sign-off, seven days in a row, BOTH WAYS (a whole match in Full and one in Broadcast), SEEN IT ALL (every pane, tab and card in `places`).
