The big occasions (the final, the lift, the draft) are left out: they fall at the same time each week.

### 25.2 What counts (`src/channel/moments.js`, pure)

Every frame the channel tells the tracker the segment, the match on screen (the viewer's feed in the final round), the layout and whether the tab shows. **Live only:** re-airs and highlights earn nothing. **Watching** is the tab showing, sound on or off; a gap between frames over `moments.frameGapSeconds` (a hidden tab, a sleeping laptop) counts nothing that played in it and breaks a whole match. A whole match needs watching from within `startGraceSeconds` of kick-off. Results are known before they air, so a clean sheet, a big win, a late winner and a 0–0 are counted **at the final whistle**, never sooner (no spoilers); a late winner needs its goal seen. Interviews (the live post-match show) and ad breaks (bumper in, the ads, bumper out) count **when they end**, if watched from within `pieceGraceSeconds` of their start with no gap (Tom, after the first day: like a whole match, not as they start); the opening from the ident before a season's first match, the sign-off from the live night (not its repeats). SEEN IT ALL is read off the screen every `placesSeconds` (`main.js` `placesOpen`: the side tab, the pane's title and tab, a team card's tab, a player card, the levels pane, Broadcast); a tab renamed must be renamed in `places` too.

### 25.3 What's kept (`src/channel/momentStore.js`)

`localStorage` (`moments.storageKey`), as JSON: **what was seen, not just totals** (Tom: so two devices could be joined later without counting a goal twice): each moment by its key (a goal by its match and event, a day by its date), with the scoring team for goals and the teams, winner and layout for whole matches; when each step was reached; each secret with when and what was on air ("S2 WED 10:13 · BGA 1–0 PCG"); the places seen; the notices' switch. Every read and write guarded (a private window or blocked storage: kept for the visit only); saves gathered, at most one every 3 s, and made at once when the page hides. The browser is **not** asked to keep the site's data for good (`navigator.storage.persist`, removed by Tom on the first day live: Firefox asked viewers for permission on arrival, which looked suspicious, for a rare case). A different format version starts afresh. Counts by team (goals; whole matches and wins) come from what's kept.

### 25.4 The notice (`src/channel/overlays/momentNotice.js`)

A ticket stub **below the scorebug** in both layouts (Tom's pick A): the kind's icon on an amber stub (gold for a secret), a perforated edge, MOMENT (or SECRET FOUND) and the words on paper ("25 GOALS SEEN", "FIRST GOAL SEEN", "SEVEN IN A ROW"). It shoots in from the left and overshoots, flashes, a glint crosses the paper and its edge glows twice (Tom: a touch flashier, so it's noticed); it holds and fades up, `noticeSeconds` (5) in all. Several wait their turn, `noticeGapSeconds` apart; none starts over an ad break, the opening, a transition or while Broadcast's scorebug is aside. No sound (Tom). **A click opens the AUDIENCE pane on MOMENTS** (switching to Full first). **CLICK FOR SOUND** moved under the speaker, pointing up at it, following it as the scorebug widens for a re-air.

### 25.5 The pane (`src/channel/overlays/momentsView.js`, `audiencePane.js`)

AUDIENCE has two tabs (§23's pane was one): **MOMENTS** first, then **POLLS** (the pane as it was). **From 2026-10-18 (Tom):** FEEDBACK (was POLLS) first, then COINS while Fan Coins are on, then MOMENTS; the pane opens on FEEDBACK, and a notice clicked still opens MOMENTS. MOMENTS: "A moment is something you saw live: a goal, a burn, a hat-trick, an interview, a whole match. Each one levels up as you see more. Replays and highlights don't count: find the next live match marked in red in the SCHEDULE (V)." (Tom, after the first day: say what a moment is), then a **wall of stubs** two to a row by group (Tom's pick B): the kind's 9×9 icon (`momentIcons.js`; interviews a speech bubble) on an amber band, the step reached and the name on the paper, dimmed until seen; SECRETS · N OF 9, found ones in gold with their own icon. A kind opens its page in the tab's place (the tabs hidden, ‹ MOMENTS back): its stub saying the step reached ("25 GOALS SEEN", as its notice did), a bar to the next step with the count ("112 / 250"; Tom), every step with the date reached (one line each: the first without "SEEN"), and by team for goals and whole matches (seen and won). A found secret opens what it was, when, and what was on air; one not found stays shut. At the foot: **NOTICES ON / OFF**, then "**Currently, moments are kept in this browser.** They stay on this device, in this browser only. A private window doesn't keep them, clearing your browser's data erases them, and Safari forgets them after a week away." The wall redraws as moments arrive while it's open. **It keeps your place** (Tom, 2026-10-20): ‹ MOMENTS goes back to the wall as far down as it was, and leaving the tab (another tab, the pane closed) and coming back shows the page that was open as far down as it was; for this visit only (not kept in the browser); a notice clicked still opens the wall at the top.

INFO says so too (Tom): GUIDE's Audience line ("your moments, the League's polls, and a note to the League") and a **Moments** section after the side panel (what they are and that they level up, the ticket under the scorebug, secrets, only what's live while the channel is on screen, kept in this browser); ABOUT: "Your moments stay in your browser; nothing about them is sent anywhere."

### 25.6 Dev aids

On the dev server only: `?moments=reset` (this browser's moments start afresh), `?moments=demo` (a viewer about two weeks in). `npm run moments` counts each kind as aired over some seasons (§25.1's steps). `moments.html` keeps the mock-ups.

## 26. [The tour] A guided tour on a first visit (Milestone 5⅞)

Why (Tom, 2026-10-13): MOMENTS will be a big reason people stay early on, and it's easy never to know it's there; a first visit is when a viewer decides whether to come back. Mock-ups on `tour.html`; Tom's picks the same day. The words are data (`data/tour.json`).

- **The welcome** (`overlays/tourView.js`), on a first visit only, centred over the pitch with the match playing round it: WELCOME TO SCORCHBALL TV; "A made-up sport, played live around the clock. New here? Take a one-minute look around."; TAKE THE TOUR and NO THANKS; "Or find it all later in INFO, under GUIDE." with INFO's own icon. Taken, finished, closed or turned down (Esc too), it's remembered (`localStorage` `scorchball.tour`, `tour.js`) and never offered again; where storage is blocked it's offered each visit. Full only: in Broadcast it waits for Full.
- **Six steps, look B:** each lights one thing in amber, gently pulsing, the rest of the screen dimmed, an amber box beside it with the words, 1 / 6, ‹ BACK, NEXT › (DONE last) and ✕: the scorebug (LIVE, REPLAYS and HIGHLIGHTS, the heat meter, which acts as a shot clock); Full or Broadcast; the side panel (lit whole, its box beside it; any player or team opens a card); SCHEDULE (live matches in red); MOMENTS (an example ticket, 25 GOALS SEEN, lit under the scorebug; AUDIENCE); the close (INFO, under GUIDE). The box goes below what it points at, above it near the bottom, or beside something tall (`tour.js` `placeBox`), and follows it (the scorebug widens for a re-air).
- **While a step shows** the channel's keys wait (Esc closes the tour) and clicks go nowhere but the box; the match plays on underneath. Switching to Broadcast closes it.
- **INFO's GUIDE** opens with TAKE THE TOUR, for any time.
- **CLICK FOR SOUND on every load** (Tom: in place of a sound step), under the speaker, until sound is turned on (§25.4); before, it showed only after a reload of the channel's own.
- **Round 2** (Tom, 2026-10-17; mock-ups in `tour.html`'s second section, `tourPhoneSheet.js`): **FAN COINS**, a step after MOMENTS lighting AUDIENCE ("Watching earns Fan Coins, fastest in live matches and the off-season show. Spend them on chants, flags, the ticker and more, in AUDIENCE (A) under COINS."), only while `fanCoins.enabled` is on (`coins: true`; 7 steps then, 6 at the launch). **On a phone** (§28) its own fewer steps (`tour.json` `phone`, no keys named): THE SCOREBUG; THE BUTTONS (the right band lit: the side panel's views and the panes, a dotted name opening a card); MOMENTS (AUDIENCE in the left band, the example ticket); FAN COINS while they're on; THAT'S IT (INFO and BROADCAST lit: Broadcast swaps the panels for the stands and the crowd; all of it in INFO's GUIDE). Tom's pick A: the box over the picture beside what it points at (left of the right band, right of the left, below the scorebug; `tour.js` `placePhoneBox`), the rest dimmed, in the phone's own pixels (16 px words, 34–40 px buttons), over the whole page since the bands are outside the stage. Offered once on a first visit when the phone is sideways and in Full; hidden upright (TURN YOUR PHONE shows); TAKE THE TOUR in INFO's GUIDE on a phone too.
- With it: the AUDIENCE button's tooltip names moments ("Audience: your moments, polls, and notes to the League (A)"), and the Rulebook's LEAGUE tab opens "The Scorch League is 8 teams from 4 regions" (Tom).

## 27. [Phones] The phone readout (Milestone 5 15/16, step 1)

Why (Tom, 2026-10-13): on a phone the picture is less smooth and the sound pops and drops; measure before fixing, so the right thing gets fixed. `?perf=1` works on the live site too (`publicParams`): it changes nothing but a box over the picture.

- **The box** (`overlays/perfView.js`), bottom-left, redrawn twice a second: the browser, the screen (CSS pixels, the pixel ratio, the sharp text layer's size), what's on air and the layout; the sound (the context's state, rate, base and output latency; renders waiting; how long the crowd's takes took after sound on; Chrome's `playbackStats`, whose underruns are pops and drops); long tasks where the browser reports them (Chrome). Then NOW (the last 5 s) and SINCE START/RESET. **COPY** puts it all on the clipboard (or selects it); **RESET** starts SINCE afresh, one test at a time; a tap on its head folds it.
- **The numbers** (`perf.js`, pure): frames (average, frames a second, worst, how many over 33 ms; gaps over a second, a hidden tab, left out); drawing per frame and the browser's share (the frame's interval less the drawing: painting, compositing, garbage collection); drawing's parts, marked in `main.js`'s draw (league, match: the segment's picture and its sounds, panels, stands, scene, rest), and of which the sharp text (`meterSharp`) and building sounds (`audio.setMeter`); sounds, how late each match, ceremony and lift cue starts against when it was due (a slow frame starts its sounds late), over 50 ms counted; rendered sounds started late waiting on their render, and crowd reactions left out as too late; the worst frame while sounds were being rendered.
- **The sound switches** (Tom's first runs, 2026-10-13: Firefox's crowd popped, Chrome's didn't; `dev.js` `soundSwitches`, the live site too, a bad value ignored there, refused on the dev server; shown in the box's "switches"): `?latency=0.25` asks for an audio buffer that many seconds long (Firefox ignored "playback": 28 ms against Chrome's 240); `?crowdrate=48000` renders the crowd's takes at that rate (24000 today, resampled as they play); `?murmur=bed` plays the murmur as one seamless 12 s loop (`renderMurmurBed`: takes every 0.6–1.1 s as `main.js` plays them, the tails past the end folded onto the start) that only changes loudness with the crowd, in place of a new overlapping take every 0.6–1.1 s. `?ahead=0.1` starts every sound and voice that many seconds after it's asked for on the audio clock (`sound.ahead` in `createAudio`'s settings): Firefox seemed to apply a sound's start late, so a short one (a pass) lost its loud start.
- **The recordings** (Tom, 2026-10-13; vision §12): the switches showed Firefox ignoring a numeric latency too, and the chop following the background renders (more at `crowdrate=48000`, which doubled them); so every synthesized sound is recorded ahead and the channel only plays files. `src/channel/recordings.js` (pure): `recordingOf` maps a sound asked for to its recording (a sound varied by its seed plays one of `variants` takes picked by the seed, alike on every screen; the crowd's `crowdVariants`, a roar in `roarSizes` sizes, a kick at one of `kickLevels` strengths, a shot at the nearest of `shotPowers`; the music, jingles, effects, lottery and anthems one each, an anthem per length the walk-out and the lift play); `catalogue` lists all 336 (about 16 minutes, 14 MB: the music 3.1 MB, the anthems 3.1, the fire's stereo takes 1.8); `recipeHash` (numbers rounded to 8 figures, since Node and Chrome can differ in the last place). **Making them:** `recordings.html` (`recordSheet.js`) renders each from its recipe in the browser (an OfflineAudioContext, the same graph; the music folded to its loop length; scaled down to fit if it peaks over full scale, the index's `gain` bringing it back) and posts a WAV to the dev server (`POST /api/recordings`), which compresses it with ffmpeg-static to `assets/sounds/<key>.<hash>.mp3` (to a file, so the header telling a decoder the encoder's silence is written) and writes `assets/sounds/index.json`; RECORD MISSING AND STALE after a recipe changes, ▶ LIVE and ▶ FILE to compare. **Playing them** (`audio.js` `playFile`): loaded and decoded once (the crowd's and the play's as sound is switched on, the rest as first played), joining part-way if one isn't loaded yet; the music one looping source that later passes leave alone; a decoder that left the encoder's whole delay at the start has it trimmed (the readout counts these; Chrome decodes to the recorded length, a few samples over at the end in 14 files); `settings.sound.recordings.enabled` or `?recordings=off` builds every sound as before. The host keeps the files a year (their names change when re-recorded), the index checked each load. Tests (`tests/recordings.test.js`): every recording there and fresh, nothing left over; every sound a season's matches, ceremonies, the lift and the programme ask for has one.
- **Sound on time with the picture** (Tom, 2026-10-14, on Chrome on his phone: a kick heard as the pass arrived): every sound left the speaker the device's delay after it was started (Chrome on a phone keeps about 250 ms of sound ready, a desktop about 50) plus `sound.ahead`. Now the play's sounds (a match's, the walk-out's and handshakes', the lift's) are started that much early (`audio.lead`, looked at every 5 s and changed only by more than 20 ms; `main.js` `leadMs`, `playDue`), each window of cues reaching ahead of the picture by the lead, and after a jump the cues within it started too (`playComing`); the commentary stays on the picture's own time (queued anyway). The readout shows the lead; "late" is now how late a sound is heard. **Firefox on Android dropped for now** (Tom, 2026-10-14): with the recordings it builds nothing (built here 0, trimmed 0) and plays the same files, yet its 28 ms output still chops the music and plays the ball's sounds softly; nothing on our side changes its buffer.

## 28. [Phones] The channel on a phone (Milestone 5 15/16, step 4; Task 278c)

Tom's picks on `phone.html` (2026-10-14): today's Full can't exist on a phone as it is, its controls too small; every control in the phone's own style, in the black bands either side of the 16:9 picture (B, the corners), none over the picture.

- **A phone** (`phone.js` `isPhone`): a touch screen (a coarse pointer) whose short side is at most 500 CSS px; `?phone=1` or `?phone=0` either way (the live site too). `body.phone`; desktops and the TV unchanged.
- **Opens in Full** as on the desktop (Tom: the information is there, and a phone tour would start from it).
- **Hidden on a phone:** the scorebug's layout and sound buttons, the side panel's tabs, the row of panes, CLICK FOR SOUND, the desktop's levels strip. The side panel and the panes run from the top down to the ticker.
- **The bands** (`phone.js` `phoneBands`, `overlays/phoneControls.js`): left, SOUND (pulsing until on), LEVELS, in Full INFO and AUDIENCE (the least immersive, moved from the right to make room), then FULL SCREEN and the layout (BROADCAST or FULL) at the bottom, where the desktop has them by the scorebug; right, in Full only, STATS, TABLE, TOP and GAMES (the side panel's views) and SCHEDULE, RULEBOOK, CHRONICLE. Broadcast has sound and the layout only. Buttons as tall as fit (`buttonHeight`: 37 px for seven in Chrome sideways, 272 px tall). Each passes its tap to the channel's own control and mirrors its state (lit: a pane open, LEVELS open; amber: the side panel's view, sound on), so everything works as on the desktop; a view tapped closes an open pane.
- **Panes 86 px** in the desktop's place (Tom, to try first). **LEVELS** opens the four switches (sound, crowd, commentary, studio), 86 px, under the scorebug beside its button.
- **FULL SCREEN** fills the phone and holds it sideways where it can (`requestFullscreen`, `screen.orientation.lock`); EXIT FULL then.
- **Upright:** a TURN YOUR PHONE card over everything, with FULL SCREEN (which turns it).
- **The tour** has the phone's own steps (§26, round 2).
- **After Tom's look on his phone** (2026-10-14): 86 px fine at full screen. **The panes' ✕ gone** (a pane closes from its own button); **their tabs stacked** (Tom's pick A on `panetabs.html`): a full-width row each, 13 stage px tall, 3 apart. **In full screen in Broadcast the bands fade** after 3 s without a touch (`FADE_MS`; not with LEVELS open), any touch bringing them back (a tap then only does that). **SOUND pulses only until sound is first on** (Tom: muting on purpose shouldn't bring it back; it asks again on the next load, as CLICK FOR SOUND does).
- **The side column's nine as one set** (Tom, 2026-10-14: on a phone they all look alike, so they behave alike): the side panel's four views and the five panes (INFO and AUDIENCE on the left too) each light fully amber when chosen, exactly one at a time (`phoneControls.js` `chosen`, `choose`); the views get the panes' amber heading (`.phone-side-head`; the side panel below it); and they're sticky: tapping the one showing does nothing (a pane no longer closes from its button), another switches to it. **FULL SCREEN and EXIT FULL SCREEN** (Tom's pick a: EXIT FULL beside FULL was confusing; two lines where long). **Cards:** a name that opens one is dotted-underlined on a phone (no hover there), and a card's back is a wide amber-edged button saying where it goes: ‹ BACK TO TOP (and AUDIENCE's, MOMENTS' and UPDATES' backs).
- **The nudge to turn sound on gives up** (Tom, 2026-10-14; desktop and phone): CLICK FOR SOUND and the phone's pulsing SOUND go after `sound.promptSeconds` (10) of the channel showing without sound, the count held while the tour is open or the tab hidden (`main.js` `nudging`); sound still turns on from the speaker or the button.
- **Add to Home Screen:** `manifest.webmanifest` (Scorchball TV, full screen, sideways, the icon at 192 and 512 px from `npm run icon`).

## 29. [Fan Coins] Earned by watching, spent on the channel (version 1, a dress-rehearsal test)

See vision §8 and `docs/tasks.md` (Fan Coins: the hand-off, the count, Tom's picks on `fancoins.html`). **Version 2 at the launch** (Tom, 2026-10-17; `docs/tasks.md`, Fan Coins version 2): shared spends, earning counted by the audience service; this section is version 1 until it's built. **Version 1** is a test during the dress rehearsal only, behind `settings.fanCoins.enabled` (off at the launch, Task 279). It's kept in this browser, every spend is seen and heard by the spender alone, and nothing is sent. Never a sim input.

### 29.1 Earning (`src/channel/fanCoins.js`, pure)
Coins are earned only while the tab shows. A gap between frames longer than `frameGapSeconds` counts nothing and breaks a piece being watched whole.
- **Live play:** a coin every `liveMinutes` (1), half-time included.
- **The off-season show:** a coin every `offseasonMinutes` (1) as it airs: the trophy lift, the champions, the awards, the farewells, the lottery, the draft, the moves. Its repeats through the night count as replays.
- **Highlights:** a coin for each package watched whole, from within `graceSeconds` of its start.
- **Everything else:** a coin every `studioMinutes` (5): re-airs (and the off-season show's repeats), the build-up, the handshakes, the full-time card, the desk, the interview, studio cards, ads, the ident and the countdown.

Tom set these on 2026-10-16. Before that, re-airs earned a coin a half watched whole (which felt low) and ads, the ident and the countdown earned nothing.

At most `dailyCap` (100) a day, counted from midnight in the viewer's own time; at the cap nothing carries over. `npm run coins` sized it: about 18 an hour, 57 in three hours, 463 all day before the cap (13, 42 and 350 under the first rates).

### 29.2 Spending
Prices are in `settings.fanCoins.prices`: ticker 10, fan shout 5, chant 20, flag 20 (30 until 2026-10-17, Tom: the same as a chant), back screen 20 (50 until Tom's notes: the desk plays only briefly and is the least watched). The COINS tab (2026-10-17, Tom): the balance, the day's bar, then how they're earned ("Watching Scorchball TV earns Fan Coins…"), then the spends with no SPEND heading, and "A test for now…" at the foot. A spend shows its wait first, then whether there are enough coins.
- **A ticker message**, once every `tickerMinutes` (10). It joins the tape at the end of the line scrolling in, never restarting it (Tom, 2026-10-16: it used to start the tape over from the right): straight after the last item if that end is still off screen, or the next pass from there (the tape's second copy is always the items as they are now, so new items come in from the right, never popping in on screen); first on the tape for that long, after a FANS tag in the team's colours for GO <TEAM>! or gold for a general one (`crawl.js`).
- **A fan shout** (Tom's notes, task 3), once every `shoutMinutes` (2), in a live match only: the viewer's words pop up over their end's fans in a small bubble for `shoutSeconds` (30; Tom: 8 was too short, and it's shown once), as soon as Broadcast's slide is done (SPEND switches to it) or after a goal's celebration if one's on; one shout per end at a time (version 2's shared shouts queue per team). Words from `data/fanCoins.json` shouts: GO <TEAM>!, LET'S GO!, COME ON!, GET A GOAL!, I ♥ <the team's top scorer this season, from matches already over on screen> (a pixel heart). The home end's bubble is low in its side stand; the away end's is in the stand above the pitch over the away corner, its tail out of its right-hand side (Tom, 2026-10-16; was at its bottom right), pointing on to the corner; both the same width (Tom, from `fancoins.html` section 5). Shouted (Tom, 2026-10-16): after it pops up, a shake in pixel steps every 1.1 s (a pixel left, up and right, up, then still; `fc-shout` in `index.html`). It doesn't count as the end's chant or flag. SPEND switches to Broadcast.
- **A chant**, once a half, in a live match only (Tom; not at half-time), lasting the rest of that half. The viewer picks the chant and the end; SPEND switches to Broadcast. Every `chantEverySeconds` (45; Tom: any oftener grates) the chant is sung:
  - The bubble shows over its end in Broadcast, with the line being sung picked out.
  - The end waves its arms (the anthems' pose) through the sung syllables, and moves as usual through claps and breaths, unless the crowd already has a pose of its own on.
  - No round starts if the half's end would cut it off, and none runs into a goal's celebration: one that would starts when the celebration ends (Tom).
  - Seen only once Broadcast's slide is complete (the bubble, the waving), so switching back and forth mid-chant never shows it half-way.
- **A flag**, once a half, in a live match only, held up by the fans in the side's end (no pole) until the half ends. It ripples and is lifted now and then (`fanArt.js` drawHeldFlag), is lowered through a goal's celebration and held up again after, and shows only once Broadcast's slide is complete. SPEND switches to Broadcast.
- **FAN BOARD** (Tom, 2026-10-21; the back screen until then, its kind still `screen` inside): the band laid flat in Full's strip beside the scorebug, in the ad still's place, in any picture, for `screenMinutes` (10), one board after another (`fanArt.js` drawFanBoard, `strips.js`; Tom's pick A on `fanboard.html`). Never in the final round (the strip picks the match then; the service starts a board after it, `coinRules.js` boardBlocked); in the playoffs only the teams still in it (`fanCoins.js` boardTeams). 30 coins; COINS: 10 MIN BESIDE SCOREBUG. The stills' clock carries on under a board. Until then:
- **The back screen** (until 2026-10-21), once every `screenMinutes` (30): a band across the middle of the studio's big screen through the desk's next talk (`fanArt.js` drawScreenBand, `sharpText.js` sharpBent).
  - The desk talks for only 15–30 s at a time between matches, so a spend waits for the next talk.
  - The light bars play on around the band, and the band bends with the screen.
  - A team's band is in its colours with its crest embossed at each end; a general one is dark with gold edges. Its words go on the sharp layer.

**One chant or flag per end at a time** (Tom, with version 2's queue in mind): an end with one going shows BUSY on the spend's page, and the other end can still have one.

The back screen's pictures and the ticker's messages come from one list: `data/fanCoins.json` pictures, then `teamWords` for each team.

### 29.3 The chants' sound
The chants are sung by the crowd's own voices (Tom's pick: no paid voices). The chant recipe (`soundRecipes.js`) sings a tune from `data/fanCoins.json` a syllable at a time (vowel, note, length; or shouted, short and breathy), with claps and breaths; a chant without a tune is the olé, as before. Each tune is recorded ahead like the crowd's other sounds (`recordings.js`: `chant/<tune>-v<n>`, `crowdVariants` takes). After changing a tune, record it again on `recordings.html`.

### 29.4 On screen
**The COINS tab** (`overlays/coinsView.js`) sits in AUDIENCE between FEEDBACK and MOMENTS (between MOMENTS and FEEDBACK until 2026-10-18), with the balance on the tab (Tom: not on the Audience button, which opens more than coins). It shows:
- the balance
- today against the limit ("TODAY 34 OF 100"; at the limit, "100 OF 100: AT THE DAILY LIMIT" and "RESETS IN 05:12")
- each spend on a line, with its price and SPEND or why not ("IN 6 MIN", "NEXT HALF", "IN A MATCH", "WAITING FOR THE DESK", "12 MORE")
- how coins are earned in plain words (the order, not the rates), and that they're kept in this browser

Each spend says where it shows, when and for how long (Tom): on the list ("10 MIN ON THE TICKER", "LIVE · TO HALF-TIME", "AT THE DESK · 06:35"); on its page, in full, with the desk's next talk worked out along the programme and, outside a live match, the next one's kick-off, as SCHEDULE shows them; and on the plate once spent ("UP NOW, TILL HALF-TIME"). A spend opens in the tab's place: what it does, the choices, SPEND, then the plate.

**The +1** (`overlays/fanOverlays.js`): +N and a coin rise from the Audience button (the phone's or the side column's) as coins come in; none in Broadcast (Tom). The Audience button's icon is one silhouette (Tom, 2026-10-15: two looked odd).

### 29.5 What's kept, and dev aids
Fan Coins are kept in `localStorage` (`fanCoins.storageKey`) through `momentStore.js`, with Fan Coins' own data: the balance, today's count and its date, the time carried towards the next coin, the last ticker message and back screen, the halves spent in, and the spends still going. On the dev server, `?coins=reset` starts afresh and `?coins=rich` gives 500 to spend.

### 29.6 Version 2: shared, banked with the service (Tom, 2026-10-17; at the launch)
Version 2 comes at the launch (vision §8; `docs/tasks.md`, Fan Coins version 2). Everyone watching sees and hears every spend, and coins are banked with the audience service, so they can't be made up in the browser. The drawing, the sounds and the spends' rules above carry over. The rules the service keeps are in `src/channel/coinRules.js`, which is pure so the worker, the dev server and the tests share it. The service code is `worker/coins.js`, routed from `audience.js` under `/api/audience/coins/`, with the tables in `worker/migrations/0003_coins.sql`. The numbers are `settings.fanCoins.shared`.

- **A viewer** is a random id the browser keeps (no accounts; clearing the browser loses the coins, as with MOMENTS). **A household** is a salted hash of the connection, as for votes (an IPv6 address by its /64).
- **Earning** keeps version 1's rates. The channel counts by them and checks in every `checkInSeconds` (60) and on closing (`POST coins/checkin`: the viewer, the coins counted since the last, the viewer's own date). The service banks at most a coin a minute: the wallet's `credited` moment moves a minute on for each coin, a new wallet starts a minute back, and at most `bankMinutes` (60) of watching can be banked at once. Each household is held to `dailyCap` (100) on the viewer's day, however many browsers it opens. The answer carries the balance, today's count, the cap and the viewer's own spends still waiting or showing.
- **Spending** (`POST coins/spend`): the kind, the choice, and in a match the end, the match (`s<season>-<slot>`, checked against the published calendar: from its kick-off to `matchWindowMinutes`, 45, after) and its halves' times as the channel has them (checked to fall inside that window). A viewer has at most one spend of a kind waiting or showing. The coins are taken only from enough, then the slot; two spends taking the same slot at the same moment can't both have it (the second is refunded and asked to try again).
- **The queues** (Tom: several may queue, each shown its place and when it starts): the ticker, one message after another for `tickerMinutes` each; the back screen, one every `screenMinutes`, which the channel shows at the desk's first talk from its start (kept in the feed `screenKeepHours`); in a match, each end's shouts one after another for `shoutSeconds`, none past the match; and each end's chants and flags together, a half each, so at most the halves still to come (none during half-time but the second). At most `queueMax` (5) wait in a queue besides the one showing (QUEUE FULL). A spend that can't happen in time is turned away before any coins are taken (NO ROOM THIS MATCH). The answer gives the start, the end and how many are ahead of it, the one showing included.
- **The feed** (`GET coins/feed`): every spend still waiting or showing, the same for everyone and naming no viewer, kept at Cloudflare's edge `feedCacheSeconds` (15). The channel reads it every `feedSeconds` (30). A shout naming a player carries the player's id, and each channel draws the name.
- **Kept, and shown again on re-airs** (built 2026-10-18, Tom: he'd expected them on a semifinal's re-air): every spend stays with its match and its times. A re-air asks the service once for its match's chants, flags and shouts (`GET coins/match/<s2-13>`: none until the match's `matchWindowMinutes` are over, then kept at Cloudflare's edge `replayCacheHours`, 24, since they never change; `coinBank` `replayOf`, asked from the re-air's intro). They show at the same moment of the match as they did live (`fanCoins.js` `matchShown`: a re-air's moment is the live match's kick-off plus its `elapsed`, so the shortened half-time doesn't shift them): the bubbles, the waving end, the chant's sound (the same take), the held flag, the shout, in Broadcast as live. Only full re-airs, not highlights; nothing is earned or spent by them. Checked on the dev server: a chant, a shout and a second-half flag in a re-air of s1-47. **Live:** after the push, `npm.cmd run worker` (until then, re-airs show none).
- **Calls given up** (2026-10-18, Tom: a chant said NOT ENOUGH with 20 showing): the channel gives up on any call to the service after `requestSeconds` (15). A check-in that never answered had left the bank waiting on it for good: nothing more was banked from 19:46 that night while the balance shown kept adding what was earned, so the service had 2 where the channel showed 20. A spend now waits for a check-in under way rather than skipping it (`coinBank.js` `call`, `checkIn`).
- **A shout waits only for a celebration that's on** (2026-10-18, Tom: away shouts late or missing in the final, 14 goals in 25 minutes): it used to wait for a clear 30 s, so in a match of many goals it was pushed back celebration after celebration, past its slot at the service, and the feed then dropped it unseen. Now it pops up at once, or as the celebration on then ends (`fanCoins.js` `afterCelebration`), and a shout still on is kept from what was shown once the feed drops it (`spendsFromFeed`'s `now`). The home end was no different: those happened to have a clear 30 s.
- **The dev server** answers `/api/audience/coins/*` with the service's own code over a local SQLite database, `output/audience/coins.db` (`src/cli/localD1.js`, which applies the service's migrations; the tests use it in memory).
- **Live:** run the migration on the live database once (`npx wrangler d1 migrations apply scorchball-audience --remote --config worker/wrangler.jsonc`, Tom's login), then `npm.cmd run worker`. The service reads `data/fanCoins.json` from the published site too.
- **The channel** (step 2a, 2026-10-17; `src/channel/coinBank.js`, `fanCoins.js` version 2's functions): the browser keeps a viewer id and what it earned but hasn't banked (`pending`; version 1's data starts afresh, Tom). The earner counts as before and the balance shows a coin at once; `coinBank` checks in every `checkInSeconds` and as the page goes (`keepalive`), the service's answer setting the balance and today (the household's) plus what's still pending. It reads the feed every `feedSeconds`; `spendsFromFeed` turns its rows into the spends `showing()` already draws (each match's tied to it by season and slot, a shout's words made for its end and its player, held past a goal's celebration; the back screen keeping the talk it claimed), and a spend made here shows at once, before the feed (kept at the edge) has it. COINS' lines come from `queueState`: `QUEUE: 2 AHEAD · IN 09:29`, `NOW · AWAY END FREE`, `YOURS IS QUEUED · IN 09:21`, `YOURS IS ON`, QUEUE FULL, NO ROOM THIS MATCH, IN A LIVE MATCH, N MORE; an end is busy only with no room left this match. SPEND waits for the service (SENDING…), then QUEUED: IT STARTS IN 09:23 or the plate as before, or why not. Nothing switches to Broadcast by itself (Tom). The foot: "Shared: everyone watching sees and hears what's spent, in turn…". `?coins=rich` asks the dev server for 500 (`POST coins/dev-rich`, the dev server only).
- **One's own spend** (step 2b, 2026-10-17; Tom's picks on `coinqueue.html`): its line says `YOURS: #2 · IN 01:06` or YOURS IS ON. Its page, in place of the choices and SPEND: queued, THE QUEUE's slots (NOW, lit when one's showing; those waiting ahead; YOU in gold; the rest free, `queueMax` in all), `#2 IN THE QUEUE` and STARTS IN with the countdown, nothing more (Tom); on now, YOUR CHANT IS ON (YOUR MESSAGE IS ON, YOUR FLAG IS UP, YOUR SHOUT IS UP, YOUR PICTURE IS ON ITS WAY) with what it's doing (ON THE TICKER NOW, FOR 10 MIN), and for one in the stands seen in Full, **WATCH IN BROADCAST**. A queued spend never switches by itself. **One that starts at once** (no queue) in the stands does switch to Broadcast (Tom, 2026-10-18: by hand the first round was missed), and for the spender it starts `broadcast.slideMs` plus `afterSlideSeconds` (0.5) later than the service's start (`coinBank` `delay`, `spendsFromFeed` `delayOf`), so its first chant, the flag going up or the shout popping is seen once the slide is done. **The bar** (pick b, unlike MOMENTS' ticket; `fanOverlays.js` `onBar`): when one's own spend starts, under the scorebug for `noticeSeconds` (12), in Full with AUDIENCE closed, below a MOMENTS ticket if one shows; BROADCAST › on one in the stands, a tap switching. Checked on the dev server: a ticker queued behind another (NOW, YOU, the countdown), the bar as it started; in a live match a chant spent, on at once, WATCH IN BROADCAST switching, its bubble over the home end from the feed.

## 30. [The big matches promoted] The final round, the semifinals and the final (Tom, 2026-10-17)

Tom (2026-10-17, as the rehearsal neared its first final round): these matches looked like any other on the channel. Mock-ups on `bigmatches.html` (`bigMatchesSheet.js`); Tom's picks **C, the poster**, and the ticker's wording **1**.

- **When:** in every gap whose next kick-off is the final round, a semifinal or the final (`cards.js` `promotedAt`): from the show after the last match before it (on today's calendar about 17:55 on day 6 for the final round), the semifinals' from the final round's show to the second semifinal, the final's from the second semifinal's show.
- **The promo card** (`cards.js` `promoCard`, drawn in `cardPanel.js`, `.promo` in `index.html`): the first card of every studio block in those gaps, the rotation carrying on after it (the promo and three), and every `cardsPerBlock` cards in a longer block (the show's last) (`programme.js`, `card · promo` in `npm run rundown`). Look C: a fire-red column with the event stacked large (THE FINAL ROUND, THE SEMIFINALS, THE FINAL), the day and time (TONIGHT 20:00, TOMORROW 14:00) and a countdown ticking as the pre-match card's; beside it what's known on screen: the final round's four matches in the channel's order with places and who's in the Pennant and playoff races; the semifinals (first against fourth, second against third) with this season's meetings and a player to watch a side (the regular season's top scorer), then the first one's result and who's through; the final's two crests face to face with places and points, this season's meetings, how each won its semifinal and a player to watch a side. Past its event (a dev jump) it gives way to the latest results.
- **The ticker** (`ticker.js`, `crawl.js`): once nothing is live before them, the big matches lead the crawl after a fiery tag (`.tick-big`), in gold, in place of their ordinary upcoming lines: FINAL ROUND · TONIGHT 20:00 · ALL AT ONCE · PCG v LIS · …; SEMIFINAL 1 · BGA v LIS · TOMORROW 14:00 and SEMIFINAL 2; THE FINAL · BGA v SKA · TONIGHT 20:00. While a match is live before them (the final round, the first semifinal), none: the semifinals' teams still come only once the final round has finished on screen.
- **Tests:** `tests/bigMatches.test.js` (the blocks, the card's contents at four moments, the ticker).

### 30.1 The PLAYOFF PREVIEW show (Tom, 2026-10-17; mock-ups on `preview.html`, Tom's picks)

The 17½ hours between the final round and the semifinals were about 90% re-airs. Now a show of about 11½ minutes (`settings.preview`) airs right after the final round's show, then again before a re-air once `everyMinutes` (150) have passed since the last began, none starting after `lastStart` (12:00) on the semifinals' day: on today's calendar at about 20:37, 23:26, 02:14, 05:03, 07:50 and 10:39 (`programme.js` `previewPieces`, `npm run rundown -- --day 6 --all`, `preview · …`). Its segments carry `preview: true`; the re-airs keep the rest.

- **The opening** (`openingSeconds`): the poster as the promo card has it, PLAYOFF / PREVIEW in the red column, SEASON N, and ON THE SHOW beside it: the season in review and the two semifinals with their times.
- **The desk**: the standings clip (after the regular season, the final table; its existing lines, sized at each airing).
- **THE TABLE'S STORY** (`cardSeconds` each card): a bump chart, a line per team in its colours (secondary under primary) through its place after each round (the table on screen before the next round's first kick-off), the cut dashed; each line ends level with its team's swatch, abbreviation and points, drawn in the chart (Tom); ROUND 1 TO N and the Pennant at the foot.
- **GOALS OF THE SEASON**, a reel (`highlights.js` `seasonReelClips`): the regular season's scorchers, then its other goals, each spread through the season, `seasonReelClips` (18) in all; named in the highlights' own sting as the playoffs' reels are (Tom), with SEASON N under it.
- **BY THE NUMBERS**: `numberTiles` (6; up to 9 fit, smaller) tiles, a big number, what and who, filled in order from what the season had (`previewCards.js` `numberTiles`): the most goals, the Pennant's points, records (none in a first season), the longest winning run, the most goals in a game, the most scorchers, saves, assists, the biggest win, the most goals in a match, the longest run without a win, the most tackles, the season's goals in all.
- **For each semifinal** (1st v 4th, 2nd v 3rd by the table on screen): **TALE OF THE TAPE**, the two sides' numbers mirrored either side of the label (place, points, W-D-L, goals for and against, scorchers, saves, home and away), the better lit, this season's meetings at the foot; **the meetings reel**, every goal from their regular-season meetings (`meetingReelClips` at most), named ANVILS v VINTNERS over THIS SEASON; **PLAYERS TO WATCH**, two halves under the crests, each side's top scorer (goals big, scorchers) and its keeper (save % big, saves), spaced as four players (Tom).
- **The SEMIFINALS promo card** (§30), then back to the re-airs.
- **The playoff analyst** (§30.2) talks after THE TABLE'S STORY and before each TALE OF THE TAPE.
- **SCHEDULE** (Tom): each airing one PLAYOFF PREVIEW line, its time in gold as a live match's is in red and its words studio grey (Tom) (`guide.js`, entry kind `preview`), not STUDIO and HL lines; the studio jingle plays as it begins, not between its pieces (`studioJingle`).
- **Not yet:** no new voice lines (Tom: graphics first).
- **Tests:** `tests/bigMatches.test.js` (when it airs and its order, the cards' contents).

### 30.2 The playoff analyst (Tom, 2026-10-17; `analyst.html`, `data/analyst.json`, `src/channel/analystTalk.js`)

A third person at the desk who only does the playoffs: **Radomir Brodek** (Krenn until 2026-10-18, Tom: Krenn is in the Kettle's surname pool; Brodek in none, id `brodek`, his clips in `assets/voices/desk/brodek/`), a veteran coach from the Kettle, grey and gruff (Tom's pick of three), voiced by Did Vishchun (an ElevenLabs Library voice, Tom's pick by ear of six), in Hedrek's seat on the left; **Ysolde hosts** from hers on the right, in her own voice (Tom). Three talks in the PLAYOFF PREVIEW show (the topic `analyst`, `seg.bit`; each sized to its lines, `preview.analystSeconds` at most; the show about 12¾ minutes):
- **THE FOUR**, after THE TABLE'S STORY (about 30 s): Ysolde brings him in (his name on the graphic), his hello, the top seed's case, "And the other three?", a case for each of the others in table order, the first that's true of it, each kind once: the most goals of the four, the fewest conceded, four or five wins in the last five, fourth within a win of fifth, one win or none, the best home record; else steady by its place.
- **Each semifinal**, before its TALE OF THE TAPE (about 20 s): Ysolde introduces it (SEMIFINAL 1 · BGA v LIS · SUN 14:00), favourites (the better seed by more than a win's points) or too close to call, then an upset (the lower seed in better form) or else how their meetings went (won both, or shared), "So who decides it?", the league's top scorer or the side's to stop, the keeper if clearly better (5 points of save % or more), a close.
- **Said only when true**, players and numbers on the graphic under the speaker, never spoken; a line names at most one team (one clip per team), none says "{team}'s". The lines are data (`data/analyst.json`, validated in `src/config.js`): each kind with who says it, its gesture, its fact ('when'), its ways (say and show), dealt as the desk's (`studio.js` `sayer`). A said line carries its seat (`Said.who`; `timeLines` otherwise alternates), and `lineTiming` takes the analyst's pair to time them by their clips; `main.js` `seatsOf` draws his figure in the left seat and plays his clips (`assets/voices/desk/krenn/`) and Ysolde's (`desk/ysolde/host…`).
- **Voiced** (`npm run voices -- --analyst [--count] --paid`): 243 clips, about 10,900 characters, 2026-10-17. **Tests:** every line over four leagues has its clip, from the right seat (`tests/bigMatches.test.js`). Renamed Brodek (2026-10-18): only Ysolde's two `hostIntro` clips say his name, re-voiced with `npm run voices -- --analyst --redo hostIntro --paid` (`--redo <pattern>` remakes clips whose path matches though they exist).

### 30.3 The FINALS PREVIEW show (Tom, 2026-10-18; mock-ups on `finalspreview.html`, Tom's picks)

PLAYOFF PREVIEW's idea for the final, in the gap between the second semifinal and the final (about 2½ hours on today's calendar), graphics first, then the analyst (Tom, the same day). About 9½ minutes, right after the second semifinal's show, then again before a re-air once `preview.finalsEveryMinutes` (60; 80 until 2026-10-18, Tom: about hourly) have passed since the last began, if it ends before the final's run-in: on today's calendar at about 17:37, 18:40 and 19:43. **Between the second semifinal and the final, the re-airs and highlights are the two semifinals alone** (Tom, 2026-10-18: no older regular-season matches), newest first and cycled, FINALS PREVIEW between them (`programme.js` `finalsPieces`, `npm run rundown -- --day 7 --all`, `finals preview · …`). Its segments carry `preview` and `finals`; SCHEDULE lists each airing as one FINALS PREVIEW line (`guide.js`, the entry's `title`), its time in gold as PLAYOFF PREVIEW's, the studio jingle as it begins.

- **The opening** (`finals_open`; A, the poster): FINALS / PREVIEW in the red column, SEASON N; ON THE SHOW beside it, THE ROAD TO THE FINAL and THE FINAL with its teams and time.
- **The desk**: the playoffs clip (the final set; its existing lines, sized at each airing).
- **THE ROAD TO THE FINAL** (`road`; B, the bracket): both semifinals in boxes, each side's place and goals, the two through lit (a golden goal or a shootout named), into THE FINAL's gold box with its time.
- **The analyst on the roads** (`analystTalk.js` bit `road`, about 20 s): Ysolde brings Brodek in (her `hostIntro`), his hello (`analystHelloFinal`), then each finalist's road, the better seed first, the first that's true: through on a shootout, a golden goal, beat a higher seed, won by three or more, else made to work for it (`roadShootout` … `roadNarrow`), Ysolde's "And the other finalists?" between; a road said for both is dealt its other way the second time (Tom).
- **THE SEMIFINALS**, a reel of both semifinals' goals (`preview.reels.semis`), in the highlights' own sting, SEASON N under it.
- **The analyst on the final** (bit `final`, about 25 s), before its tape: Ysolde introduces it (`hostFinal`, THE FINAL · BGA v SKA · SUN 20:00), then a semifinal bit's lines, none of which names a semifinal: favourites or too close, an upset or how their meetings went, who decides it, the keeper if clearly better, a close.
- **Voiced** 2026-10-18 (`npm run voices -- --analyst --paid`): 86 clips, about 3,562 characters (about 1,532 credits).
- **TALE OF THE TAPE · THE FINAL** (`tape_final`; A, PLAYOFF PREVIEW's sheet): the regular season's numbers, then each side's semifinal ("3-2 v LIS") in gold as the last row; this season's meetings at the foot. Then the finalists' meetings reel (`meetingReelClips` at most), named by its two teams over THIS SEASON.
- **PLAYERS TO WATCH · THE FINAL** (`watch_final`; A, PLAYOFF PREVIEW's halves): each side's top scorer and keeper as there, each with what they did in the semifinal ("2 IN THE SEMIFINAL", "5 SAVES IN THE SEMIFINAL") as a last line, so every player's block is alike and the space between a side's two players the same (Tom).
- **THE FINAL promo card**, then back to the re-airs.
- **The cards** (`previewCards.js` `finalsCard`) show only once both semifinals have finished on screen and until the final kicks off (past it, the latest results); the finalists the better seed at home, as the sim has them.
- **Tests:** `tests/bigMatches.test.js` (when it airs and its order, SCHEDULE's lines, the cards' contents).

## 31. [A new non-match segment] The light by the time of day (Tom's note 14, 2026-10-19)

Picked on `groundstour.html` (the grounds tour's mock-ups, three rounds): the ground is lit by the hour on the league's clock (`live.timeZone`). Live matches, ceremonies and the grounds tour take the hour now; **re-airs and highlights take the hour the match was played** (its kick-off and how far in; Tom, 2026-10-19: a night match stays a night match; `daylight.js` `litMoment`). Presentation only: results never change.
- **When it was played, in STATS** (Tom, 2026-10-19): under REPLAY or HIGHLIGHTS at the head of the STATS tab, a muted PLAYED TUE 00:39 (the kick-off), since a replay keeps the light of its kick-off (`sidePanel.js` `statsView` `played`).
- **The changes on the clock** (as SCHEDULE shows it): night to dawn 04:45–05:15, dawn to day 06:45–07:15, day to dusk 16:45–17:15, dusk to night 19:45–20:15.

- **The bands** (`settings.lighting`): day from 07:00, dusk from 17:00, night from 20:00, dusk again (dawn) from 05:00, easing from one to the next over 30 minutes centred on each change (`lightLevel`: 0 day, 1 dusk, 2 night).
- **The tint** (Tom's pick C, pools of light, at **50%**, covering everything): the picture multiplied by a tint round the ground (Broadcast's stands and the dark round the ground, fading in with the stands as the layout slides) and on the ground (its turf below the boards; in Full the turf down to the ticker), each eased toward none by `strength`. Full's top strip (the ad still) is never tinted.
- **The pools of light**: each floodlight's light falling on the ground a share of the way toward the centre spot, kept to the ground.
- **The floodlights**: in Broadcast, one in each corner of the bowl on a concrete mount (Tom: something built round them, the seats under it cleared; `stands.js` `drawFloodlightMounts`), there all day, the lamps off by day and lit with a glow from dusk. Not in Full (no stands).
- **The replay inset** takes the ground's tint and an even share of the pools (`lightPitch`).
- **Never lit:** the studio, the scene, the desk, the interview, the ads, the cards, the HTML overlays (the scorebug, captions, panes).
- **Code:** `src/channel/lighting.js` (`lightAt` pure; `drawLight` after the stands and before the scene in `main.js`; the zone's offset looked up once a minute). `lighting.on: false` leaves every picture as it was. `?at=` on the dev server shows any hour.
- **Tests:** `tests/lighting.test.js` (the bands, the ease, the strength, the clock's offset).

## 32. [A new non-match segment] AROUND THE GROUNDS (Tom's note 14, 2026-10-19)

Picked on `groundstour.html` (three rounds of mock-ups): a tour of the league's eight grounds with no match on, each with something going on there. No voices. Presentation only.

- **Where** (`programme.js` `maybeTour`): once a gap, after the first re-air and its studio block (or before the highlights when no re-air fits), only if the whole tour and a block after it end before the run-in; on the night after the final, before its filler; never between the second semifinal and the final (note 16).
- **It takes a re-air's place** (Tom, 2026-10-19: re-airs are the least interesting thing on air): in a gap with room for two re-airs or more, one fewer airs, which one turning from gap to gap so every match keeps its share (two re-airs at least, three for most, over season 1). On a regular-season day (`npm run rundown -- --season 2 --day 3 --seed flareball`): re-airs 13h38m → 10h12m (37 → 28), highlights 1h13m → 2h57m, studio cards 2h48m → 3h57m, the tour 48 minutes; a match on screen 71.2% → 56.9%, play with highlights 76.3% → 69.3%. More new segments after the tour are to fill the space (Tom).
- **The order** (`groundsTour.js` `tourVisits`): every ground once, seeded by the gap, ending at the next match's (its home team's), `groundsTour.visitSeconds` (40) at each, about 5½ minutes in all.
- **The scenes** (`groundScenes.js`), dealt like the studio cards so none repeats in a tour: SHOOTING (the keeper facing a queue of shooters and playing each ball back out, pairs passing, one on a lap who waits for a free moment, the coach by the cones), THE KEEPERS' DRILLS, A CHAT (two talking, the ball kept up with kicks), DRILLS round the cones on the manager's whistle, CARDIO in training wear; and for the groundstaff MOWING (with the sprinklers; down one band and up the next, back and forth across the pitch, driving across to each new band, 10 s a band, Tom), THE LINES and THE EMBLEM (each one unhurried pass over the ground's 40 s, done by nine tenths of it, Tom: not over and over), THE NETS, THE DUGOUTS. Round 4 (Tom, 2026-10-19: a night tour of only five repeated): THE MANAGER alone by the tunnel with a clipboard, THE NAME on the turf repainted in one pass (faded ahead of the painter), LITTER (two crew by the tunnel with a bin and bags, more in the stands in Broadcast), A LONE LAP in training wear, THE NIGHT WATCH (a guard with a torch walking very slowly along the touchline, stopping at the end of his walk and turning back, nights only). At night (the light past `nightLevel`) and on the night after the final the ten fit for it, so a tour never repeats; by day all but the night watch. (THE GOALMOUTH re-turfed is on the mock-up page, left out: unclear what it shows, Tom.)
- **On screen** (`main.js`): the ground's own marks and its colours at both ends, the dugouts empty, the gate open (Tom), a few early fans for training (`groundsTour.fans`), lit by the time of day (§31). The caption plate (Tom's pick A): the ground's name and whose home it is ("HIGHMOOR FIELD · Home of the Skarren Rime"; Tom: no words for what's going on). The scorebug counts down to the next match.
- **The wipe** (Tom's pick C): each ground after the first comes in behind a band in its club's colours (`wipeWidth` of the picture, over `wipeMs`), the old ground's last moment kept ahead of the band. Into the tour and out of it, the glass sweep as for any match picture.
- **SCHEDULE**: one line, AROUND THE GROUNDS (`guide.js`, entry kind `grounds`).
- **Sound** (task 3, 2026-10-19; Tom judged the new ones on `sounds.html`): a `swish` as each ground after the first wipes in; for training a thin murmur (the few early fans) and the scene's own, timed from the beats that draw it (`groundScenes.js` `sceneCues`): each shot, pass, keeper's gather and roll-out, the touches kept up in the chat, the manager's and coach's short whistles. For the groundstaff, four new effects (`musicRecipes.js`, recorded in `assets/sounds/effect/`): the **mower** (every 2.6 s, takes running together; at 0.4, Tom: quieter), an impact **sprinkler** ticking without a break (14 ticks 0.13 s apart a take, played back to back, Tom; the second sprinkler half a tick behind), a **hammer** on the nets (three unhurried knocks, Tom), a **broom** in the dugouts (at 0.2, Tom: quieter). The lines' painters work quietly. At night (Tom) **crickets** under all but the mower, the sprinklers and the broom: one low chirp five times a second apart, about every 13 s (three times in a ground's 40 s). Round 4's own (Tom): LITTER the bin's lid as each bag goes in (the ads' clunk), A LONE LAP footsteps now and then (the ads' steps), THE MANAGER a page turned now and then (`page`, new), THE NIGHT WATCH a walkie-talkie crackle (`radio`, new) as he stops at the end of his walk, before turning back.
- **The grounds' names elsewhere** (task 4, Tom's picks; `groundsTour.js` `groundName`, from the data, never the league): the pre-match card ("TUE 14:03 · at the Rookery, Wrenhollow"; in the playoffs the better seed's), the walk-out's first caption ("WELCOME TO THE ROOKERY · Wrenhollow", the first half of the walk-on, then THE TEAMS WALK OUT), the team card under its name ("The Rookery, Wrenhollow · The Gullcoast"), the Rulebook's LEAGUE tab (each club at its ground, the `{grounds}` fact). Not the ticker, SCHEDULE or the turf (Tom).
- **Tests:** `tests/groundsTour.test.js` (the order, the deal, the caption, one tour a gap, none before the final, the groundstaff after it, SCHEDULE, the names elsewhere); `tests/entries.test.js`: every script the published pages load parses (a second `groundOf` in main.js once stopped the channel at TUNING IN while every unit test passed).

## 33. [A new non-match segment] FROM THE VAULT (Tom's note 14, 2026-10-19)

Picked on `vault.html` (Tom's picks from round 1): a short package on one big match from the past. No voices. Presentation only.

- **The story** (`vault.js` `vaultStory`): for now last season's final (Tom's placeholder for the first seasons), from `vault.fromSeason` (2). Its headline says why it's in the vault, never the result ("THE LEAGUE'S FIRST FINAL", "THE SEASON 4 FINAL"). Other stories (a record broken, a milestone, a hat-trick, the biggest win) will each need a build of their own with words and data that suit them (Tom), and their matches' events kept beyond last season.
- **When** (`programme.js` `maybeVault`): about each of `vault.at` (10:00 and 19:00, Tom). A time from the last match's run-in to this one's belongs to this gap; it airs at the first break from it, or just before a re-air or package that would carry past it, if the package and a block fit before the run-in. Never between the second semifinal and the final (note 16), nor on the night after the final. On the dev league's Tuesday of season 2: 09:50 and 18:58, 5:40 a day in all.
- **The running order:** the opening card (`openSeconds`, 20): the amber bar headed FROM THE VAULT, the headline in gold, the two crests with "v" (never the score), "SEASON 1 · SUN 20:00 · THE CRUCIBLE, BRASSGATE". Then the match's reel (as its highlights package, every goal first, up to `highlights.maxClips`), lit as when it was played, the scorebug saying FROM THE VAULT in the replays' blue, STATS saying PLAYED SUN 20:00. Then SINCE THEN (`sinceSeconds`, 20): each finalist then (CHAMPIONS, RUNNERS-UP) and now (this season's place and points, or NOT PLAYED YET). Then a studio block. WHY IT MATTERS was dropped (the headline says it, Tom).
- **SCHEDULE:** one line, FROM THE VAULT, its time in the replays' blue like a re-air's (`guide.js` entry kind `vault`).
- **Tests:** `tests/vault.test.js` (the story, its cards with no score, about each time a day in its order, none in season 1, SCHEDULE).

## 34. [A new non-match segment] OFF THE BALL, the podcast (Tom, 2026-10-20)

The whole story (the ideation, Tom's picks, the lines, the voices) is in `docs/podcast.md`; the lines in `data/podcast.json`, validated in `src/config.js`; the timings in `settings.podcast`.

- **An episode** (`podcastTalk.js`, pure): made each regular-season day at `settings.podcast.at` (18:00) from what has aired by then, SEASON AHEAD on day 1, SEASON REVIEW at the final's whistle; the opening, a hook (the theme's, each line only when true), the theme's next discussion (a seeded schedule: each theme once in every run of eight episodes, never twice running), PICKS, the close. Seeded from the channel's public look seed (`live.looks`), so a day built from the seed or from the keys says the same. Each line plays its own clip, `assets/voices/podcast/<tamsin|samir>/…` (`podcastClipKey`), and lasts as long as it (`podcastTiming`).
- **The longer episode** (Tom, 2026-10-20: a major event): three talks (`parts`) around the day's goals (a reel, `settings.podcast.reelClips`) and an ad break, three discussions (`settings.podcast.discussions`); about 8½ minutes; docs/podcast.md has the running order.
- **On air** (`programme.js`): its pieces (talks, the reel, the ads, each marked with `podcast`) (`topic: 'podcast'`, its `podcast` naming the episode, `repeat` when it airs again) at the first break from its time, never before, a re-air giving way when it no longer fits, then a studio block; again overnight from `repeatAt` (03:00) the next day; never in PLAYOFF PREVIEW's or FINALS PREVIEW's gap, so the last daily episode airs once. SEASON REVIEW opens the night after the final and airs again before its repeats, the filler leaving room.
- **SCHEDULE:** one gold line, OFF THE BALL, REP when it airs again; `npm run rundown` lists it (`off the ball` in the day's totals).
- **On screen: the booth** (`podcastBooth.js`, step 4c): set B4 in the scene's place, the window over the ground of the day lit by the time of day; during a topic the side panes frost and the middle pane is the screen (THE TABLE's table, otherwise a card from the line's show; OFF THE BALL's own lines clear it); the day's goals play on it following the ball (the reel is the scene's picture, no stings or captions), the pair reacting to each goal in turn (`reelReactions`); the lower third says who's talking, the bubbles sit outward of the pair. The interjections and the sound are step 4d.
